“That man?” interrogated Christopher with uplifted brows.

===Chinese===
他们在金鱼旅店找到了米尔沃德先生。见到二人，他似觉如释重负。罗克斯海斯邀他共进晚餐，克里斯托弗则出门接他哥哥去了。

他和罗德里克在离王宫不远的一家小客栈用了饭。兄弟俩两年未见，彼此都有满腹的话要说。直到饭毕，罗德里克才提起罗克斯海斯，一开口便直奔要害。

“克里斯托弗，你怎么会去给那个人当差？”

“那个人？”克里斯托弗挑起眉毛反问。

---

===Original===
“Lord Roxhythe.”

“He was in need of a secretary; I, of work.”

“There are a score of better men in need of secretaries!”

“Indeed?”

===Chinese===
“罗克斯海斯勋爵。”

“他正缺一位秘书；我呢，正缺一份差事。”

“比他体面、用得着秘书的主顾，少说也有二十个！”

“哦？”

---

===Original===
“Don’t speak like that, child! You should have consulted me. I might have known you would act foolishly when my father died.”

“I am perfectly well able to care for myself! And I resent—your tone!”

Roderick ignored this.

“Were my father alive he would be more than displeased to see you in such company.”

===Chinese===
“别这么说话，孩子！你本该先同我商量。我早该料到，父亲一去世，你就会做出蠢事来。”

“我完全照管得了自己！而且我很反感——你这种口气！”

罗德里克只当没听见。

“父亲若还在世，见你混在这号人里头，决不止是不悦。”

---

===Original===
“Roderick, what do you mean? What have you against my lord?”

“What every sane man has against him. He is a libertine—a rakehelly fellow, with no morals, and less honour.”

“How dare you say that? He is no more rakehell than the others at Whitehall! And as to honour!—You speak of what you do not know!”

“Do I so? Even an what you say is true, which it is not—that he is no more rakehell than the rest of that licentious circle, it is no excuse for entering his service. I would not have my brother in the company of one of them.”

===Chinese===
“罗德里克，你什么意思？你对爵爷有什么不满？”

“凡是头脑清醒的人对他都有的那份不满。他是个放荡子——一个荒唐透顶的浪荡儿，没有半点德行，荣誉更是少得可怜。”

“你怎么敢这么说？同白厅里那班人比，他半点不见得更荒唐！至于荣誉！——你说的尽是你不知道的事！”

“是么？就算你说的当真——其实并不——就算他同那帮荒淫圈子里其余的人相比不见得更荒唐，那也构不成你去给他当差的理由。我决不容我弟弟同他们中间的任何一个为伍。”

---

===Original===
Christopher essayed a sneer.

“Why, are you turned Puritan?”

“I am no more Puritan than ever I was, as you very well know. Had I been in England a month ago I would have prevented you taking this disastrous step.”

“And I tell you that you would not! My Lord Roxhythe is a very honourable, brave gentleman, and I am proud to be in his service!”

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗试着摆出一副讥诮。

“怎么，你改当清教徒了？”

“我同从前一样，半点不是清教徒，这你心里再明白不过。一个月前我若在英格兰，断不会由你走出这步祸害棋。”

“我告诉你，你拦不住！罗克斯海斯勋爵是位极重荣誉、极有胆魄的绅士，我为他当差，与有荣焉！”

---

===Original===
“A patriotic gentleman also, I suppose?”

“Yes!”

“You are infatuated.”

“Then so are you! What induced you to enter the Prince of Orange his service? I would not work for a foreigner!”

===Chinese===
“想必还是位爱国的绅士？”

“不错！”

“你这是鬼迷了心窍。”

“那你也是！你又怎么会去给奥兰治亲王当差？我可不给外国人做活！”

---

===Original===
Roderick gripped his wrist, shaking him.

“You young fool, be silent!”

“Why?” Christopher stared.

“Do you think no Dutchman understands English that you shout what might be mine undoing in an inn parlour?”

===Chinese===
罗德里克攥住他的手腕，摇撼着他。

“你这小傻瓜，住口！”

“为什么？”克里斯托弗瞪着眼。

“你当荷兰人里没有一个听得懂英语么，竟在客栈的堂屋里嚷嚷那些足以叫我身败名裂的话？”

---

===Original===
“Your pardon!” … Christopher rolled the words out caressingly. “I had forgot you played a double part. Odds-life, Roderick! I would not serve two men as you do. To my mind it is no gentlemanly thing to do.”

Roderick coloured angrily.

“You do not understand. I serve the Prince and no other!”

“Who is imprudent now?” jeered Christopher. “I thought you served De Witt once? Your devotion to the Orange smacks somewhat of double-dealing.”

===Chinese===
“失敬失敬！”……克里斯托弗慢条斯理地把这几个字把玩着吐出来。“我倒忘了，你原是玩两面手段的。上帝的生命，罗德里克！像你这样一仆二主，我可不干。照我看，这可不是绅士的行径。”

罗德里克气得涨红了脸。

“你不懂。我只效忠亲王，绝无二主！”

“如今是谁口没遮拦了？”克里斯托弗讥诮道。“我记得你从前不是伺候过德·维特么？你这对奥兰治的忠心，多少有点两面三刀的味道。”

---

===Original===
“I am not going to quarrel with you, Christopher.”

“Then do not seek to malign my master to me! I know him as you do not, and I tell you he is the soul of honour!”

“You fool,” said Dart quietly. “Do you believe that? I warn you that one day you will be disillusioned. Roxhythe works for himself alone. He would dupe you did the need arise, or crush you beneath his heel. You think him a man of scruples, but I tell you—and I know—that he is without heart and without honour. Chris, you are very young, be advised by me and quit his service. He only wants you for a tool.”

===Chinese===
“我不打算同你吵，克里斯托弗。”

“那就别想在我面前毁谤我家主人！我了解他，你不了解，我告诉你，他是荣誉的化身！”

“你这傻瓜，”达特不动声色地说。“你信这个？我警告你，总有一天你会大梦初醒。罗克斯海斯只为他自己打算。一旦需要，他会把你当傻子耍，或者一脚把你踩在脚下。你当他是个有顾忌的人，可我告诉你——我知道底细——他既无心肝，也无荣誉。克里斯，你还年轻，听我一句劝，离开他吧。他要你，无非当件使唤的工具。”

---

===Original===
“’Tis you who are the fool! My Lord wants me for a secretary! There is no question of duping, or tools.”

“Do you deny then that you are with him solely for the purpose of bringing King Charles his message to the Prince?”

Christopher was silent.

“Roxhythe hath it in mind to use you in his machinations for his master. And if you are overnice in your scruples, he’ll trick you. Be warned, Chris, I implore you!”

===Chinese===
“傻瓜的是你！爵爷要的是一名秘书！什么诓骗、什么工具，压根没有的事。”

“那么你否认么——你跟着他，唯一的目的就是把查理国王的口信带给亲王？”

克里斯托弗沉默了。

“罗克斯海斯心里盘算的，是要在他替他家主上搞的种种诡谋里使唤你。你若顾忌太多、碍手碍脚，他就会设局诓你。谨防着些吧，克里斯，我求你！”

---

===Original===
“You are undoubtedly mad,” said Christopher with conviction. “If I were required to work for His Majesty there would be no need of trickery. I would die for His Majesty and the Country.”

“You cannot die twice!” snapped Roderick.

“The King and his Country are one, as you should know.”

“Are they?” said Roderick heavily. “Not your King and his Country.”

“You are disloyal! My King? He is also yours!”

“Alas, yes!”

===Chinese===
“你准是疯了，”克里斯托弗言之凿凿。“就算真要我替陛下当差，也用不着耍什么诡计。为陛下、为国家，我甘愿赴死。”

“你可死不了两回！”罗德里克抢白道。

“国王与国家本为一体，这你该懂得。”

“是么？”罗德里克沉痛地说。“你的国王和他的国家，可不是。”

“你不忠！我的国王？他也是你的国王！”

“唉，是啊！”

---

===Original===
Christopher betrayed anxiety.

“Roderick, consorting with these stiff-necked Dutchmen has affected your brain. I do not pretend to understand this strange talk of yours. You had best quit Holland and come home!”

“My dear brother, living as I do, I have had opportunities of studying politics, and of viewing politicians and Princes that you have not. I have seen the intrigues within intrigues that are always afoot—the treachery, the lying! More I cannot say, but rest assured that I speak the truth. I have seen what manner of men live in England and in France, and I know that amongst them all there is not one who is honest. There is only one man to be trusted. Him, I serve.”

“You have become bigoted, Dick, and hard. What you say is utterly false. Is it possible that you think your master the one honest man? Why, even I, whom you so freely call fool, am not so mad!”

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗露出焦灼之色。

“罗德里克，同这些犟脖子的荷兰人厮混，把你的脑子都搅坏了。你这套怪谈，我也不装作听得懂。你顶好是离开荷兰，回家去！”

“我亲爱的弟弟，凭我如今过的这种日子，我有机会钻研政事、阅历政客与亲王，这是你没有的。我看透了那永在暗中滋生的、阴谋里头套阴谋——那背叛，那谎骗！再多我不能说了，但你只管放心，我说的是实话。英格兰和法兰西住的是何等样人物，我见识过；我知道，他们当中找不出一个诚实之人。天下只有一个男人值得信赖。我侍奉的，正是他。”

“你变得偏执了，迪克（Dick），也变得冷酷了。你说的话全然是歪理。难道你当真把你的主子看作那唯一的诚实之人？哎呀，连我——被你张口傻瓜闭口傻瓜地叫着的这位——也还没有疯到那个地步！”

---

===Original===
Roderick sighed.

“I see you will go your own wilful way, Chris. You have fallen a victim to Roxhythe’s notorious charm, and I suppose you will follow him headlong to destruction.”

Christopher leaned his head in his hands and gave way to helpless laughter. When he had recovered, Roderick started to talk on some other topic. They spoke no more of Roxhythe that evening.

===Chinese===
罗德里克叹了口气。

“我看你是一意孤行到底了，克里斯。你已做了罗克斯海斯那出了名的魅力的俘虏，我猜你会跟着他一头栽向毁灭。”

克里斯托弗双手抱头，止不住哈哈大笑。待他笑够了，罗德里克才转了话头，谈起别的事。那晚他们再没提罗克斯海斯。

---

===Original===
My lord was in his dressing-gown when Christopher came back to the inn. He gave his secretary one shrewd, calculating glance.

“You look heated, Chris. You have been quarrelling with your brother.”

“Nearly,” said Christopher. “I fear for his sanity. He speaks so wildly, and so foolishly.”

“In fact he disapproves of my Lord Roxhythe most sincerely,” nodded his lordship. “Does he seek to remove you from my evil influence?”

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗回到旅店时，爵爷已换上了晨衣。他朝自己的秘书锐利而掂量地瞥了一眼。

“你一脸火气，克里斯。你同你哥哥吵过了。”

“差一点，”克里斯托弗说。“我怕他神智不大正常了。他说起话来那么狂乱，那么愚蠢。”

“合着他对罗克斯海斯勋爵是诚心诚意地看不入眼喽，”爵爷颔首。“他可是想把你从我这份坏影响底下解救出去？”

---

===Original===
“Oh, yes, sir! I think he seeks to put me in a glass case, for he avows that there is but one honest man alive today, and he will not have me serve under any other!”

“Lud! Does he expect you to enroll yourself under the Prince’s banner? Don’t do it, Chris! ’Tis a gloomy youth.”

“Not I, sir!” said Christopher flippantly. “I had sooner tread the path to destruction in your company.”

“So ho!” said Roxhythe to his reflection in the mirror. He was seated before his dressing-table. “Is that the way the wind blows? Be warned, Chris! I am an unscrupulous fellow.”

===Chinese===
“哎，正是，先生！我看他恨不得把我供进玻璃匣子里去，因为他一口咬定，当世只有一个诚实之人，除他之外，他不许我在任何人手下当差！”

“老天！他要你投到亲王的旗下去不成？千万别去，克里斯！那可是个阴沉沉的少年。”

“我才不去呢，先生！”克里斯托弗油嘴滑舌地说。“要走那条毁灭之路，我宁可由您作伴。”

“嚯嗬！”罗克斯海斯对着镜中的影子说。他正坐在梳妆台前。“原来风是这么个刮法？留神吧，克里斯！我可是个寡廉鲜耻的家伙。”

---

===Original===
“I know,” said Christopher, smiling. “He told me so.”

“Well, it’s true enough.”

“Is it, sir?” Christopher’s voice vibrated with indignation. “Do you seek to warn me ’gainst yourself?”

“It would appear so,” said Roxhythe.

===Chinese===
“我知道，”克里斯托弗微笑着说。“他对我就是这么讲的。”

“唔，倒也八九不离十。”

“是么，先生？”克里斯托弗的声音气得发颤。“您这是要教我自己提防您自己？”

“看来如此，”罗克斯海斯说。

---

===Original===
Three days later my lord entered their private parlour, and sat down at the table. He dipped a quill in the ink, and drew a sheet of parchment towards him.

“Milward sticks like a leech. I feel it behooves me to write to my little master.” He bent over the parchment.

Christopher assented vaguely.

The quill scratched tranquilly along. Presently Roxhythe sat back, and dusted the sheet. He folded, addressed, and sealed it.

===Chinese===
三天后，爵爷走进两人的私厅，在桌边落座。他将鹅毛笔蘸了墨，把一页羊皮纸拖到面前。

“米尔沃德叮着咱们，跟条水蛭似的。我觉得有义务给我那位小主上修书一封了。”他俯首伏在羊皮纸上。

克里斯托弗含混地应了一声。

鹅毛笔安然地沙沙游走。不多时，罗克斯海斯向后一靠，掸了掸纸面，折好信，题上名址，封缄停当。

---

===Original===
“That will go by special courier. It should interest Mynheer Spy.”

“If it goes by courier, how should Milward see it,” asked Christopher.

“There are ways,” placidly replied his lordship.

“But he would scarce dare to steal a letter from you to His Majesty, sir!”

===Chinese===
“这个要走专差。想必密探先生会感兴趣的。”

“既然走专差，米尔沃德又怎么看得见呢？”克里斯托弗问。

“办法总是有的，”爵爷安之若素地答。

“可他总不见得敢偷您呈给陛下的信吧，先生！”

---

===Original===
“No,” agreed Roxhythe. “He would very easily dare to borrow it for some few minutes, however.”

“To read the contents? He hopes to learn the result of your mission—or—or whether you have been on a mission at all—so that Louis might make an outcry?”

“Something like that, no doubt.”

“I see,” said Christopher profoundly. “Yet how dare he break the seal?”

===Chinese===
“是不敢，”罗克斯海斯表示赞同。“不过，把它‘借’去几分钟，他倒是很容易就有这份胆子的。”

“好读里面的内容？他想探明您那趟差事的结果——或者——或者您究竟有没有出差事——好叫路易好生嚷嚷一番？”

“多半正是此类勾当。”

“明白了，”克里斯托弗意味深长地说。“可他怎么敢破火漆印呢？”

---

===Original===
“You do not realize that I have been so considerate as to place the seal on the wafer,” said Roxhythe, pained. “He will not break it.”

“Were you born in intrigue, sir?” asked Christopher.

“I believe I must have been,” said his lordship.

===Chinese===
“你没有意识到，我是何等体贴，把火漆印盖在了封缄薄饼的上头，”罗克斯海斯一脸痛心。“他破的不会是它。”

“您莫不是生在阴谋里的吧，先生？”克里斯托弗问。

“想必是的，”爵爷说。

---

===Original===
Thus it came to pass that when the not incorruptible courier slept off his doctored potations at Delft, his late host, accredited agent to his Most Christian Majesty, removed a certain document from his wallet, and in the deserted coffee-room, carefully slit open the wafer that sealed it. He was well versed in ciphers but he found a cipher unnecessary. The letter was short, and was written in English.

===Chinese===
于是便有了这样一幕：那位未必廉洁的信使正在代尔夫特酣睡，以消解他腹中那份被动过手脚的酒力；他方才的东道主人——最虔诚的国王陛下的正式密使——从他的行囊里抽出某份文书，在空无一人的咖啡室里，小心翼翼地挑开了封缄的薄饼。密码之学他本烂熟于胸，此番却发觉密码全然多余。信很短，以英文写成。

---

===Original===
> “**Yr. Majesty**—Has Yr. Majesty punished His Servant enough, or must Roxhythe Remain a Wanderer? If he might Crave Yr. Pardon againe, he Does so, Still more Humbly and Contritely than Before. Yet more earnestly Does he Implore Yr. Majesty to Allow him to Return, when he will Endeavoure To Showe Yr. Majesty How Great is his Remorse for that Unpardonable Offence which he Committed.
> “He is Yr. Majesty’s most Devoted, Humble Servant, **Roxhythe**.”

===Chinese===
> “**陛下**——陛下对臣仆的惩戒可已足够，抑或仍须教罗克斯海斯漂泊在外？臣仆若可再度恳乞陛下垂怜宽宥，臣仆谨再拜恳乞，较之先前愈加谦卑，愈加痛悔。臣仆更切切吁请陛下俯允臣仆归返；届时臣仆必当竭诚上达，使陛下知悉：彼次臣仆所犯那桩不可饶恕的罪过，臣仆如今悔恨何其深重。
> “臣仆谨为陛下最忠顺、最谦卑的仆人，**罗克斯海斯**。”

---

===Original===
His Most Christian Majesty’s accredited agent was annoyed and perplexed. Deftly he re-stuck the wafer, and restored the packet to the courier’s wallet. Then he rode back to the Hague.

The amiable Mr. Milward when apprised of the contents of the letter wrinkled his brow uncertainly.

“It seems we are come on a fruitless errand, Dupont. Roxhythe has not the wit to write such a plausible blind. King Charles is not a fool, and only a fool sends a fool to work his intrigues.”

“You are assured that milor’ is a fool, then?”

“He is a brainless court-darling. Yet. … It was strange that he should fall into such sudden disgrace. I had thought him too perfect a courtier to offend as he did. I confess I am at a loss. He has not had word with the Prince, nor any of his servants, unless it be Mr. Dart who is De Witt’s man. I have dogged his steps, and he suspects naught!” He laughed contemptuously. “I believe we are on a fool’s errand!”

===Chinese===
最虔诚的国王陛下这位正式密使，又是气恼，又是困惑。他利利落落把薄饼重新粘好，将信件放回信使的行囊，随后骑马回海牙去了。

和蔼可亲的米尔沃德先生得知信的内容之后，狐疑不定地皱起了眉头。

“看来咱们此行是白忙一场了，杜邦（Dupont）。罗克斯海斯没有那份才智，写不出这等像样的幌子。查理国王不是傻子，而只有傻子，才会派个傻子去替自己施展阴谋。”

“那么，您断定米洛是个傻子了？”

“他是个没脑子的宫廷宠儿。可是。……他败落得这般突然，委实蹊跷。我原当他是位太完美的廷臣，断不至于像那回那样开罪圣上。我承认自己摸不着头脑。他没同亲王通过话，也没同亲王手下的任何仆从打照面——除非算上达特先生，而那是德·维特的人。我一路钉他的梢，他浑然不觉！”他轻蔑地嗤笑一声。“我看咱们这是在办一桩傻子的差事！”

---

===Original===
“Maybe, m’sieu’. But I do not think that milor’ is quite the brainless nincompoop he pretends to be. I would I had been at Rotterdam in place of Grant. I am uneasy.”

“He is either a fool or a marvellously astute man. In any case, what more can be done?”

“Naught,” said Dupont sadly. “But I mistrust him.”

===Chinese===
“也许吧，先生。可我并不觉得米洛当真像他装出来的那样，是个没脑子的糊涂虫。但愿当时在鹿特丹的是我，换下了格兰特（Grant）就好了。我心里总不踏实。”

“他不是傻子，就是个精明绝伦的人物。无论怎样，咱们还能做什么呢？”

“无可再做了，”杜邦怏怏道。“可我信不过他。”

---

===Original===
In due time came a letter for Roxhythe. It was brought to him as he sat at dinner with Christopher and Mr. Milward.

“From my master,” he said. “You will excuse me?” He tore open the seals and read. Then he gave a relieved laugh, and laid the sheet down in such a way that Mr. Milward might easily read what was written there.

“His Majesty is pleased to forgive me! So it’s boot and saddle for us, Chris, as soon as may be.”

===Chinese===
到了日子，一封给罗克斯海斯的信送到了。他正同克里斯托弗和米尔沃德先生同桌用饭，信便呈到了他面前。

“主上寄来的，”他说。“诸位恕罪？”他撕开封印读罢，随即发出一声如释重负的笑，把信纸摆在一个米尔沃德先生对上面字迹一目了然的位置。

“陛下开恩，饶恕我了！那就上马备鞍吧，克里斯，愈快愈好。”

---

===Original===
King Charles’ letter ran:

> **My Little Davy**—My Majesty must needs Forgive you, for I cannot Live Without You. I am Surrounded by Dolts and Sycophants; I must have My Roxhythe. Return at once.—**Charles R.**

===Chinese===
查理国王的信是这样写的：

> **我的小达维**——朕这个陛下嘛，是非饶恕你不可的，因为没有你，朕活不下去。朕被一帮蠢才和马屁精团团围困；朕非有我的罗克斯海斯不可。即刻回来。——**查理王**

---

===Original===
Mr. Milward tried to drown his fears.

Before they left the Hague, Roderick had speech with Roxhythe again.

“His Highness desires me to say, sir, that his answer is final.”

“That is his last word?”

===Chinese===
米尔沃德先生竭力把满腹疑惧压下去。

动身离开海牙之前，罗德里克又同罗克斯海斯谈了一次。

“殿下要我转告，先生：他的答复再无更改。”

“这就是他的最后回话？”

---

===Original===
Roderick bowed.

“I am sorry,” said Roxhythe.

The brothers parted on quite cordial terms. Roderick, seeing that it was useless, did not again press Christopher to leave my lord’s service.

Mr. Milward bade the travellers a touching farewell. Roxhythe addressed him mournfully.

===Chinese===
罗德里克躬身。

“我很遗憾，”罗克斯海斯说。

兄弟俩辞别时倒也颇为亲睦。罗德里克见多说无益，便不再催克里斯托弗离开爵爷门下。

米尔沃德先生同两位旅人道别，情辞恳切，煞是动人。罗克斯海斯凄凄然对他讲话。

---

===Original===
“I shall miss your pleasant companionship, Mr. Milward. We have seen much of each other of late.”

Mr. Milward watched the little cavalcade depart. He became aware of Dupont at his elbow.

“He laughs at us,” said the Frenchman gloomily. “He slips through our fingers like water. Me, I have had dealings with him before. I suspected, but evidence? Pouf!”

“Nonsense!” said Milward uneasily. “I have scarce left his side since he has been at the Hague!”

===Chinese===
“我会想念您这份宜人的相伴的，米尔沃德先生。近来咱们可是常常相见呐。”

米尔沃德先生目送那一小队人马远去，忽觉杜邦已立在肘边。

“他在笑话咱们，”那法国人阴郁地说。“他从咱们指缝里溜走，就跟水一样。我嘛，从前同他打过交道。疑心我是起过的，可证据？噗！”

“胡说！”米尔沃德不安地说。“他到海牙以来，我几乎寸步没离开过他身边！”

---

===Original===
“It would not worry him. I tell you, he is a devil. You might be bound to him with chains, and he would give you the slip. He is not a man. He is a devil.” He walked away, depressed.

Four days later, on board ship, Roxhythe broached a subject that had been weighing heavily on Christopher’s mind. He laid his hand on the young man’s shoulder, and spoke with some affection.

“Well, Chris, so the journey is at an end. In a few hours’ time we shall be in London. What then?”

Christopher looked at him.

===Chinese===
“那可烦不着他。我告诉你，他是个魔鬼。你拿铁链子把他拴牢，他也能从你手里溜掉。他不是人。他是个魔鬼。”他悻悻地走开了。

四天之后，在船上，罗克斯海斯提起了一桩久压在克里斯托弗心头的事。他把手搭上年轻人的肩头，语气里带着几分眷爱。

“好了，克里斯，旅程总算到了头。再过几个钟头，咱们就到伦敦了。然后呢？”

克里斯托弗望着他。

---

===Original===
“You will be no longer in need of a secretary, sir?”

“I? Oh, I must have a secretary, of course.”

Christopher became still more crestfallen.

“I—you—that is, you wish me to leave you now, sir?”

===Chinese===
“您不再需要一名秘书了吧，先生？”

“我？哦，秘书嘛，我自然是少不了的。”

克里斯托弗愈发垂头丧气了。

“我——您——就是说，您这是要我这就走么，先生？”

---

===Original===
“No. But I think you would be wise to be counselled by that excellent brother of yours.”

“Oh, sir, never give him a thought!” cried Christopher. “If I might stay with you, I will serve you—unquestioningly.”

“Very well,” said Roxhythe. “You shall stay.”
===Chinese===
“不要。不过我想，你若肯听你那位好哥哥的指点，才是明智之举。”

“哦，先生，别把他放在心上！”克里斯托弗嚷道。“只要您容我留下，我就服侍您——不问情由，毫无二话。”

“很好，”罗克斯海斯说。“你留下吧。”

## IX The King of England / 英格兰之王

===Original===
Gorgeous in a pale-blue velvet suit with crimson facings and ribbons, and much rich lace, the Most Noble the Marquis of Roxhythe entered the doors of Whitehall. He carried his gloves and his cane in one hand and in the other he held his comfit box. The page at the door cast one glance at the handsome face beneath the wide brimmed hat with its drooping feather, and straightened in every line of his body.

Roxhythe’s slumbrous eyes travelled over him.

“His Majesty?” he said.

The page bowed.

“I think His Majesty is in his closet, sir. Shall I send Master Hutchins to announce you?”

“Do,” said Roxhythe.

===Chinese===
最尊贵的罗克斯海斯侯爵翩然踏入白厅大门，一身淡蓝天鹅绒礼服，绯红的镶边与缎带，遍缀华贵的蕾丝。他一手拿着手套和手杖，另一只手执着蜜饯盒。门口的小侍从朝那顶垂羽宽檐帽下一张俊脸溜了一眼，登时浑身上下每一根线条都挺得笔直。

罗克斯海斯睡意惺忪的眸子在他身上逡巡了一遍。

“陛下么？”他说。

小侍从躬身一礼。

“想必陛下正在私室，大人。要不要小的唤哈钦斯先生（Master Hutchins）来替您通禀？”

“去办吧。”罗克斯海斯道。

---

===Original===
An elegant gentleman crossed the hall towards him.

“I protest ’tis a joy to see you again, Roxhythe,” he simpered. “You had a fair crossing?”

“I believe I did,” assented my lord. “How doth her ladyship?”

“Very well, very well,” said the elegant gentleman. “His Majesty is all a-fret to see you. He will be delighted. He had not expected you so soon.”

On his way up the Grand Staircase, Roxhythe met some half a dozen gentlemen, who were all duly enthusiastic over his return. He reflected that his was something of a triumphal entry.

===Chinese===
一位仪态优雅的绅士穿过大厅，向他迎来。

“说句由衷的话，能再见到您真是喜不自胜，罗克斯海斯，”他忸怩作态地陪笑。“渡海一路还顺当么？”

“还算顺当，”爵爷应道。“尊夫人一向安好？”

“好得很，好得很，”那文雅的绅士说。“陛下正眼巴巴地盼着您呢。他一定欢喜极了。他没料到您来得这么快。”

上大楼梯时，罗克斯海斯迎面遇上五六位绅士，个个对他的荣归表示了恰到好处的热忱。他暗忖，自己这一场进宫，倒有几分凯旋的意味。

---

===Original===
As he neared the King’s closet he spoke to the page beside him.

“Is His Majesty disengaged?”

“I think Lord Lauderdale is with him, my lord.”

“In that case you may announce me,” said Roxhythe.

“Yes, my lord. His Majesty gave orders that you were to be taken to him at once.” He knocked on the door of the closet.

===Chinese===
将近国王的私室，他向身边的小侍从问话。

“陛下此刻得空么？”

“想必劳德代尔勋爵（Lord Lauderdale）正在陪驾，大人。”

“既是如此，那就替我通禀吧。”罗克斯海斯道。

“是，大人。陛下有旨，您一到便即刻带见。”他叩响了私室的门。

---

===Original===
Charles’ voice bade him enter. It held a peevish note.

The page opened wide the door.

“The Most Noble the Marquis of Roxhythe!”

Hat in hand Roxhythe walked into the room.

===Chinese===
查理的声音叫他进来，语调里带着几分愠恼。

小侍从把门敞开。

“最尊贵的罗克斯海斯侯爵阁下！”

罗克斯海斯一手持帽，踱进屋去。

---

===Original===
Charles was seated at the table, opposite Lord Lauderdale, but at the favourite’s entrance, he pushed back his chair and rose quickly to his feet.

“David! Already!” He embraced Roxhythe before my lord had time to make his bow. He held him by the shoulders, scanning his face.

“Zoons, Davy! how I have waited for this moment!”

“Not so impatiently as have I, Sir.” Roxhythe smiled back into his eyes.

My Lord Lauderdale rose, a red spot on either cheek.

===Chinese===
查理正坐在桌边，对面是劳德代尔勋爵；宠臣一进门，他便推开椅子，霍地立起身来。

“大卫！这么快！”爵爷还未及行礼，他已一把搂住了罗克斯海斯。他扶定他的双肩，细细端详那张脸。

“上帝的伤痕，达维！这一刻我等得望眼欲穿！”

“陛下可没我等得那么心焦。”罗克斯海斯含笑迎着他的目光。

劳德代尔勋爵站起身来，两颊各泛起一块红斑。

---

===Original===
“I take it Your Majesty has no further use for me,” he grated.

“None whatsoever, my lord,” answered Charles gaily. “Some other time. …”

Lauderdale jerked a bow to him, and another to Roxhythe. He went angrily out of the room.

“A good riddance to his dourness,” said Charles. “Davy, Davy, I have missed you so sorely I swear I’ll never send you from me again!”

===Chinese===
“想来陛下再没有用得着我的地方了，”他磨着牙道。

“半点儿也用不着了，勋爵大人，”查理兴致勃勃地答道。“改日再叙吧……”

劳德代尔朝他草草一躬，又向罗克斯海斯一躬，怒气冲冲出了房门。

“他那一副阴沉脸，走了正好落得清净，”查理说。“达维，达维，我想你想得苦极了，我发誓再不撵你离开我半步！”

---

===Original===
“I am glad you have missed me, Sir,” said Roxhythe. “So you may have some conception of my poor feelings. These weeks have seemed months!”

Charles drew him to a couch.

“I vow you have had the best of it, David. I have been harassed and overrun with petitions, and whatnot, and empty-headed dolts beside. You would not believe the number that have tried to take your place! But there is only one Roxhythe. How have you fared?”

Roxhythe grew solemn.

===Chinese===
“陛下想我，我不胜欣慰，”罗克斯海斯说。“这样您多少能体会我这份可怜的心绪。这几个星期，漫长得恍如数月！”

查理拉他到一张卧榻边坐下。

“我敢说，占便宜的是你，大卫。请愿书呀、诸如此类的琐事呀，把我搅得焦头烂额，旁边还围着一帮空脑壳的蠢材。想顶替你位子的有多少人，你压根儿想不到！可罗克斯海斯只有一个。你这趟差事办得如何？”

罗克斯海斯神色郑重起来。

---

===Original===
“Very badly, Sir.”

“What’s that? You have been in trouble?—danger?”

“Not I, Sir. ’Twas my errand that I spoke of.”

Charles showed surprise.

“Did you fail, David?”

===Chinese===
“糟得很，陛下。”

“什么？你遇上了麻烦？——危险？”

“并非是我，陛下。我说的是我那趟差事。”

查理面露诧异。

“你失手了，大卫？”

---

===Original===
“Ay, Sir. There was no hope for success.”

The King laughed a little ruefully.

“Well, well! And so you failed! Has such a thing ever before come to pass?”

“Seldom,” said his lordship. “But this time I was very grievously beaten.”

The King settled himself against the cushions.

===Chinese===
“正是，陛下。成功本就无望。”

国王苦涩地轻轻一笑。

“哦哦！这么说你失手了！这等事从前可曾有过？”

“鲜少，”爵爷说道。“不过这一回，我败得极惨。”

国王往靠枕上舒舒服服一靠。

---

===Original===
“If ’twere not so damned annoying, ’twould be most diverting,” he said. “Tell me, David.”

Roxhythe proceeded to relate his adventures up to the point of Roderick’s appearance on the scene.

“I would Your Majesty could have seen that man!” he sighed. “He gave himself the airs of a duke, and he paraded his Puritan views for my edification. I do assure you, Sir; that I have never been so set at naught in my life. He spoke of the Prince as though he were Master of the Universe, and his whole manner was as formal as your father his courtiers never were. He came the second day with the news that the Prince would grant me an audience, as though ’twere some giant favour he were bestowing. I went to the Palace at eight in the evening—I should tell you, Sir, that I was requested to be punctual—and taken to Dart’s room. He led me out by another door, along countless gloomy vaults, each darker than the last, until we came to a hall. Then we met one of the Prince’s not so faithful gentlemen and I became a lackey, and was severely reprimanded. We shook off the man, and proceeded to the Prince’s apartments. Outside one of the doors was yet another man. He, though, was one of the Prince’s faithful ones. He sped us on our way. Dart ushered me into the room with all solemnity. Then he took my pistol from me. I suppose he thought I might assassinate His Highness in a fit of depression. At all events he took it, and left me to kick my heels, awaiting the Prince. And all as though I were in very sooth a lackey! I, Roxhythe, the King’s envoy!

“Then came William of Orange …” he paused.

===Chinese===
“若不是气人得紧，倒真个妙趣横生，”他说。“讲来听听，大卫。”

罗克斯海斯于是把他的历险细细道来，一直讲到罗德里克登场方休。

“但愿陛下能亲眼得见那个人！”他喟然一叹。“他摆着一副公爵的架子，还把他的清教高论搬出来好生开导我。我向您担保，陛下，我这辈子从未受过这般轻贱。他谈起亲王来，口气仿佛亲王是普天下的主宰；他通身的做派，拘谨得连您父王的朝臣也从未有过。第二天他来传话，说亲王肯赏我一次接见，那口吻，倒像在施舍天大的恩典。晚上八点钟我到了王府——陛下，我该说明一句，人家是吩咐我务必准时的——随后被领到达特的房里。他领我从另一道门出去，穿过一条又一条阴森的拱廊，一程暗过一程，末了来到一座大厅。半道上撞见一位对亲王不那么忠心的爷们，我便充作了个听差，还结结实实挨了一顿训斥。甩脱了那人，我们才往亲王的寝殿去。一扇门外还守着另一条汉子。这一位，倒是亲王的忠仆，放我们过去了。达特一脸郑重其事，把我让进房去，跟着便收走了我的手枪。我猜他是怕我一时愁闷发作，顺手把殿下给刺杀了。横竖枪是叫他收去了，丢下我干耗着脚跟，恭候亲王。而这一切，就仿佛我千真万确是个听差！我，罗克斯海斯，国王的使臣！

“接着，奥兰治的威廉进来了……”他顿住了。

---

===Original===
Charles, who had been shaken with suppressed laughter, sat up.

“I’ faith, Davy, I’d give much to have been with you! So you were pushed from pillar to post, my poor Marquis? Oh, lud!” Again he shook. “But what of my nephew?”

Roxhythe spoke gravely.

===Chinese===
查理早憋着笑浑身发颤，此刻坐直了身子。

“说真个的，达维，我情愿出大价钱，换自己也在场！这么说，我可怜的侯爵，你是被人推来搡去、不得安生了？哎哟，老天！”他又笑得抖起来。“我那外甥怎么样？”

罗克斯海斯神色肃然。

---

===Original===
“Sir, he is a remarkable youth. In appearance he is slight, with a hook nose, and eyes that see everything at a glance. His manner is cold, brusque, repressed. His personality is overwhelming.”

“So?” said Charles, interested. “As great as mine?”

“In its way, Sir, greater. He has none of Your Majesty’s charm, but he forces himself into one’s memory. He attracts, and he repels. In spite of his youth, and his lack of polish, he holds the stage.”

“Why, Roxhythe, this is marvellous! Proceed!”

“He received me with as much ceremony as I had by that time learnt to expect. He had the air of an Emperor giving audience to one poor subject. He read Ashley’s packet. Then he asked me what Your Majesty hoped to gain by the bargain.”

===Chinese===
“陛下，此子卓异不凡。容貌清瘦，生一个鹰钩鼻，一双眼睛扫上一眼，万事都瞒不过。举止冷淡、生硬、隐忍收敛。气派却咄咄逼人。”

“哦？”查理听得起了兴致。“跟我一样了得？”

“论各自的路数，陛下，他犹有过之。他全无陛下的魅力，却能硬生生楔进人的记忆。他既诱人亲近，又叫人却步。虽则年少，未经琢磨，压得住整座戏台的却是他。”

“啧，罗克斯海斯，这可太叫人称奇了！接着讲！”

“他接见我的礼数，同我那时已经学会预期的分毫不差。那架势，俨然一位皇帝接见卑微的臣子。他读了阿什利的包裹，随后问我，陛下想从这桩交易里捞得什么好处。”

---

===Original===
Charles burst out laughing again.

“That I should have missed all this!”

“I do confess, Sir, I was taken aback. In a very short time he showed me that if he was young in years, he was old in wisdom. He perceived that Ashley was little more than a catspaw; he sneered at the idea of your helping him to his rightful place. In fact, Sir, he desires to know why you have not come to his aid before now.”

“The impudent young cockerel!”

“You would not think so did you but see and hear him, Sir. He asked me what was to be the price of all you offered. I told him—glibly enough, and evasively. He caught me up, and told me all too rightly what I meant. He saw through and through that proposition, Sir, and at last I was frank with him. I explained Your Majesty’s attitude—with reservations. Then the storm burst. Odds body, Sir, but when the Prince loses his temper, one shivers in one’s shoes. He hailed words about me. He cried that Your Majesty was asking him to barter his Country and his honour for his own advancement. He bade me tell you that he was not a Stuart. He said he saw very clearly how you were seeking to trick him into an alliance by which only you would profit. Finally he tore the packet into shreds and bade me tell you that that was his answer. Then he fell to coughing, and I tried to collect my wits. Sir, I argued with that boy until my throat was parched, and always he had a ready answer wherewith to dumbfound me. He gave away naught without meaning to, and I could not gather what were his intentions. But he has evidently received advances from King Louis, and I think he hopes to frighten you by holding that over your head. That he will ally himself with any foreign power to gain the Stadtholdership, I do not think for a moment. He is as honest as the day, and as astute as old Nick himself. He thinks to rise without foreign aid, but he told me he would not seek to overthrow a Government that he fully acknowledged. He is to be feared, Sir.”

===Chinese===
查理又放声大笑。

“这般好戏我竟错过了！”

“我承认，陛下，我着实吃了一惊。不多时他便叫我明白：论年岁他尚幼，论见识他已老成。他看穿了阿什利不过是枚替人火中取栗的猫爪；您要扶他登上他名分所归的位子——这主意他嗤之以鼻。说实在的，陛下，他倒想知道，您为何至今迟迟不肯助他一臂之力。”

“好一只不知天高地厚的小公鸡！”

“陛下若亲见其人、亲闻其声，就不会这么说了。他问我，您许下的这一切，价钱几何。我答了——答得油滑，也答得闪躲。他当即揪住话头，把我的心思一语道破。那套章程，他看得通通透透，陛下，到头来我只好同他交了底。我把陛下的意思剖析给他听——自然有所保留。跟着，暴风雨就来了。上帝的圣体，陛下，这位亲王发起脾气来，真叫人两腿打颤。他劈头盖脸朝我泼来一顿抢白。他嚷道，陛下这是要他拿他的国家、他的名节，去换他一己的腾达。他命我转告您：他不是斯图亚特家的人。他说他看得清清楚楚，您是想设局诓他订一门同盟，好处尽归您一家独占。最后他把包裹撕成碎片，吩咐我告诉您：这便是他的答复。跟着他咳嗽起来，我便趁势收拢神智。陛下，我与那孩子唇枪舌剑，直争到喉干舌燥，他却回回都有现成的答对，堵得我哑口无言。他无心处半句不漏，有意处滴水不进，我到底摸不透他的意向。不过，路易国王显然向他递过殷勤，我想他正打算把这一层攥在手里，吓唬您。至于说他肯为图谋执政之位同任何外国势力结盟——我是片刻也不信的。他诚实得如青天白日，又狡黠得如同魔王老尼克（old Nick）本尊。他一心要不靠外援而崛起，还告诉我，他绝不谋求推翻一个他全然承认的政府。此人可畏，陛下。”

---

===Original===
“My dear David, I must make his further acquaintance. Did he send any more insulting messages to me?”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的大卫，我非得再同他深交不可。他还捎了什么顶撞我的话没有？”

---

===Original===
“A score. He does not trust you or anyone else, Sir, and he told me so in good round terms. He bade me tell you that Nassau does not stoop to intrigue behind the backs of his ministers. I think already he has quite a little Court.”

Charles was deeply interested.

“And you think him one to be reckoned with?”

“More than that, Sir. I think him a great man; one to be propitiated at all costs. I foresee that he will rise suddenly, and at no very distant date.”

“We must invite him to England,” said the King. “I am agog to see him.”

===Chinese===
“一箩筐。他信不过您，也信不过任何旁人，陛下，这话他毫不容情、原原本本甩给了我。他吩咐我转告您：拿骚家的人，不齿于背着大臣们暗中捣鬼。我看他手底下俨然已有了一个小小的朝廷。”

查理听得兴致大发。

“依你看，此子是个不容小觑的人物？”

“何止，陛下。我看他是个伟人；是不惜一切代价也要笼络的人物。我料定他骤然崛起之日，已为期不远。”

“咱们该请他来英格兰，”国王说。“我心痒难耐，急着见他。”

---

===Original===
“I doubt he would not come. He holds England and the English in contempt. Also King Louis. All this he told me. I spent a pleasant evening, Sir.”

Charles rocked with laughter.

“And I thought I could twist him round my finger! Zounds, why was I not there to see?”

“It is no laughing matter, Sir. I am too old to be ordered about by petty princelings and their servants.”

Charles sobered suddenly.

===Chinese===
“只怕他未必肯来。他把英格兰和英格兰人全不放在眼里，路易国王也不例外。这些都是他亲口对我说的。那一晚我过得可真愉快，陛下。”

查理笑得前仰后合。

“我还当能把他玩弄于股掌之上呢！上帝的伤痕，我当时怎么没在场亲眼瞧瞧？”

“这可不是笑话，陛下。我上了年纪，受不了被一群小亲王和他们的仆从呼来喝去。”

查理忽地收起笑容。

---

===Original===
“But, Roxhythe, it is a plaguey nuisance. This means I must turn to France.” He bit his fingernail, frowning. Then he smiled again. “So you came away with a flea in your ear, my poor Roxhythe? God’s Body, how I have ill-used you! But tell me more of William. You say he has personality; he attracts. But does he inspire his followers with confidence?”

“Judging from Dart’s airs, yes, Sir. He is very well served. It seems his servants would undergo any torture ever invented sooner than betray him.”

Charles made a rueful grimace.

“And,” continued Roxhythe, “he says himself that he will not have any man about him whom he could not trust implicitly.”

“If I said that, I had only you left,” remarked Charles.

===Chinese===
“可是，罗克斯海斯，这真是一桩遭瘟的烦人事。这就是说，我只得转向法国了。”他咬着指甲，眉头深锁。随即又展颜一笑。“这么说，我可怜的罗克斯海斯，你是碰了一鼻子灰回来的？上帝的圣体，我把你亏待得好苦！再多讲讲威廉。你说他有气派；他吸引人。可他叫不叫他的追随者觉得可恃？”

“单看达特那副气派，靠得住，陛下。他手下人伺候得极为得力。看样子他的仆从宁可爱尽世间一切酷刑，也不肯背弃他。”

查理做了个苦相。

“而且，”罗克斯海斯接着说，“他自己讲过，身边容不得一个他不能全然信赖的人。”

“这话换作我来说，那我身边可就只剩你一个了，”查理道。

---

===Original===
“Precisely. And he seems to allow no familiarity—no license. He lives in an atmosphere of gloom and depression.” Roxhythe looked round the luxurious room. “Thank God for Whitehall, and mine own Prince!” he said devoutly.

Charles smiled.

“He is more kingly than I am, eh?”

“No,” said Roxhythe instantly. “He is too young to unbend. But in intrigue, Sir, you have met your match in William of Nassau.”

“I must have a care,” laughed the King.

===Chinese===
“正是。而且他似乎容不得半分亲狎——半分放肆。他活在一片阴郁晦暗的空气里。”罗克斯海斯环顾这间奢华的屋子。“感谢上帝，赐下白厅，赐下我自己这位君上！”他一脸虔诚地说。

查理微微一笑。

“他比我更像一位王，嗯？”

“不，”罗克斯海斯应声便答。“他太年轻，还不懂得舒展。可论起阴谋权术，陛下，您已然棋逢对手——对手便是拿骚家的威廉。”

“那我须得留神才是。”国王笑道。

---

===Original===
“Indeed yes, Sir. Remember, the Orange is a man, and one who must not be forgotten. I foresee trouble. Guard against him.”

“I will,” promised the King. “And now, David, we must look to France.”

===Chinese===
“千真万确，陛下。记住，这位奥兰治是个人物，一个断不容忘诸脑后的人物。我预见祸患。防着他。”

“我会的。”国王许诺道。“如今呢，大卫，咱们该指靠法国了。”

## Book
 II The Ways Diverge

## Book II The Ways Diverge / 第二部 分道扬镳

## I January, 1669 / 一六六九年正月

===Original===
Christopher settled down very quickly on his return from Holland and took up his abode at Bevan House, Charing Cross. His duties as secretary were not arduous, and consisted for the most part of attending to the affairs of Roxhythe’s country estate, and answering the many invitations that flocked in.

He was supremely happy. In spite of all Roderick’s gloomy prognostications his love for Roxhythe grew steadily. True, he had to some extent readjusted his ideas. He no longer held my lord up as a model of good behaviour; he knew that Roxhythe was careless, frivolous, sometimes ruthless. A year ago these facts would have been enough to damn my lord in his eyes, but now he flattered himself that he was broader-minded. He no longer condemned the immoral lives that were led by Roxhythe and his associates. Their frivolity and their rakishness were at times to be deplored, but Christopher could not see that they were without honour. Roderick had insinuated that those who frequented Whitehall were entirely lacking in morals. He had said that each one would barter away his honour for position or money. He had even hinted that there were few who would scruple to betray their country.

As far as Christopher could see there was no question of such a contingency. It might be true of some, but of others it was manifestly untrue. With regard to Roxhythe it was ridiculous. He had no interest in politics; he laughed at intrigue. His whole life was spent in waiting on the King, and amusing himself either at Whitehall or at the Louvre. He was above the petty machinations of the day; he belonged to no party; he never schemed for his own ends.

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗自荷兰归来，很快便安顿下来，在查令十字的贝文府住定了。他的秘书差事并不繁重，大半是照管罗克斯海斯乡下田庄的事务，再回复雪片般涌来的请帖。

他快活到了十分。任凭罗德里克把话说得怎样阴郁，他对罗克斯海斯的爱慕却有增无已。诚然，他的想法多少修正过了。他不再把爵爷奉作端方楷模；他知道罗克斯海斯马虎、轻浮，有时且冷酷。一年之前，这些已足以叫爵爷在他眼里一败涂地；如今他却自诩心胸宽大了。他不再非难罗克斯海斯同他那伙人过的荒唐日子。他们的轻佻放浪时有可叹之处，可克里斯托弗瞧不出他们没有荣誉。罗德里克曾含沙射影，说常走动白厅的那班人全然无德。他说他们个个肯拿荣誉去换职位钱财，甚至影影绰绰地暗示，出卖起国家来会良心不安的，倒没有几个。

在克里斯托弗看来，这样的变故全然无从谈起。这话搁在某些人身上或许属实，搁在另一些人身上却分明是冤枉。至于罗克斯海斯，简直可笑。他对政治没有半点兴致；阴谋诡计他一笑置之。他整副生涯都花在伺候国王、在白厅或卢浮宫寻快活上。时下那些蝇营狗苟的小算盘他一概不在眼下；他不入任何一党；他从不为自己营谋。

---

===Original===
Christopher did not pretend to understand him. It almost seemed as though his was a dual personality, yet the second side of him had appeared for so short a space that Christopher half doubted whether he had not been suffering from an illusion.

In Holland Roxhythe had shown himself to be cool-headed, energetic, astute. Above all he had proved an expert plotter. He had dropped much of his lazy cynicism; his languor had mysteriously vanished. But when the intrigue was at an end back had come the old Roxhythe, just as languid, just as indifferent. No word of politics ever passed his lips; no suspicion of plotting was evident.

Christopher was nonplussed. Eventually he came to the conclusion that Roxhythe was not an intriguer from choice. It was only when commanded by His Majesty that he roused himself.

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗不敢自命了解他。这人几乎像是有双重人格，只是那另一面露脸的时辰太短，克里斯托弗连自己也疑心，怕那不过是一场幻梦。

在荷兰，罗克斯海斯显得头脑冷静、精力充沛、精明老辣，而最要紧的，是显出他是一位中用的密谋行家。他那副懒散的玩世气卸下了大半；神秘的倦怠消失得无影无踪。可密谋一收场，旧日的罗克斯海斯便又回来了，一样地恹恹，一样地漠漠。政治二字他绝口不提；密谋的形迹也半点看不出。

克里斯托弗给他弄得没了主张。末了他断定，罗克斯海斯并非生性爱弄权谋；唯有奉了陛下明谕，他才抖擞起来。

---

===Original===
Roderick had inferred that Roxhythe worked not for England but for his own ends. The idea was beneath contempt. Roxhythe had shown clearly that he worked for the King alone. The King, of course, worked for the Country. It was all one.

Christopher understood that Roxhythe did not wish his powers as an intriguer to be known. That was natural. Once discovered, he could not act with the same freedom. At first Christopher had thought his habitual indifference a mask, but as time went on he decided that it was as real as the other half of him. He realized that he could never hope to fathom the depths of my lord’s nature; perhaps he did not wish to try. He was content to love an enigma. He knew that Roxhythe could be astute; he knew that he was mostly obtuse; he could be ruthless, or he could be kindness personified. To Christopher he had been kind. He seemed to take an amused pleasure in fathering him; he introduced him at Jeremy’s, one of the great coffeehouses; he took him to routs and to balls. In all matters of dress and fashion he advised him; his house and servants were at Christopher’s disposal.

It was a curious friendship. On the one side was boundless affection and unlimited confidence; on the other a casual liking and absolute reticence. Partly it was accounted for by the difference in age. Roxhythe was more than twenty years Christopher’s senior and it was not to be expected that he should confide in the younger man to any great extent. But Christopher knew nothing of Roxhythe’s life. The surface was free for inspection. There were countless amours, countless trivialities, but of what lay beneath the boy had been allowed only a glimpse. Never again had he seen it; all that met his eyes was a cynical roué, fascinating and repellent by turns. He loved this roué as he had never loved before. There had been no woman in his short life, there still was not. Roxhythe possessed his whole heart. Whatever Roderick might say to the contrary, Christopher knew that Roxhythe would always possess it. It was to no avail to analyse the why and wherefore of his love; there was no reason for it but that subtle attraction which my lord held for him. He was content to love, secure in the belief that his love would never be betrayed. He wanted nothing in return; he asked no confidences and was not disappointed that he received none. He gave the very best that was in him, happy that this should be so.

===Chinese===
罗德里克曾下断语，说罗克斯海斯做事并非为英格兰，只为私图。这念头卑污得不屑一驳。罗克斯海斯明明表明了他是单为国王一人效力。国王自然是为国家效力。这原是一码事。

克里斯托弗懂得，罗克斯海斯不愿他那手密谋的本领为人所知。这再自然不过：一旦露了底，行事便没有这般伸缩自如。起初克里斯托弗当他惯常的冷漠是张面具，日子一久，他断定这冷漠同他另一半一样是真的。他明白自己永远休想测透爵爷天性的深处；或许他也无意去探。他满足于爱一个哑谜。他知道罗克斯海斯能精明绝伦，也知道他多半木讷迟钝；他可以冷酷无情，也可以是和蔼的化身。待克里斯托弗，他是和蔼的。他仿佛乐呵呵地以父执自居来照拂他；领他出入杰里米咖啡馆（Jeremy’s）——城中顶大的咖啡馆之一；带他去逛夜会、赴舞会。衣饰时样，事事承他指点；他的宅子、他的仆人，克里斯托弗尽可调用。

这是一段奇特的友谊。一边是无限的眷爱、无涯的信任；另一边是不经心的喜欢、十足十的守口如瓶。年岁之差算是一部分缘由。罗克斯海斯比克里斯托弗年长二十开外，本不能指望他向这年轻人倾吐到何等地步。再说，罗克斯海斯的生活，克里斯托弗一无所知。表面尽人可观：数不清的风流账，数不清的琐碎事；至于底下藏着什么，这年轻人只被容许窥过一眼。此后再无缘得见；扑进他眼里的，只是一个玩世的浪荡子，忽而迷人，忽而可憎。他却爱着这浪荡子，爱得前所未有。他短促的一生里不曾有过女人，如今依旧没有。罗克斯海斯占有着他整颗心。任罗德里克怎么说三道四，克里斯托弗知道这颗心永远归罗克斯海斯所有。去剖析这爱的缘由是徒劳的；它没有缘由，唯有爵爷身上那股说不清的吸力。他满足于爱，坚信这份爱决计不会遭辜负。他不求回报；他不索人推心置腹，也毫不失望于所得皆无。他把自己最好的都捧了出来，且为此欣然。

---

===Original===
Before he had entered Roxhythe’s service his life had been singularly devoid of colour. His mother died when he was a child, and his father had had Roderick’s cold nature. They came of old Puritan stock; they were very godly, and also very repressed. Madam Dart, who was not of her husband’s persuasions, had bequeathed her sunny personality to Christopher. She had nothing else to give him. On account of this bequest there had been no understanding between Christopher and his father, and very little between Christopher and his brother. Both Mr. Dart and Roderick felt that he was not akin to them; they feared that he was weak and easily led astray. They deplored his early craving for excitement, and they did their uttermost to quench the craving. Then, shortly after Roderick’s departure for Holland, Mr. Dart died. At first Christopher felt lost and vaguely frightened, but the feeling had faded and given place to a glorious sensation of freedom. Then Roxhythe had blazed into his life, shocking him. Disapproval had, in its turn, given way to love. His mind had broadened; he lost the shreds of intolerance that had been instilled into him. One thing he retained: love for Country counted above all else. His father’s creed as Christopher’s also. Nothing mattered as much as England. Her honour and welfare must stand first.

Roderick had conceived that they did not stand first with either Roxhythe or the King. He was undoubtedly crazed. The King was naturally above reproach. Equally above reproach was Roxhythe. Christopher cast his brother’s warnings to the four winds.

He was interested in politics and listened closely to all the arguments that took place at Jeremy’s. There was usually some conversation concerning Home Affairs and the King’s intentions. It was well known that Charles squandered away more money than he possessed, and men wondered how he would contrive to pay off his load of debts.

===Chinese===
投到罗克斯海斯门下之前，他的生活素来灰败得异乎寻常。母亲死在他孩提时节，父亲则有着罗德里克那份冷性子。这一族出自清教旧世家：极虔敬，也极压抑。达特太太与丈夫信仰不投，把一副晴朗性子留给了克里斯托弗；除此之外，她没有别的可以留给他。为了这份遗产，克里斯托弗同父亲之间从不曾相知，同兄长之间也所剩无几。达特先生和罗德里克都觉得他同他们不是一路人；他们怕他性子软，容易被引入歧途。他自幼渴慕刺激，两人痛心不已，用尽办法想掐灭这点渴慕。后来，罗德里克去荷兰后不久，达特先生故去了。起初克里斯托弗惶惶然若有所失，隐隐生畏；这感觉渐渐消退，换上来一种灿烂的自由之感。再后来，罗克斯海斯烈火一般闯进他的生命，震得他目瞪口呆。不以为然又继而让位于爱。他的心界开阔了；自幼灌输进来的那点褊狭的渣滓也涤荡干净了。唯有一件他存着：对国家的爱高于一切。父亲的信条，也是克里斯托弗的信条。没有什么比英格兰更要紧。她的荣誉与福祉必须摆在最前。

罗德里克却认定，无论在罗克斯海斯还是国王那里，它都不曾摆在最前。这人准是疯癫了。国王自然无可指摘。罗克斯海斯同样无可指摘。兄长的告警，克里斯托弗一股脑儿抛到了九霄云外。

他对政治有心，杰里米咖啡馆里的种种争论，他都凝神听过。那边的话题多半围着国内政务和国王的意图打转。人人都知道查理花的比他有的还多，也都在揣度他打算怎样清偿那座债山。

---

===Original===
Foreign Affairs were also discussed, especially the menace of France to Holland. Everyone knew that Louis would never rest until he had annexed those Provinces which he claimed, and everyone hoped that England would throw herself seriously into the conflict. Such a contingency would surely turn the scale against Louis. King Louis was universally feared; he was growing too powerful, and too belligerent. It was known, too, that he coveted the throne of Spain for one of his own children. If the ailing young Spanish King died, matters would become serious. On no account must Louis be allowed to seize Spain.

Christopher was deeply interested. He had the hatred of the average Englishman of the time for France. He wished that Roxhythe would discuss these matters with him, but Roxhythe only laughed and protested that such conversation was too deep for him.

At the present moment my lord was in Paris. He had been away a week, and on Christopher’s shoulders had fallen the strenuous task of making his excuses to the various people whose invitations to routs and dinners he had ignored.

===Chinese===
外国的事也有人议论，尤其是法国加于荷兰的威胁。人人都知道，路易不把他声索的那几省并吞到手绝不罢休，也人人都盼着英格兰肯认真投身这场争端。果有那一日，天平断然倒向不利于路易的一边。路易王是众所畏惧的；他权力膨胀得太快，也太好战了。大家还知道，他觊觎西班牙王位，想把它弄给自己一个孩子。那位多病的年轻西班牙国王一旦晏驾，事情就严重了。万万不可容路易染指西班牙。

克里斯托弗听得入神。当时寻常英格兰人都憎恨法国，他亦然。他巴望罗克斯海斯也来同他议议这些事，罗克斯海斯却只哈哈一笑，一口咬定这般深奥的题目他招架不住。

眼下爵爷正在巴黎。他离家已有一个礼拜；那些下帖请他赴夜会、宴席而不得他要领的人家不少，代他一一登门致歉解释这桩苦差，便落在克里斯托弗肩上。

---

===Original===
These visits to France were always unexpected. Suddenly my lord would remark that he could bear London no longer. He would depart with perhaps a day’s warning. More suddenly would he return, with no warning at all. Christopher supposed that he went because of some Frenchwoman. Gossip said so, and he, knowing Roxhythe, believed Gossip.

He was seated in the library one morning, sorting out my lord’s correspondence, when he heard a leisurely footfall without. He lifted his head, listening, for the step was familiar.

The curtains over the doorway parted. Roxhythe came into the room.

===Chinese===
这类法兰西之行向来突如其来。爵爷会蓦地宣称伦敦教他待不下去了，兴许提前一天知会一声便动身；回来得更突然，半点招呼也没有。克里斯托弗揣想他去是为了哪个法国女人。流言蜚语如是说，而他深知罗克斯海斯其人，便信了流言蜚语。

一天早晨，他正坐在藏书室里分拣爵爷的信件，忽听得门外传来一阵从容的脚步声。他抬头凝听，那步点听着耳熟。

门口的帷帘一分，罗克斯海斯走进屋来。

---

===Original===
Accustomed as he was to my lord’s ways, Christopher was still surprised. He sprang up.

“Sir! I had no idea you were in town!”

Roxhythe smiled at him.

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗虽已惯见爵爷的做派，仍旧吃了一惊。他霍地立起。

“先生！我不想您已经回城了！”

罗克斯海斯朝他一笑。

---

===Original===
“Nor was I until an hour ago.” He went to the table and turned over his letters. “You are well, Chris?”

“Very well. And you? You had a pleasant visit?”

“Quite amusing,” nodded Roxhythe. “Need I look at all these?” He flipped a pile of letters with his finger.

===Chinese===
“一个钟头之前我自己也不知道。”他走到桌前，翻了翻信。“你还好么，克里斯？”

“好得很。您呢？此行还尽兴么？”

“相当有趣，”罗克斯海斯点头。“这些我都得看么？”他指头一拨一堆信。

---

===Original===
Christopher glanced through them.

“There are one or two letters from Lady Flora, sir,” he said.

“They will keep. She is becoming wearisome.” He sat down. “Have you any news, Chris?”

Christopher put the letters in the drawer.

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗把它们浏览了一遍。

“有一两封弗洛拉夫人（Lady Flora）的信，先生，”他说。

“搁着罢。她越来越叫人腻烦了。”他坐下来。“有什么新闻么，克里斯？”

克里斯托弗把信放进抽屉。

---

===Original===
“Nothing of great import, sir. Lord Buckhurst requests the pleasure of your company at a supper-party he is giving on Wednesday. I accepted for you. Sir Malcom Digby begs you will honour him on Friday at Shawn House. Mr. Carver gives a dance for Miss Rosiland next month. I have not answered that.”

“Carver? I do not think I have the honour. …”

“You have forgotten, sir. He is the man who gave us shelter the night we rode to Bevan in the storm.”

===Chinese===
“没什么大要紧的，先生。巴克赫斯特勋爵（Lord Buckhurst）恭请您赏光他星期三的夜宴，我已代您应承。马尔科姆·迪格比爵士（Sir Malcom Digby）敬求您星期五光临肖恩府（Shawn House）。卡弗先生（Mr. Carver）下月为罗莎兰德小姐（Miss Rosiland）设一场舞会，这封我还没回。”

“卡弗？我恐怕没有结识的荣幸。……”

“您忘了，先生。就是风雨那夜咱们策马投奔贝文府时，收留咱们的那位。”

---

===Original===
“That wealthy tradesman?” asked his lordship. “What impudence!”

“I am to refuse?”

“Naturally. Stay—this Rosiland—have you seen her?”

“She’s young and shy, sir.”

===Chinese===
“那个发了财的买卖人？”爵爷问。“好大的胆子！”

“回绝么？”

“自然。慢着——这个罗莎兰德——你可见过她？”

“年轻，怕生，先生。”

---

===Original===
“Oh, refuse, refuse!” said Roxhythe impatiently. “Odds-life, what is the world coming to that that upstart should invite me to his house? Naught else?”

“Naught else, sir, unless it be my Lord Arlington’s invitation to supper and cards. I accepted.”

“Well I need not go,” remarked my lord.

“Then I think you will greatly offend Lord Arlington, sir.”

===Chinese===
“哦，回绝，回绝！”罗克斯海斯不耐烦道。“上帝的生命，世道成了什么样，那等暴发户也敢请我登门？再没有别的了？”

“再没有别的了，先生——除了阿灵顿勋爵（Lord Arlington）请赴晚宴打牌的帖子。这个我应了。”

“那个我不去也罢，”爵爷说。

“那恐怕要把阿灵顿勋爵大大开罪了，先生。”

---

===Original===
One haughty eyebrow rose the fraction of an inch.

“Oh? What maggot has Bennett in his head now?”

“He seemed anxious that you should go. And—and he has influence. He was not pleased that you refused his last invitation.”

“Oho! You think I should do well not to offend his lordship?”

===Chinese===
一道倨傲的眉毛扬起了分毫。

“哦？贝内特（Bennett）这会儿脑子里又转着什么怪念头？”

“他千恳万请要您去。况且——况且他是有势力的。上回您回绝他的请帖，他老大不痛快。”

“哦嗬！你是说，那位勋爵大人开罪不得？”

---

===Original===
“Well, sir, he would make a powerful enemy.”

“But not, I think, so powerful an enemy as Roxhythe.” My lord rose and stretched himself. “I suppose I must to Whitehall.” He lounged out.

An hour later, his dress changed, his person powdered and perfumed, he walked into the King’s presence.

Charles was in the midst of his Court, talking to Lady Castlemaine. Way was made for my lord to pass up to his couch. He went forward gracefully, bowing to right and left in answer to the many nods and smiles.

===Chinese===
“是，先生，他做起仇家来可不好惹。”

“只是我想，总还不及罗克斯海斯这么个仇家难缠。”爵爷立起身来，伸了个懒腰。“我想我得上白厅走一遭了。”他懒洋洋地踱了出去。

一个钟头后，他换了衣裳，周身扑粉熏香，走到了国王驾前。

查理正在满朝臣仆之中，同卡斯尔梅恩夫人叙话。众人为爵爷让开道来，容他行到君王的软榻前。他雍容地趋前，左右还礼，应接四下里无数的颔首与微笑。

---

===Original===
“Why, here is our good Roxhythe!” cried her ladyship, welcoming him. “See, Sir!”

“I see a base deserter,” said Charles. He held out his hand. “I believe you love his French Majesty more than me, David.”

Roxhythe bent over it.

“No,” he said, inimitably. “His French Majesty was an interlude, no more.”

===Chinese===
“哟，咱们的好罗克斯海斯来啦！”夫人欢然叫道。“您瞧，陛下！”

“我看见的可是个卑劣的逃兵，”查理说着伸出手来。“我看你爱你那位法国陛下，胜过爱我，大卫。”

罗克斯海斯朝那只手俯下身去。

“不，”他以那副无可摹仿的腔调说。“法国陛下不过一出插曲，仅此而已。”

---

===Original===
Charles joined in the general laugh.

“How doth His Majesty?” he asked.

“Very well,” said Roxhythe. “Very expensively.”

“Surely that’s Roxhythe?” came a voice from behind. “I thought so! Well, my lord? So you’ve returned to us?”

===Chinese===
查理也随着众人笑起来。

“陛下他安好么？”他问。

“好得很，”罗克斯海斯说。“阔绰得很。”

“那不是罗克斯海斯么？”背后有人出声。“我道是谁！怎么，大人？这就回我们这儿来了？”

---

===Original===
Roxhythe bowed to the slim, graceful youth who came up to the group about the King.

“As your Grace sees,” he said.

Monmouth leant on the back of the couch, above Charles, smiling, debonair.

“I thought I could not be mistaken. I would swear to your presence in a room of a thousand people!”

===Chinese===
罗克斯海斯向那个走近国王周围一群人来的颀秀青年一躬身。

“一如公爵大人眼见，”他说。

蒙茅斯（Monmouth）倚在软榻背上，正在查理头顶上方，笑容可掬，风流蕴藉。

“我就知道断乎错不了。便是一千人的屋子，我也敢赌咒认出您来！”

---

===Original===
“You are a flatterer,” Roxhythe shook his head. “You had best visit Versailles.”

Monmouth sighed. He put back his curls with one delicate, white hand.

“I have a mind to. I have a great desire to visit the French Court.”

“Ah, no!” said Charles, quickly, raising his hand. He laid it affectionately on his son’s arm. “I cannot spare you, James.”

===Chinese===
“你真是个谄谀之徒，”罗克斯海斯摇头。“你顶好去凡尔赛见识见识。”

蒙茅斯叹了口气，拿一只纤柔的白手拢了拢鬈发。

“我正有此心。我渴想去看一看法国宫廷。”

“啊，使不得！”查理急急抬手，又慈爱地把手按在儿子臂上。“我舍不得放你走，詹姆斯。”

---

===Original===
“You spared Roxhythe,” shrugged Monmouth. There was a suspicion of triumph in the glance he shot at my lord.

“Needs must,” quoth Charles, ruefully. “Roxhythe stays for no man.”

“Not even for Your Majesty?” asked Lady Castlemaine.

Charles smiled.

“I wonder?” he said. “Can one catch a star?”

===Chinese===
“罗克斯海斯您倒放走了，”蒙茅斯一耸肩。他投向爵爷的眼风里隐含三分得意。

“无可如何呀，”查理怏怏道。“罗克斯海斯是不肯为任何人驻足的。”

“连陛下您也留他不住？”卡斯尔梅恩夫人问。

查理一笑。

“谁知道呢？”他说。“人可捉得住一颗星？”

---

===Original===
“I take it ’tis a question of holding a star,” remarked Killigrew, coming up to them. “ ’Twould be interesting to test Lord Roxhythe his devotion.” He spoke lightly, jestingly, but there was too much of the sneer in his voice for Charles’ liking. The look he gave him was cold.

“See the wretch now!” exclaimed Lady Castlemaine. She was in great good humour today. “I’ll swear he is searching for his ladylove! Are you not, Roxhythe?”

Roxhythe turned his head.

“Which one, Lady Castlemaine?”

===Chinese===
“依我看，难的是拴得住一颗星，”基利格鲁（Killigrew）凑上前来。“罗克斯海斯勋爵的忠心究竟几钱重，倒值得试它一试。”他说得轻飘飘，像句玩笑，可话音里的讥诮太重，查理听着不入耳，冷冷地瞪了他一眼。

“瞧瞧这薄幸种子！”卡斯尔梅恩夫人嚷道。她今日兴致极佳。“我敢赌咒，他正满处找他的心上人呢！是不是，罗克斯海斯？”

罗克斯海斯掉过头来。

“哪一位呢，卡斯尔梅恩夫人？”

---

===Original===
She spread out her hands in mock protest.

“The latest; the dearest!” smiled Monmouth.

“Alas! There is no dearest.”

“What! Dead, my lord?”

“No.” Roxhythe sat down. “Faded, sir.”

===Chinese===
她双手一摊，佯作抗议。

“最新的那位；最疼的那位！”蒙茅斯笑道。

“唉！没有最疼的。”

“什么！死了，大人？”

“不。”罗克斯海斯坐了下来。“是褪了色，阁下。”

---

===Original===
“Already? I thought the little Crosby woman—”

“But I tire so easily,” complained his lordship.

“You are a scoundrel, Roxhythe.” It was the King who spoke. “I vow I do not know why I keep you near me!”

“Oh, I can give Your Majesty the answer to that!” replied Roxhythe, placidly.

===Chinese===
“这就褪了？我还道克罗斯比（Crosby）家那位小妇人——”

“可我这人最容易腻，”爵爷叫屈道。

“你真是个恶棍，罗克斯海斯。”说话的是国王。“我赌神罚誓，真不明白干么把你留在身边！”

“哦，这个么，我能答陛下！”罗克斯海斯安安静静地回道。

---

===Original===
“Give it then!” Charles turned, ready to be amused.

Roxhythe’s eyes travelled slowly round the room.

“Amongst so much virtue. …” he began, and got no further. Protesting, laughing voices chided him.

Charles rose.

===Chinese===
“那便说来！”查理转过身，预备着开开心。

罗克斯海斯的眼睛慢悠悠地环着屋子扫了一圈。

“在这满屋的美德里头……”他才起了个头，便再说不下去。抗议的、大笑的声音纷纷数落他。

查理站起身。

---

===Original===
“Ye all appear horrified at the idea of any virtue being found amongst you,” he sighed, his eyes alight with laughter. “Well, I’ll set your minds at rest. Without doubt ye are the most outrageous subjects in Christendom. David, I want you! I have it in mind to give a ball which shall excel all others ever held between these walls. I must have your sage counsel.” He linked his arm through Roxhythe’s, and shook a reproving finger at his small court. “I’ll leave you to your various wickednesses,” he said. “God wot, ye are not fit company for me!”

A slight stir by the door announced the entrance of the Queen. She came slowly across the room on her way to her apartments, a short, insignificant figure, primly garbed in riding-dress.

Charles withdrew his arm and went to meet her.

===Chinese===
“一提你们中间竟还找得出美德来，你们一个个都像吓着了似的，”他喟然道，眼里燃着笑。“也罢，我叫你们安心。毫无疑问，你们是全基督教世界最无法无天的一班臣民。大卫，我要你！我心里盘算着办一场舞会，要办得盖过这几堵墙里历来有过的任何一场。务须借重你的高明主意。”他挽起罗克斯海斯的手臂，又朝他这一小撮朝臣摇着手指以示责备。“你们各式各样的恶业，我就撇下不管了，”他说。“上帝晓得，你们实在不配给我作伴！”

门口一阵轻微的扰动，通报王后驾到。她缓缓穿过屋子，往自己寝殿去，小小一个不起眼的身影，板板正正一身骑装。

查理抽回手臂，迎上前去。

---

===Original===
Her dark eyes with their vague unhappiness scanned him a thought anxiously as he bent over her hand.

“Well, madam? You have been riding?” The King spoke kindly, as one speaks to a child.

Katherine inclined her head.

===Chinese===
他俯身吻她手时，她那双噙着莫名愁绪的黑眼睛，略带几分不安地端详着他。

“怎么，夫人？骑马去了？”国王和颜悦色，像对孩童说话一般。

凯瑟琳（Katherine）微微颔首。

---

===Original===
“Yes, Sir.” Some of the apathy went out of her voice. “The daffodils are already blooming in the Park.”

“Are they so? It was a pleasant ride, sweet?”

“Not very, Sir. I wished you had been with me.” She looked up at him sadly. “The people would have cheered.”

“Why, madam, they cheer for you!” said Charles, patting her hand.

===Chinese===
“是的，陛下。”她话音里的那股木然淡去了几分。“御苑里的水仙已经开了。”

“是么？这一趟骑得畅快吧，亲爱的？”

“不很畅快，陛下。您若同去就好了。”她凄然仰面望他。“百姓们会欢呼的。”

“哎呀，夫人，他们欢呼的是您呀！”查理拍着她的手说。

---

===Original===
“No,” said the Queen. “No.” She looked round the room. Lady Castlemaine curtseyed as the lacklustre eyes passed her. Katherine made no sign. Then she saw Roxhythe, and smiled. The smile changed her whole face. It held appeal, shy coquetry; it dispersed some of her stiffness.

“I did not know you were in London, my lord,” she said.

Roxhythe came forward at once, and kissed her hand.

“I hope Your Majesty is better than when I left you?” His tone held something near warmth.

===Chinese===
“不，”王后说。“不。”她环视屋子。黯淡的目光扫过时，卡斯尔梅恩夫人屈了屈膝。凯瑟琳毫无表示。随后她看见了罗克斯海斯，笑了。这一笑改换了她整张面孔。里面有哀恳，有羞怯的娇态；她那一身拘谨，也散去了几分。

“我不知道您在伦敦，勋爵大人。”她说。

罗克斯海斯立时趋前，吻了她的手。

“但愿陛下比我辞行那日安好？”他语气里含着近乎温热的东西。

---

===Original===
“I am very well,” answered Katherine. “We have missed you, my lord.”

“Your Majesty is too kind,” he said gravely.

“You should have been with us today,” she continued. “The spring has come so early this year! The flowers are all opening in the Park. It was very pretty.” Her eyes went back to the King, wistfully. Charles had no interest in flowers; he was not attending. Roxhythe it was who answered.

===Chinese===
“我好得很，”凯瑟琳答道。“我们一直惦记您呢，勋爵大人。”

“陛下太客气了，”他一本正经地说。

“您今天本该同我们一道去，”她续道。“今年春天来得这样早！御苑里花儿全开了。好看得很。”她的目光怅怅地回到国王身上。查理于花毫无兴致，压根儿没在听。答话的是罗克斯海斯。

---

===Original===
“I shall beg you to let me come in your train again one day, madam,” he said. “You have not asked me of late.”

“Because I thought you would not care to,” she replied. “I do wish, though, that you might accompany me.”

“I should be very greatly honoured, madam,” he bowed. “I shall await your commands.”

“One would almost imagine that he wanted to ride with her!” murmured Killigrew in Buckingham’s ear. “Is it possible that the fastidious Roxhythe is *épris* in that direction?”

===Chinese===
“我斗胆乞请陛下，他日再许我随您的仪从同行，夫人，”他说。“近来您可不曾邀过我。”

“我怕您不情愿呢，”她答。“不过我实在巴望您能陪我走一遭。”

“那将是我莫大的荣幸，夫人，”他躬身道。“我静候您的吩咐。”

“简直要当他当真想陪她骑马了！”基利格鲁对白金汉（Buckingham）耳语。“莫非百般挑剔的罗克斯海斯，往那个方向竟动了真心？”

---

===Original===
Buckingham shook his head.

“No. He has always been the same with her. I suppose he seeks to curry favour!” He sneered.

Roxhythe followed Charles to his closet. There the King flung himself down on the luxuriously cushioned window-seat.

“I doubt the Queen is not happy,” he remarked, frowning. “She takes no interest in our pleasures and she hath always the air of one moped to death.”

===Chinese===
白金汉摇头。

“不是。他对她向来是这个样子。依我看，无非想讨好邀宠！”他讥诮道。

罗克斯海斯随查理进了内室。国王一歪身，倒进垫衬华软的窗座里。

“我怕王后并不快活，”他皱着眉说。“我们的乐子她一概没有兴致，成天一副闷得要死的神气。”

---

===Original===
“The Queen hath a great regard for Your Majesty,” said Roxhythe deliberately.

Charles jerked an impatient shoulder.

“Oh, ay! Ye think I do not notice her enough. She should be more cheerful. She wearies me—poor woman,” he added. “Ye heard today; the people do not like her nor cheer when she passes. Well, ’tis not my fault.”

“If Your Majesty went with the Queen, the people would cheer,” answered my lord.

===Chinese===
“王后对陛下极存敬爱，”罗克斯海斯不慌不忙地说。

查理不耐烦地一耸肩。

“哦，是啊！你们总说我冷落了她。她本该多些笑模样。她叫我乏味——可怜的女人，”他又添上一句。“你今日也听见了；百姓不喜欢她，她经过时也不欢呼。唉，这可不是我的过错。”

“陛下若陪着王后同去，百姓自会欢呼，”爵爷答道。

---

===Original===
“Roxhythe, I will not be taken to task like this!” said Charles pettishly. “You had best accompany her! The people do always cheer when you ride out. Mayhap she will think ’tis for her.”

“You make a mistake, Sir, when you dub Her Majesty a fool.”

Charles stared at him.

“Are you an admirer?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“罗克斯海斯，我不许你这样抢白我！”查理负气地说。“那你陪她去好了！你出门时，百姓是总要欢呼的。没准儿她便当是为她欢呼了。”

“陛下管王后叫傻子，可是弄错了。”

查理直盯着他。

“你倒是她的倾慕者？”他问。

---

===Original===
“I admire, and have always admired Her Majesty’s brave spirit,” said Roxhythe.

Charles was silent. Presently he changed the subject, speaking coldly.

“Well, did you ascertain Louis his attitude?”

Roxhythe shrugged.

===Chinese===
“王后陛下那份刚勇的心性，我钦佩，一向钦佩，”罗克斯海斯说。

查理不言语了。片刻后他换过话头，语气冷淡。

“好了，路易的态度探明了么？”

罗克斯海斯一耸肩。

---

===Original===
“He realizes that he must come to the movement of your finger, Sir.”

Charles’ brow cleared as if by magic.

“I always thought he would! He may rest assured that I shall move that finger to some purpose. I tell you, Roxhythe, I must have money, or I fall. What is Louis his figure?”

“I do not know, Sir.”

===Chinese===
“他明白，他得跟着您手指的挥动而动，陛下。”

查理的眉头像施了魔法一般舒展开来。

“我一向料定他会！他只管放心，我这根指头动起来，必定动出个名堂。我对你说，罗克斯海斯，我非有钱不可，不然便要垮台。路易开的什么数目？”

“我不知道，陛下。”

---

===Original===
“Arundell said two million.”

“I am sorry to have to disagree with my Lord Arundell, but it is too much.”

“Are you sure, David?”

“As sure as I am over anything, Sir.”

===Chinese===
“阿伦德尔（Arundell）说是两百万。”

“很遗憾不敢苟同阿伦德尔勋爵的高见——这数目太多了。”

“你有把握么，大卫？”

“凡我肯下断语的事，陛下，我都一般有把握。”

---

===Original===
“A pity.” Charles rested his chin in his hand. “Ye think Arundell a fool?”

“No. I do not think he understands the French mind.”

“Nor I. ’Tis for that reason that I want you to help me. Colbert has proposals to put before me.”

M. Colbert de Croissy was the French ambassador, brother to the great Colbert, Minister of Finance in France.

===Chinese===
“可惜。”查理以手支颐。“你说阿伦德尔是个蠢材？”

“不是。我只想他不懂法国人的心性。”

“我也不懂。正为这个，我才要你帮我。科尔贝（Colbert）有几项提议要向我陈说。”

科尔贝·德·克鲁瓦西先生（M. Colbert de Croissy）是法国大使，法国财政大臣、大名鼎鼎的科尔贝的胞弟。

---

===Original===
Roxhythe was amused.

“It should be interesting to hear what he says,” he remarked.

“So I think. Roxhythe, I have seen that Clifford may be won over.”

“Our incorruptible Chancellor!” said Roxhythe. “Well, well!”

===Chinese===
罗克斯海斯觉得有趣。

“听听他怎么说，想必有趣，”他说。

“我也这样想。罗克斯海斯，我看克利福德（Clifford）是有法子拉拢过来的。”

“咱们那位一尘不染的大臣！”罗克斯海斯说。“了不得，了不得！”

---

===Original===
“Not at all. He hath great faith in mine integrity. From what he said I gather that Colbert means to put the matter very plausibly. Arlington is, of course, my man; Arundell also. I propose to summon Clifford to my chamber next week when I shall also receive M. Colbert. Then we shall see how the matter strikes Clifford.”

Roxhythe nodded.

“And what of His Grace of York, Sir?”

“James is mad for the promotion of the Catholic faith in the country. All else will fade before that.”

===Chinese===
“全然不是。他对我的清白正直深信不疑。听他的话音，我料想科尔贝打算把这桩事说得入情入理。阿灵顿自然是我的人；阿伦德尔也是。我打算下星期召克利福德到我内室来，届时也一并接见科尔贝先生。到那时，且看这桩事在克利福德眼里是个什么分量。”

罗克斯海斯点头。

“那么约克公爵大人那边呢，陛下？”

“詹姆斯一心疯魔着要在国内弘传天主教信仰，别的都会在那前头黯然失色。”

---

===Original===
“It seems very well,” said Roxhythe. He sighed.

Charles heard the sigh.

“You do not like it, Roxhythe?”

There was a pause.

“No, Sir, I do not.”

===Chinese===
“那看来很好，”罗克斯海斯说。他叹了口气。

查理听见了那声叹息。

“你不以为然么，罗克斯海斯？”

停了一停。

“不以为然，陛下。”

---

===Original===
“You do not like that I should make an alliance with France?”

“I would it might have been otherwise.”

“But it cannot be otherwise,” fretted the King.

“No, Sir?”

===Chinese===
“你不愿我跟法兰西结盟？”

“但愿事情能是另一个样子。”

“可它偏偏不能，”国王焦灼道。

“不能么，陛下？”

---

===Original===
“You know it cannot! I tried to avoid it by an alliance with Nassau, but he’d have none of it. What else can I do? I must have means or I shall be entirely dependent on Parliament. I am sore beset! And now if you turn against me—”

“I shall never do that, Sir.” The answer came quietly, but very emphatically. “If you want a compact with France you must have it. It matters nothing to me. Only one thing do I consider and that is your pleasure.”

Charles put out his hand.

===Chinese===
“你明知不能！我本想同拿骚（Nassau）结盟避开这一步，可他偏不肯依。教我还能怎样？我没有钱，就得全听议会摆布。我困顿极了！如今若连你也来反对我——”

“那样的事我永远不会做，陛下。”答话来得很平静，却极有分量。“您要与法兰西订约，便订好了。这与我不相干。我只考量一件事，那便是您的欢心。”

查理伸出手来。

---

===Original===
“Ah, David! And yet you dislike it?”

“I dislike it—yes.”

“Because you think I am planning to sell England to Louis?”

His smile crept into Roxhythe’s eyes.

===Chinese===
“唉，大卫！可你还是不喜欢它？”

“不喜欢——是的。”

“因为你以为我打算把英格兰卖给路易？”

笑意悄悄爬进了罗克斯海斯的眼睛。

---

===Original===
“Now you are ridiculous, Sir.”

Charles’ hand gripped his.

“You believe in me?”

“I believe that you will contrive to outwit Louis.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿陛下可荒唐了。”

查理的手紧紧扣住他的手。

“你信得过我？”

“我相信您自有手段，智赢路易。”

---

===Original===
“That is evasive. You think I am not acting for England?”

“I know you are not.”

Charles laughed.

“I suppose you are right. But I do not think I shall harm her.”

===Chinese===
“这是躲闪。你以为我不是为英格兰行事？”

“我知道你不是。”

查理大笑起来。

“想必你是对的。不过我想我不至于伤了她。”

---

===Original===
“Nor I, Sir. And I do not think you will do her any good.”

Charles looked at him curiously.

“Roxhythe—you care for England?”

“I used to, Sir.”

===Chinese===
“我也想不至于，陛下。只是我也不以为您能于她有何益处。”

查理好奇地端详他。

“罗克斯海斯——你把英格兰放在心上么？”

“从前放在心上，陛下。”

---

===Original===
“And now?”

“Nothing counts save your pleasure. I stand or fall with you.”

Brown eyes met brown.

“And naught else counts… not even the Country?” said Charles slowly.

===Chinese===
“如今呢？”

“除了您的欢心，什么都不算数。我同您一体存亡。”

棕色的眼睛迎着棕色的眼睛。

“旁的都不算数了……连国家也不算数？”查理缓缓地说。

---

===Original===
Roxhythe carried the hand that lay in his to his lips.

“Since your ways diverge, Sir, no.”

===Chinese===
罗克斯海斯把躺在掌中的那只手举到唇边。

“既然您同它分道扬镳了，陛下，不算数了。”

## II The Offer / 提议

===Original===
James, Duke of York, sat at the table drumming on it with restless fingers. Charles, the King, lolled as usual on the window-seat, playing with one of his spaniels. Sir Thomas Clifford, Chancellor, sat stiffly by the fire and looked ill-at-ease, which indeed he was. There was silence save for the yapping of the spaniel.

Presently the Duke pushed back his chair.

“Will the man never come?” he said.

Charles did not raise his eyes. Sir Thomas glanced at the clock.

“It—it wants ten minutes to the hour, sir,” he said propitiatingly.

===Chinese===
约克公爵詹姆斯坐在桌边，手指头不耐烦地敲着桌面。国王查理照例懒洋洋地歪在窗座上，逗弄着他养的一只小猎犬。财政大臣托马斯·克利福德爵士（Sir Thomas Clifford）直挺挺地坐在炉火旁，一脸局促不安——事实上他也确实不安。屋里一片寂静，唯有小猎犬的吠声。

不多时，公爵把椅子往后一推。

“那个人到底还来不来了？”他说。

查理眼皮也不抬。托马斯爵士瞥了一眼座钟。

“还——还差十分钟才到点呢，陛下，”他赔着小心说。

---

===Original===
James shrugged impatiently. He looked over at his brother. The spaniel’s yapping jarred on him.

“Is Lord Roxhythe not expected until three, Sir?” he asked.

“No,” answered the King.

“Would it not have been better to have seen him before M. Colbert?” James did not conceal his impatience well.

“Wherefore?”

Charles yawned.

===Chinese===
詹姆斯不耐烦地耸了耸肩。他望向他的兄长。小猎犬的吠声吵得他心烦。

“罗克斯海斯爵爷不是要到三点才来见吗，陛下？”他问。

“不是，”国王答道。

“先召见罗克斯海斯，再见科尔贝先生，岂不更好？”詹姆斯不大掩饰得住自己的性急。

“为何？”

查理打了个哈欠。

---

===Original===
Quick footsteps came along the passage.

“Perhaps this is he?” hazarded Clifford.

“My Lord Roxhythe never hurries,” sneered James. “This is M. Colbert.”

The French ambassador entered, and cast a swift glance round the room. He swept a bow to the King.

“Sire!” He turned. “Altesse!” He turned again. “Sir Thomas!”

===Chinese===
走廊上传来一阵急促的脚步声。

“说不定这就是他了？”克利福德试探道。

“罗克斯海斯爵爷是从不匆忙的，”詹姆斯讥笑道。“这位是科尔贝先生。”

法国大使走了进来，目光倏地扫过满室。他向国王潇洒地深深一躬。

“陛下！”他转过身。“殿下！”他再一转。“托马斯爵士！”

---

===Original===
Charles nodded easily.

“Pray sit down, monsieur. We are delighted to see you.”

James rose jerkily and went forward.

“You are very punctual, M. Colbert. Will you take this chair? We still lack Lord Roxhythe.”

“It still wants three minutes to the hour, sir,” remarked Clifford.

“No doubt ye have heard from His French Majesty?” asked James, ignoring the interruption.

===Chinese===
查理从容地点了点头。

“请坐，先生。见到你，我们很高兴。”

詹姆斯生硬地站起身，迎上前去。

“您真守时，科尔贝先生。请坐这把椅子？我们还缺一位罗克斯海斯爵爷。”

“离整点还差着三分钟呢，殿下，”克利福德插了一句。

“法兰西国王陛下那边，想必已有消息了吧？”詹姆斯问道，全不理会这句插话。

---

===Original===
Colbert bowed.

“A plaguey damp day, eh, monsieur?” drawled the King.

The Frenchman’s dark eyes twinkled.

“As Your Majesty says,” he agreed.

“You don’t have such weather in your country,” went on Charles. “’Tis a fortunate land.”

===Chinese===
科尔贝躬了躬身。

“真是遭瘟的阴雨天，嗯，先生？”国王拖长了腔调说。

法国人一双黑眼睛闪了闪。

“正如陛下所言，”他附和道。

“这样的天气，贵国可是没有的，”查理接着说。“那是一块福地啊。”

---

===Original===
The Duke shut his eyes, exasperated. Charles glanced at the clock.

“The hour,” he said. “And—I think—Roxhythe.”

The three other men turned to look at the door.

It opened. Mr. Chiffinch, the King’s confidential page, announced my Lord Roxhythe and my lord came in unhurriedly.

His chestnut wig was nicely curled, and hung down over his shoulders; his dress was carefully chosen. In one hand he carried his hat; in the other, his comfit-box and cane. His calm, rather ironic eyes travelled slowly round the room, and came to rest on the King. He made a very low bow. Then he made another which included all the room.

===Chinese===
公爵气恼地闭上了眼睛。查理瞥了一眼时钟。

“钟点了，”他说。“而且——我想——是罗克斯海斯。”

其余三人一齐转头去看那扇门。

门开了。国王的心腹侍童奇芬奇先生（Mr. Chiffinch）通传罗克斯海斯爵爷驾到，爵爷便从容不迫地走了进来。

他那头栗色假发卷得恰到好处，垂披肩头；衣着是经心挑拣过的。一手拿着帽子，另一手拿着蜜饯盒和手杖。一双沉静而略带讥诮的眼睛缓缓扫过满屋的人，末了落在国王身上。他深深地躬下腰去。接着又补上一躬，算向满座致意。

---

===Original===
The Duke of York’s brows drew closer together.

“Davy,” sighed the King. “You are punctuality personified! How a-God’s name, do you manage it?”

“I really don’t know, Sir,” said Roxhythe. “I believe it must be a habit.”

“A vice,” answered Charles, mournfully. “One which I do not possess. I think you know M. Colbert?”

“I have that honour,” bowed Roxhythe.

“Then I need present nobody,” said the King. “How very fortunate! Oh, sit down, Davy! sit down!” He waved him to a chair.

===Chinese===
约克公爵的眉头拧得更紧了。

“达维，”国王叹了口气。“你简直是守时的化身！看在上帝份上，你是怎么做到的？”

“我也实在说不上来，陛下，”罗克斯海斯说。“想必是一种习惯吧。”

“是恶习，”查理答道，一脸忧伤。“一种我没有的恶习。我想你认识科尔贝先生吧？”

“深以为荣，”罗克斯海斯躬身道。

“那么我就一个也不必引见了，”国王说。“多叫人庆幸！哦，坐吧，达维！坐吧！”他挥手示意他坐下一把椅子。

---

===Original===
Roxhythe sat down and crossed one leg over the other. He looked expectant.

Charles sighed.

“Proceed, gentlemen,” he counselled them, stroking his dog’s head.

“Do you wish me to—conduct the conversation, Sir?” asked James bluntly.

“Pray do!” begged the King.

===Chinese===
罗克斯海斯坐了下去，一条腿架上另一条。他一脸等着瞧的神气。

查理叹了口气。

“诸位请吧，”他抚着狗脑袋，向二人示意。

“您是要我——来主持这场谈话么，陛下？”詹姆斯直捅捅地问。

“请吧！”国王央道。

---

===Original===
James turned to the Frenchman.

“M. Colbert, you advised His Majesty some time since that you had certain propositions to lay before him, coming from King Louis. For the sake of these other gentlemen, will you be good enough to repeat them?” He sat back in his chair, his eyes keen and alert.

M. Colbert twisted one of his rings round.

“But certainly, m’sieu’. It comes to my master’s ears that King Charles was not quite at one with his Cabinet over the Dutch Alliance. He is relieved, *naturellement*, for he has always been King Charles’ very good friend and cousin. He feared once that the alliance might lead to war between England and France—oh, ridiculous, of course! but there was a chance of such a thing—and he would be loth to fight one whom he wishes so well. Of course. He knows that there are in England, many Catholic gentlemen…” he bowed to Clifford. “… of whom Sir Thomas is one. The interests of the Church are his, as they are of every good Catholic…” he bowed to James. “… he is desirous of seeing England return to the old Faith. He believes, too, that King Charles would give much to see this.”

===Chinese===
詹姆斯转向那法国人。

“科尔贝先生，前些日子您奏知陛下，说您奉路易国王之命，有几项提议须向他当面陈明。为了在座这几位先生起见，可否劳驾您再讲一遍？”他往椅背上一靠，目光锐利而警觉。

科尔贝先生把指上的一枚戒指转了半圈。

“当然可以，先生。风声传到我主上耳中，说查理国王在荷兰同盟一事上，与他的内阁诸公不甚同心。他为此深感宽慰——*naturellement*，这是自然——因为他向来是查理国王极好的朋友和表亲。他曾一度担心，那同盟或许会把英格兰与法兰西推向战端——哦，这自然荒唐！可那样的可能并非没有——而他极不愿同自己满心亲善的对象兵戎相见。自然是如此。他知道，英格兰有许多天主教绅士……”他向克利福德欠身。“……托马斯爵士便是其中一位。教会的事业便是他的事业，一如它是每一位善良天主教徒的事业……”他向詹姆斯欠身。“……他热切盼望着英格兰重回古老的信仰。他还相信，为了见到这一天，查理国王必不惜付出许多。”

---

===Original===
King Charles twisted the spaniel’s ears above its head, and eyed the effect pensively.

“He realizes, however, that it is not an easy matter for King Charles to drive the true Faith into his country—without assistance. But for such a cause—a cause that is ever nearest his heart—His Majesty would readily lend assistance.

“There is also another side, messieurs. For many years England and France have been enemies. His Majesty desires that this shall be so no longer. He would make a compact with England that should seal forever the friendship of two countries that lie at each other’s doors—two countries that are the most powerful in the world. He thinks it more than a pity that such countries should be at enmity, as must be the case if England allies herself with the Provinces.

“His Majesty knows that, together, England and France are all-powerful. Together they might do aught they pleased. It might be thought, messieurs, that His Majesty would be desirous of annexing England for his own. Such could never be the case. His Majesty would never interfere with England save at England’s wish. It might be thought that he would desire England to pay him tribute. He does not. If it should be that France makes war, he would want England to join her, should he call for help. He would share with King Charles, not the expenses, which he would pay himself, but the profits of war. He would hold himself bound to come to England’s aid if ever she should call, exacting naught in payment, messieurs, save a share in the profits. More than that even would he be prepared to do: he would bear all the expenses of any war of his making, and a large share of the expenses of a war on England’s side. You must agree, messieurs, that His Majesty is generous.”

===Chinese===
查理国王把小猎犬的两只耳朵拧到它脑袋顶上，若有所思地审视着这番成果。

“然而他也明白，查理国王要想把真正的信仰推行到他的国土上——若无援助——绝非易事。而为了这样一项事业——一项永远系在他心头的事业——法王陛下当乐于伸出援手。

“诸君，此事还有另一层。多少年来，英格兰与法兰西互为仇敌。法王陛下不愿再让这样的局面继续下去。他要与英格兰缔结一纸盟约，把两个比邻而居的国度——两个举世最强的国度——的友谊永远封缄。依他看，这样的国家竟相互仇视，岂止可惜；而倘若英格兰与联省结了盟，这份仇视便势不可免。

“法王陛下深知，英格兰与法兰西联起手来，便天下无敌；联手之际，想做什么便能成什么。诸君或许会想，法王陛下必欲吞并英格兰，据为己有。断无这等事。除非英格兰自愿，法王陛下绝不干涉英格兰分毫。诸君或许又会想，他要英格兰向他纳贡称臣。他并无此意。倘若法兰西开战，只消他开口求援，他便要英格兰与她并肩作战。他愿与查理国王分享的，不是战费——战费由他自己承担——而是战争的利得。英格兰但有所召，他必驰援，义不容辞；所索取的报酬，诸君，不过利得中的一份罢了。甚至犹不止此：凡由他挑起的战争，开销他一力承担；战火若烧至英格兰一方，开销他也分担一大半。诸君总得承认，法王陛下是慷慨的。”

---

===Original===
Clifford moved, turning his head.

“To what ends, monsieur?”

“To the end, Sir Thomas, that he may prove himself England’s friend, and the Church’s friend.”

Clifford pursed his lips.

“If this be so, His Majesty is indeed generous,” he said.

The Frenchman inclined his head.

===Chinese===
克利福德动了动，转过头来。

“所图者何，先生？”

“所图者，托马斯爵士，是要证明自己是英格兰之友、教会之友。”

克利福德抿紧了嘴唇。

“果真如此，法王陛下倒真当得起慷慨二字，”他说。

法国人微微颔首。

---

===Original===
“There is another private matter,” he pursued, more slowly. “His Majesty apprehends that King Charles finds it difficult to live as should live a Monarch of his degree.” He turned to Charles. “Your Majesty will forgive me if I speak too plainly.”

Charles nodded casually.

“I thank Your Majesty. King Louis, I say, knows this. During the years of his exile, King Charles his purse was very surely drained, as was natural, in his efforts to come back to an ungrateful people. The many grievous losses that he sustained could not be repaid to him in full. His Majesty, my master, hath a great regard for his cousin; it grieves him to see King Charles in any way pressed for money. He, King Louis, has not had to bear such drains on his private purse, and he is, as you, messieurs, no doubt know, moderately wealthy. Because of this love which he bears King Charles, he is anxious to supply him with means wherewith to live as he should. He knows that King Charles is too noble, too kindly a man to tax his people as did—forgive me, Sire—his father. He thinks it little less than shameful that by reason of this true nobility of mind King Charles should be in want. Quite privately he would desire to make King Charles an allowance. This he could not do, as you must realize, messieurs, if England were at enmity with France. But this private matter has naught to do with the other matter of which I have already spoken. It is a gift from one cousin to another, if King Charles will have it so, and not deem himself insulted by King Louis his offer.”

===Chinese===
“另有一件私事，”他放缓语速，接着说。“法王陛下知悉，查理国王度日艰难，难以维持他这样身份的一位君主应有的体统。”他转向查理。“倘若我话说得太直，还望陛下海涵。”

查理漫不经心地点点头。

“多谢陛下。方才说了，路易国王深知此情。流亡的岁月里，查理国王为求回到这片忘恩负义的土地，私帑自然被掏了个干净。他蒙受的种种惨重损失，无人能够如数偿还。我的主上路易国王，对他这位表亲怀着极深的敬重；眼见查理国王手头困窘，他便心中难过。他路易国王从不曾受过这般私帑之累，而且他——想必诸君清楚——家资颇为殷实。出于他对查理国王的这一份情爱，他急欲接济他一笔款项，让他活得如仪合体。他知道，查理国王为人太高贵、太仁厚，绝不会像——恕我直言，陛下——他先王那样向百姓横征暴敛。他认为，查理国王竟因这份真正的高贵情怀而陷于匮乏，简直近乎耻辱。他极愿全然私下里给查理国王一笔年金。诸君想必明白，倘若英格兰与法兰西交恶，此事便无从谈起。但这桩私事，与我方才谈过的事毫不相干。这是表亲赠与表亲的一份礼物——只要查理国王肯笑纳，不把路易国王这番提议看作对自己的羞辱。”

---

===Original===
There was a long pause.

Roxhythe played idly with the tassels of his glove, his face inscrutable. James was looking at Clifford, who sat staring into the fire.

“It is for you, Sir Thomas, to speak,” said the Duke at last.

Clifford raised his head. He spoke bluntly.

“M. Colbert, King Louis his offer would appear to be generous to an extreme. Yet this offer of money to His Majesty savours too much of bribery for my liking.”

The Duke of York stiffened. Over M. Colbert’s face came a look of pained surprise. Roxhythe stopped playing with his gloves. Only Charles paid no heed.

===Chinese===
屋里静了许久。

罗克斯海斯百无聊赖地把弄着手套上的穗子，脸上的神色深不可测。詹姆斯望着克利福德，后者干坐着，直勾勾地盯着炉火。

“该由您来说话了，托马斯爵士，”公爵终于开口。

克利福德抬起头来。他说话直来直去。

“科尔贝先生，路易国王这番提议，看上去慷慨到了极点。可这份送到陛下跟前的钱，贿赂的气味太重，不合我的口味。”

约克公爵全身一僵。科尔贝先生脸上掠过痛心的诧异之色。罗克斯海斯不再把玩手套。唯有查理浑不在意。

---

===Original===
“Sir Thomas, I am sure you cannot realize that such a suggestion is little less than an insult to His Majesty!” said James harshly.

“And to my master,” came haughtily from Colbert.

“I crave His Majesty’s pardon if I offended,” answered Clifford, red to the ears. “But I still say that the offer has that appearance.”

James was about to reply, but with a deprecating smile Colbert forestalled him.

“You are perfectly right, Sir Thomas. To an evil-thinking world that to no man accords the desire to do good for goodness’ sake alone, King Louis’ entirely disinterested offer smacks of bribery. It is for this reason that he would wish the matter kept secret. He expects to gain nothing by this offer. It is made out of his love for King Charles, not from any desire of gain for himself. He could not, of course, voice such a proposal were England and France at disagreement. I have given you his reasons for wishing their friendship; this private offer to King Charles is no part of it. It has naught to do with the State; it is between man and man. Yet His Majesty foresaw that the public, who, being low-minded, credit all others with their same motives, would cry shame to King Charles for accepting a bribe. The muck and run of men, Sir Thomas, will not believe that a man can be generous, hoping to gain nothing by his generosity. I had not judged you to be of this class; I still do not. I know that you spoke in the heat of the moment, not giving yourself time to reflect. Had you done so, you would have seen how wrong—pardon me—how base were your suspicions.”

===Chinese===
“托马斯爵士，我相信您没有意识到，这样的说法简直无异于对国王陛下的侮辱！”詹姆斯声色俱厉。

“也侮辱了我的主上，”科尔贝傲然接道。

“倘有冒犯，臣恳请陛下恕罪，”克利福德答道，耳根子红到了耳朵尖。“可我还是要说，那提议就是那副嘴脸。”

詹姆斯正要答话，科尔贝却带着一脸不以为意的笑容抢在了前头。

“您说得千真万确，托马斯爵士。世人惯以歹心度人，从不肯相信有人行善只为行善本身；在那号人眼里，路易国王这份全无私心的提议，自然满是贿赂的气味。正因如此，他才愿此事秘而不宣。他并不指望靠这份提议捞到什么。此举全然出于他对查理国王的情爱，绝非图谋一己之利。当然，倘若英格兰与法兰西失和，这样的提议他也就无从出口。我已向诸位陈明他盼望两国交好的缘由；这份献给查理国王的私人馈赠，并不在其中。它与国事无涉，只是人与人之间的事。然而法王陛下早有预见：那班心术低下的世人，总拿自己那副心肠去揣度别人，定会大骂查理国王收受贿赂，斥他无耻。那班人间的渣滓浊流，托马斯爵士，是绝不肯相信一个人能够慷慨为怀而不图回报的。我原本没有把您归到这路人里头；如今也不。我知道您是一时激愤，没给自己留出思量的工夫。倘若您思量了，便会看出您的疑心错得——恕我直言——卑劣到何等地步。”

---

===Original===
Clifford met his reproachful look, and stammered hopelessly.

“His Majesty knows—that I—that you—that I meant no offence—by what I said. Perhaps—that is, of course, I spoke heatedly. I would not accuse King Louis—of—of descending to—bribery. I could not think that His Majesty—” he cast a flurried glance at Charles, “—would countenance a—a bribe. I—I but said it savoured of that, as—as I think it does, sir!”

“It is for that reason that the matter should be kept secret, Sir Thomas. I confess, if I had thought you would take this tone I had not mentioned the matter to you. Knowing you to be an upright man, I had hardly expected you to impute dishonourable motives to others.”

Sir Thomas collapsed.

“Sir, you misunderstand me! I—I meant no rudeness to His Majesty! I—” he floundered hopelessly and stopped.

===Chinese===
克利福德迎着他那责备的目光，语无伦次地结巴起来。

“陛下知道——我——您——我方才的话没有恶意。也许——就是说，当然，我说得太急了。我不敢指摘路易国王——会——会堕落到——行贿。我万万不敢认为陛下——”他慌忙朝查理瞟了一眼，“——会纵容一件——一件贿赂。我——我只是说那事有那个味道，因为——因为我觉得它确有那个味道，先生！”

“正是为此，此事才应秘而不宣，托马斯爵士。不瞒您说，早知您会拿出这种腔调，我便不会向您提起此事。我素知您是个方正之人，原没料到您竟会把不名誉的动机栽到旁人头上。”

托马斯爵士垮了下来。

“先生，您错怪我了！我——我对陛下绝无唐突之意！我——”他狼狈地支吾着，终于住口。

---

===Original===
M. Colbert wiped his lips with a delicately laced handkerchief.

“I am much relieved to hear you say so, Sir Thomas. May I now go on to my next point?”

The Duke nodded shortly.

“It is this: His Majesty is fully alive to the fact that there are in your English Cabinet many honest gentlemen who are yet very stubborn and narrow. They, like many other ignorant people, are averse from dealing with France. They are Protestants, messieurs. They may be drawn to the right way of looking at a French alliance, but at present they will not listen to reason. Once it was proved to them that France desired nothing but friendship from England, their objections would fade. They would see that an alliance with France was for their country’s good. In holding away from it they, all unwittingly, work great harm on England. They cannot be brought round at present, but is it to be permitted that they should do England this great wrong? They have proved stubborn; they have showed that they will not listen to argument. Shall they not then be ignored? They would raise an outcry; they would prevail upon the ignorance of the people; they would prevent an alliance. Then they must know naught until the thing is done, when they will soon see that it is not a great evil, but a great good.”

“You mean a secret treaty?” asked Clifford blankly.

“Secret only for the moment,” promptly replied Colbert. “Think on what I have said, monsieur, and tell me if this great thing for England is to be quenched by a party of dogged Protestants.”

===Chinese===
科尔贝先生拿一方绣着精致蕾丝的手帕拭了拭嘴唇。

“听您这么说我便放心多了，托马斯爵士。眼下容我讲下一点，可好？”

公爵干巴巴地点了点头。

“是这么回事：法王陛下十分清楚，贵英国内阁之中，有许多诚实的君子，却也非常顽固狭隘。他们同其他许多无知之辈一样，不愿与法兰西往来。他们是新教徒，诸君。本可以引他们走上看待法英同盟的正路，只是眼下他们不肯听道理。一旦向他们证明，法兰西对英格兰别无他求、唯求友谊，他们的反对自会烟消云散。他们自会看清，与法兰西结盟是为他们的国家好。他们一味疏远，便在不知不觉间给英格兰造成大害。眼下他们是劝不转的，可难道就该容许他们对英格兰犯下这等大恶么？他们已表现出顽固；已表明不肯听人论理。那么，难道不该把他们撇过一边么？他们定会大嚷大叫；定会煽动无知的民众；定会阻挠结盟。故而在事成之前，须让他们一无所知；事成之后，他们很快便会看出，这不是大恶，而是大善。”

“您是指一纸密约？”克利福德茫然地问。

“只在眼下保密，”科尔贝应声答道。“细想一下我说的话吧，先生，再告诉我：这桩对英格兰天大的好事，难道该断送在一伙死硬的新教徒手里不成？”

---

===Original===
“I do see the truth in what you say, sir, but there are many points against it.”

“Will you not name them, Sir Thomas?”
