Mademoiselle de Kéroualle, now Duchess of Portsmouth, and mistress-in-chief, came in his train, and was pleased to be very fascinating; the Duke of Monmouth, in high spirits, could talk of nothing but the prospect of his journey to Holland next year at the head of the English troops.

Christopher lived the few days in a positive whirl of excitement. He was very busy, for Roxhythe paid no attention to any of the arrangements, but this did not trouble him. He was helping to entertain the most gracious King in the world; he had kissed this King’s hand; the King had spoken to him several times; his cup was full.

===Chinese===
贝文庄园之行，处处圆满。国王受到极尽奢华的款待。拉凯鲁阿勒小姐——如今已是朴茨茅斯公爵夫人（Duchess of Portsmouth）、位居首席的情妇——随驾同行，欣然使出浑身妩媚，顾盼生姿；蒙茅斯公爵兴高采烈，满口不离明年统领英军远征荷兰的前景。

那几天，克里斯托弗着实过得像在一阵欢腾的旋风里。他忙得不可开交——罗克斯海斯对种种安排一概不闻不问——但他毫不以为意。他是在襄助款待世上最宽仁的国王；他吻过这位国王的手；国王同他讲过好几回话；他已心满意足到了极处。

---

===Original===
All through the winter Roxhythe continued to make trifling and spasmodic love to Lady Crewe. Christopher watched, troubled. He saw that Millicent was taking his master very seriously, and he was sorry, for he knew that Roxhythe was beginning to weary of her. He dared not speak to him on the subject, but he was a tenderhearted boy, and he wished that my lord would amuse himself with someone older, and more accustomed to the game. He saw that Millicent was not treating it as a game any longer. He saw also that Sir Henry Crewe was less and less at his wife’s side, and had begun to look at her coldly. He wondered how it would end.

Meanwhile, great changes were taking place in England. Ashley, now the Earl of Shaftesbury, was Chancellor, and led the Cabinet. The long recess of Parliament still went on. The Country party was more than ever opposed to the Court party and dissension was growing apace.

From Harcourt Christopher learned that the Declaration of Indulgence was becoming more and more unpopular. War with Holland was imminent; the King continued to amuse himself.

===Chinese===
整整一冬，罗克斯海斯照旧时断时续地向克鲁夫人献些无足轻重的殷勤。克里斯托弗看在眼里，心里发沉。他看出米莉森特把他的主子当了真，很是难过，因为他知道罗克斯海斯已渐渐对她生厌。这事他不敢对爵爷启齿，但他是个心肠软的孩子，但愿爵爷换个年长些、更精于此道的人来解闷。他看出米莉森特已不再当它是一场游戏。他还看出亨利·克鲁爵士（Sir Henry Crewe）日益少在妻子身边，而且开始用冷眼瞧她。他不知道这将如何收场。

与此同时，英格兰正经历着巨变。阿什利——如今的沙夫茨伯里伯爵（Earl of Shaftesbury）——已就任大法官（Chancellor），执掌内阁。议会的长期休会仍在继续。乡村党与宫廷党的对峙更胜往昔，龃龉一日千里。

克里斯托弗从哈考特那里得悉，《信教自由宣言》愈来愈不得人心。对荷兰的战事一触即发；国王照旧自行其乐。

---

===Original===
Roxhythe still ignored the universal unrest. Christopher was more than ever sure that he had no interest in politics, was of no party, and was above all the petty jealousies and intrigues that went on daily. Now and then he went to Paris, but mostly he was at Whitehall, pursuing his brilliant course. Christopher no longer puzzled over these visits to France. Roxhythe had assured him that his vague fears were groundless; he trusted Roxhythe.

And so the year crept out, and the New Year was upon them.

===Chinese===
罗克斯海斯照旧无视这漫天的风潮。克里斯托弗愈发确信：他对政治毫无兴味，无所偏党，超然于日常上演的种种小小猜忌与阴谋之上。他偶尔去巴黎，但多半身在白厅，走他那条风光显赫的门路。克里斯托弗不再为这些法兰西之行纳闷。罗克斯海斯向他担保过，他那些隐隐的忧惧纯属无根；他信任罗克斯海斯。

就这样，一年挨挨蹭蹭地走到了尽头，新年已临到他们头上。

## Book
 III Quo Vadis?

## Book III Quo Vadis? / 第三部 何去何从？

## I The New Master / 新主人

===Original===
In April of 1677 Christopher left Roxhythe after nine long years and took rooms in Cheapside. After the first struggle he seemed to sink into a state of apathy. He hardly stirred from his rooms and he received no one. At present he was living in some horrible nightmare; he could not even now realise all that had happened.

In May of the same year Lady Frances returned from Scotland where she had been staying. She made her curtsey to the King at Whitehall and stayed by his side for some time, laughing and talking with him in a reminiscent vein. After that she exchanged frivolities with Lord Buckhurst. It was at that moment that Roxhythe appeared on the scene.

He stood for some while by the King, but presently he perceived his cousin and came across the room towards her.

===Chinese===
1677 年四月，克里斯托弗在漫长的九年之后离开了罗克斯海斯，在齐普赛街（Cheapside）赁屋住下。头一阵挣扎过后，他仿佛沉入了一种麻木呆滞的状态。他几乎足不出户，也不见任何来客。此刻他还活在一场可怕的梦魇里；直到如今，他仍不能明白已发生的一切究竟是怎么回事。

同年五月，弗朗西丝夫人（Lady Frances）从她小住的苏格兰回来。她在白厅向国王行过屈膝礼，便在国王身边待了一阵，同他笑着谈着，叙一番旧话。随后她又同巴克赫斯特勋爵（Lord Buckhurst）打趣调笑起来。就在那一刻，罗克斯海斯来到了眼前。

他在国王身边站了一会儿，不多时瞥见了他的表妹，便穿过屋子朝她走来。

---

===Original===
Lady Frances gave him her hand.

“Well, David!” She eyed Lord Buckhurst with her head on one side. “Dear me, Charles, I believe Lady Finchley wants you!”

He laughed in answer to her twinkling glance.

“Which means that you do not? Very well! I’ll go!” He strolled off to join Killigrew.

Lady Frances smiled up at Roxhythe.

===Chinese===
弗朗西丝夫人向他伸出手去。

“哟，大卫！”她偏着头，眼睛打量着巴克赫斯特勋爵。“哎呀，查尔斯，我瞧芬奇利夫人（Lady Finchley）要找你呢！”

他一笑，算是回答她那闪光的斜睨。

“这就是说，你并不想要我？好得很！我走便是！”他信步走开，找基利格鲁（Killigrew）去了。

弗朗西丝夫人仰起脸，朝罗克斯海斯微笑。

---

===Original===
“Charles is very charming, is he not?” she said. “Sit down, David. How are you?”

“The same as ever,” he answered. “And you?”

“How do I look?” she parried.

“Marvellous!” he said lazily.

“Then that is how I feel. How is Chris?”

===Chinese===
“查尔斯这个人很有魅力，是不是？”她说。“坐吧，大卫。你好吗？”

“老样子，”他答道。“你呢？”

“你瞧我模样如何？”她挡了回去。

“好得很！”他懒洋洋地说。

“那么我心里也正如此。克里斯怎么样？”

---

===Original===
My lord regarded the rosettes on his shoes.

“I really don’t know. He has left me.”

Lady Frances gasped.

“Left you? Christopher? Good gracious, Roxhythe, what has happened?”

“We had a difference of opinion and he decided that our ways lay apart.”

===Chinese===
爵爷端详起自己鞋上的缎结花来。

“我还真不知道。他离开我了。”

弗朗西丝夫人倒抽了一口气。

“离开你？克里斯托弗？我的天，罗克斯海斯，这是出了什么事？”

“我们起了一场争执，他便认定我们的路数该各走各的了。”

---

===Original===
Lady Frances to some extent recovered her composure. She laid a compelling hand on his arm.

“Roxhythe, you must have shown yourself very vile! I insist on knowing everything!”

“I am sorry to have to disappoint you, my dear. Suffice it that we agreed to part.”

“It does not suffice! Something terrible must have happened to induce Chris to leave you.”

“No, not at all.”

===Chinese===
弗朗西丝夫人多少镇定了下来。她伸一只手按住他的胳膊，带着不容抗拒的意思。

“罗克斯海斯，你准是把自个儿表现得下作极了！我非要问个一清二楚不可！”

“很遗憾，只好叫你失望了，亲爱的。只消知道我们议定了分手，也就尽够。”

“这尽不了够！必定是出了什么可怕的事，才会叫克里斯离开你。”

“没有，全然没有。”

---

===Original===
“Roxhythe, do not play with me! He is—disillusioned?”

“Thoroughly.”

“He knows that you are not—so idle?”

My lord raised his brows.

“Oh, pho! You know very well that I see through your pose! Others may be blind, but I am not. You are the King his man.”

===Chinese===
“罗克斯海斯，别耍弄我！他这算是——幻梦醒了？”

“醒了个透。”

“他知道你并不是——当真那么闲散？”

爵爷挑起了眉毛。

“啐，得了！我看得穿你那副伪装，你自己一清二楚！别人或许眼瞎，我不瞎。你是国王的人。”

---

===Original===
“Is not this a rather public spot wherein to discuss such matters?”

“Has Chris found out?”

“Why not ask him?”

“I shall! Have no fear of that! But I want it from your lips. Oh, come, David! I too have lived in intrigue; I am not blameless myself. Chris discovered that you were plotting?”

===Chinese===
“在这儿——大庭广众的场合——谈论这等事，怕不甚相宜吧？”

“克里斯发觉了？”

“那怎么不去问他？”

“我自然要问！这一点你放心！可我要亲耳听你说的。哦，行了，大卫！我也在阴谋堆里泡过，我自己也说不上干净。克里斯发觉了你在搞密谋？”

---

===Original===
“Something of the sort!”

“And so he left you? No, that is not enough. You used him?”

“You should have been born a man, my dear.”

“My mother knew better. Did you use Christopher?”

“You weary me,” said Roxhythe. “You were never wont to do that. I did use him.”

===Chinese===
“差不多是这一类的事！”

“于是他就走了？不，不止如此。你利用了他？”

“你呀，真该托生成个男人，亲爱的。”

“我母亲可比这明白。你到底利用了克里斯托弗没有？”

“你叫我腻烦了，”罗克斯海斯说。“你从前从不曾这样。我的确利用了他。”

---

===Original===
“Then you are utterly without a heart, without shame! You are loathsome!” said my lady vehemently.

“You always knew that I had no heart. Shame is an unknown quantity. But as to loathsome … h’m!”

“It is true. Oh, David, why did you do it?”

“I forget. There was a reason.”

“For heaven’s sake don’t be flippant!” she snapped. “Where is Christopher?”

===Chinese===
“这么说你是全然没有心肝，也没有廉耻！你真可憎！”夫人激切地说。

“没有心肝，你向来知道。廉耻为何物，我素来不懂。至于可憎么……嗯！”

“倒是不假。哦，大卫，你为什么要这样？”

“我忘了。总归是有个缘故的。”

“看在老天份上，别油嘴滑舌！”她抢白道。“克里斯托弗在哪儿？”

---

===Original===
“In rooms. 94, Cheapside.”

“I shall tell him to visit me. Perhaps he will be more explicit!”

“I doubt it.” My lord smiled insufferably.

“We shall see. I suppose you have killed his love for you?”

“On the contrary.”

===Chinese===
“赁了屋子住。齐普赛街 94 号。”

“我要他来见我。或许他肯说得爽利些！”

“我瞧未必。”爵爷笑得叫人受不了。

“走着瞧吧。想必你把他对你的爱恋已经杀死了？”

“恰恰相反。”

---

===Original===
“Do you mean to say that Christopher still adores you?”

“I believe so.”

“And you sit there and tell me that in that calm, disinterested way! Roxhythe, I have never found you less to my taste!”

He looked into her flashing eyes. She was sitting very straight.

“Well, my dear, there is a remedy.” He rose. “Pray give me leave!”

She nodded angrily. My lord strolled back to the King.

===Chinese===
“你的意思是说，克里斯托弗至今还把你崇拜得五体投地？”

“我想是。”

“你就那么坐着，拿那副平静而事不关己的腔调讲给我听！罗克斯海斯，你从没有像今天这样叫我倒足胃口！”

他直望着她那双怒火闪亮的眼睛。她坐得笔直。

“呃，亲爱的，这病有药可医。”他站起身来。“请容我告退！”

她气冲冲地点了点头。爵爷踱回国王身边去了。

---

===Original===
Next day a note was brought to Christopher. The serving-maid bore it up to his room.

Christopher was trying to write to his brother. The task was a difficult one. It was hard to acknowledge himself to have been in the wrong throughout.

The serving-maid gave a sniff and proffered the note. When he saw Lady Frances’ handwriting a little colour came to Christopher’s pale cheeks and he tore the letter open. It was very short.

> “Deare Chris.—Come and See me this After Noon.—**F. M.**”

===Chinese===
第二天，一封短简送到了克里斯托弗手上。女仆把它送上了楼来。

克里斯托弗正试着给他哥哥写信。这差事好生艰难。要自认从始至终一概是错，谈何容易。

女仆抽了抽鼻子，把短简递上前来。一见是弗朗西丝夫人的笔迹，克里斯托弗苍白的两颊浮起一丝血色，他把信拆开了。信很短。

> “亲爱的克里斯——今日午后请来见我。——**弗·蒙**”

---

===Original===
“Do they await an answer?” asked Christopher.

“No, sir.” The girl twisted her apron between her fingers and giggled a little for no better reason than that she admired him. “The footman went away at once.”

“Thank you.”

The maid departed, clattering across the floor in shoes two sizes too large for her. She was something of a contrast to the well-trained lackeys at Bevan House.

===Chinese===
“要等回话么？”克里斯托弗问。

“不等，先生。”姑娘把围裙在指间绞来绞去，咯咯笑了两声——没什么别的缘故，不过因为瞧着这位先生喜欢。“听差放下信就走了。”

“多谢。”

女仆去了，一双大了两号的鞋把地板踩得咯噔咯噔响。比起贝文府里调教有素的跟班，她总算得是另一路人物。

---

===Original===
That afternoon Christopher surveyed his many suits deliberately. If he was to wait on Lady Fanny he must be carefully dressed. At the back of the cupboard hung a brown velvet suit, heavily laced with gold. Christopher fingered it dreamily. He had worn this coat last when he had returned from Flanders. … Then there was the blue cloth with its cream facings. That had been bought for a garden-party at Lady Pommeroy’s house. Roxhythe had worn apricot velvet. … He put it back slowly. The lilac velvet? No. … That had been his summer suit down at Bevan last year. … The grey cloth with the pale blue ribbons? … Not that. Roxhythe had approved of that dress. What was it he had said? … Almost roughly Christopher thrust it back into the cupboard. From its depths he drew a green coat laced with silver. He had seldom worn this, thinking it ugly. Well, it should be worn now. He shut the cupboard.

===Chinese===
那天下午，克里斯托弗把自己的许多套衣裳细细检点了一番。既要去拜望芬妮夫人，穿戴着务必考究。柜子深处挂着一套棕色天鹅绒装，金线花边镶得密密实实。克里斯托弗恍恍惚惚地拿指头摩挲它。上一回穿这身外套，还是他打佛兰德（Flanders）回来那趟。……再有便是那套蓝呢装，镶着乳白的边。那是为着波莫罗伊夫人（Lady Pommeroy）府上的游园会置办的。罗克斯海斯那天穿的是杏色天鹅绒。……他慢慢把它放回原处。淡紫的那套天鹅绒？不。……那是去年在贝文乡间度夏时的衣裳。……灰呢配淡蓝缎带的那件？……别穿它。罗克斯海斯夸过那身衣裳。他说的是句什么来着？……克里斯托弗近乎粗暴地把它塞回柜子里去。他从柜底拖出一件镶银线的绿外套。这衣裳他难得上身，嫌它丑。也罢，如今就该穿它。他关上了柜门。

---

===Original===
An hour later he was ushered into Lady Fanny’s boudoir.

Frances looked up quickly, scanning his face as he made his leg. She was shocked at the change that had taken place. There was no sparkle in his eyes, no colour in his face. He had aged extraordinarily since last she had seen him.

“My very dear Chris!” She came forward, hands outstretched.

He took them in his.

“How kind of you to invite me, Lady Frances! I did not know you were in town.” His voice was graver than of old.

===Chinese===
一个钟头后，他被引进了芬妮夫人的闺房。

弗朗西丝倏地抬起头，就着他打躬行礼的当儿细看他的脸。眼前这变化叫她心头一惊。他眼里没有了光彩，脸上没有了血色。自上回相见，他竟苍老得不成样子。

“我最最亲爱的克里斯！”她张开双手迎上前来。

他伸手握住了那双手。

“承蒙您相邀，弗朗西丝夫人！我不知道您进了城。”他的嗓音比往昔沉重了。

---

===Original===
“Dear boy, it was more of a summons!” she smiled. “Now come and sit down beside me and tell me everything.” She drew him to a couch.

“I don’t think that there is much to tell,” said Christopher, trying hard to sound unconcerned. “You heard that I have left Roxhythe?”

“Yes, Chris. Tell me all about it.” She patted his hand as she spoke. “You know that I can be discreet.”

“It’s nothing, Lady Fanny. I found that I wanted to leave—so I—left.” He spoke with would-be lightness.

===Chinese===
“好孩子，那与其说是相邀，不如说是传召！”她莞尔一笑。“来，坐到我身边来，把什么都讲给我听。”她拉他在软榻上坐下。

“我想并没有多少可讲的，”克里斯托弗说道，竭力装出无所谓的声口。“您听说我离开罗克斯海斯了？”

“听说了，克里斯。原原本本讲给我听。”她说着，拍拍他的手。“你知道，我的口风是紧的。”

“没什么，芬妮夫人。我发觉自己想走——于是我便——走了。”他极力说得轻飘些。

---

===Original===
“Chris, that is not enough. I have seen Roxhythe, and I know that something has happened.”

“Oh, no! I was tired of playing secretary. I am a man of leisure now!”

“And do you like it, Chris?”

He looked away.

“Tell me, dear boy. …”

===Chinese===
“克里斯，这不够。我见过罗克斯海斯了，我知道是出了事。”

“哦，没有的事！我当秘书当腻了。如今我是个有闲的人啦！”

“那你喜欢么，克里斯？”

他把目光移开了。

“讲给我听，好孩子。……”

---

===Original===
“I can’t!”

“You can. Roxhythe has treated you shamefully I know.”

A wry smile twisted his lips.

“Don’t say you warned me!”

“Of course I shall not! He used you in some way? tricked you?”

“That I cannot tell you. But you will be glad to know—that my eyes are open—at last.”

===Chinese===
“我讲不出！”

“你讲得出。罗克斯海斯待你不义，我知道。”

一丝苦笑扭歪了他的嘴角。

“您别说早警告过我！”

“我自然不会！他在什么事情上用了你？诓了你？”

“这个我不能讲。不过有一桩你听了会高兴——我的眼睛睁开了——到底睁开了。”

---

===Original===
“I am not glad, dear. I am very, very sorry. You thought he was—”

“I thought he was the soul of honour and truth. Well, I was mistaken.”

“He has hurt you badly, then. It was bound to happen. He lives only for the King. It is his one good point.”

“Not at all. He has many good points. Don’t think that I have ceased to care for him! I love him as much as before—but I—cannot live with him. Shall we talk of something else?” His eyes pleaded.

“Yes, Chris. We will talk of what you contemplate doing.”

===Chinese===
“我并不高兴，亲爱的。我难受得很，很得很。你原以为他是——”

“我原以为他是荣誉与真诚的化身。唉，我看错了。”

“这么说，他把你伤得很深。这原是迟早的事。他活着只为国王一个人。这是他唯一的好处。”

“全然不是。他的好处多着呢。别当我不再疼他了！我爱他一如往昔——只是我——没法同他一处过活。咱们谈点别的可好？”他的眼睛在央告。

“好，克里斯。咱们谈谈你打算做什么。”

---

===Original===
“I hardly know. I thought I would continue to be a man of leisure. I find I must have some occupation.”

“Quite right. What have you thought of?”

“A secretaryship, I suppose.”

Lady Frances nodded briskly.

“You’ve someone in mind?”

===Chinese===
“我也说不上来。我本想着就一直做个有闲人。可我发现总得有桩事做才行。”

“很对。你想过什么主意？”

“我想，还是当秘书吧。”

弗朗西丝夫人爽利地点了点头。

“心里有人选了？”

---

===Original===
“Not yet.”

“Then I know the very thing for you!”

Christopher looked apprehensive.

“What is it, Lady Frances?”

“Do you know Sir Richard Worth?”

“One of the Country Party?”

===Chinese===
“还没有。”

“那我知道有一桩再合适你不过的事！”

克里斯托弗显得惴惴不安。

“什么事，弗朗西丝夫人？”

“你可认得理查德·沃思爵士（Sir Richard Worth）？”

“乡村党的人？”

---

===Original===
“Yes; under Cavendish.”

“I believe I have seen him. I do not know him.”

“It so happens that he is in need of a secretary. Jasper knows him. You must apply for the post.”

“Oh—oh—I do not think so!”

“Indeed yes! It is the very thing for you. You want occupation, and it had best be with a man very different from Roxhythe.”

===Chinese===
“正是；在卡文迪什（Cavendish）麾下。”

“想必见过他。人并不相识。”

“偏巧他正要觅一名秘书。贾斯珀认得他。这个位置你非去谋不可。”

“哦——哦——我怕不妥吧！”

“非也，务必去！这差事再合适你不过。你要有桩营生，而且顶好是在一位同罗克斯海斯全然两路的人手下。”

---

===Original===
“But I doubt—I am not fitted for—public work.”

“You will learn. Your name stands in your favour—”

“And my nine years’ service to Roxhythe stands well against me.”

“That is true, of course. However, I shall see what can be done. I have bidden Sir Richard to dinner tonight. I shall talk gently to him.”

“Please do not, Lady Frances! Indeed, I do not think that I want to be his secretary!”

===Chinese===
“可是我怕——公务一途——我干不来。”

“学得出。你的家名于你有利——”

“而我伺候罗克斯海斯的这九年，于我却是大大的不利。”

“这话自然不假。不过，且看我能想些什么法子。今晚我请了理查德爵士来吃饭，我会同他软语相商的。”

“求求您别这样，弗朗西丝夫人！说真的，我不想做他的秘书！”

---

===Original===
“Whose then?”

“I don’t know—I—”

“Very well then! No, you must not argue! You do not want to do anything but mope at home. And I say you are not to. Have you any objection to Worth?”

“No. I know nothing about him.”

“Then apply for the post. Ah, Chris, please!”

“It’s very kind of you, Lady Fanny. I’ll apply for it. After all, what odds does it make whom I serve?”

===Chinese===
“那想当谁的？”

“我不知道——我——”

“好，那就这样！不，你不许争！你除了成天闷坐在家里发愁，什么也不想干。我说了，不许这样。沃思这人，你可有不愿意的地方？”

“没有。我只是对他一无所知。”

“那就去谋这个位置。啊，克里斯，我求你！”

“您太好了，芬妮夫人。我去谋。反正，伺候谁又有什么要紧？”

---

===Original===
“There! I knew you would be sensible. And you’ll wait on him tomorrow?”

“If you like.”

“I do like. And Chris—don’t wear that dress! Indeed, green becomes you not.”

He smiled.

“I’ll go clad in sober black.”

===Chinese===
“这就成了！我早知道你是明白人。那么明天你就去拜他？”

“您愿意，便依您。”

“我正愿意。还有，克里斯——那身衣裳可别穿！说真的，绿色不衬你。”

他莞尔一笑。

“那我穿一身素净的黑去。”

---

===Original===
“No, nor that either. Wear that nice blue coat worked with cream.”

There was a slight pause.

“Yes,” said Christopher.

He did go, although against his inclination. When he arrived at Worth’s house he was ushered into the study, which was severely furnished and dark, and which looked out on to the backs of houses. Christopher shivered. A single ray of sunlight contrived to squeeze in at the window and showed a million specks of dust.

The door opened. A short, middle-aged man came into the room, Christopher’s card in his hand.

===Chinese===
“也不行，黑的也不成。穿那件好看的蓝外套，乳白绣色的那件。”

短短的静默。

“好，”克里斯托弗说。

他到底去了，虽然心里老大不情愿。到了沃思府上，他被引进书房：陈设刻板森然，光线晦暗，窗外望出去正对人家的后墙。克里斯托弗打了个寒噤。一缕日光千方百计从窗口挤进来，照出亿万点浮尘。

门开了。一个矮矮的中年人走进屋来，手里捏着克里斯托弗的名帖。

---

===Original===
“Mr. Dart?” The voice was fussy, slightly peevish.

Christopher bowed.

Sir Richard clasped his hand.

“Yours is an old name. I knew your father. A most noble gentleman.”

Again Christopher bowed.

===Chinese===
“达特先生？”那嗓音琐碎劳叨，微含愠意。

克里斯托弗一躬身。

理查德爵士双手握住他的手。

“府上是旧族。我认得令尊。极高尚的一位绅士。”

克里斯托弗又是一躬。

---

===Original===
“Yes. Well, will you be seated? Oh, there are papers on the chair! Allow me!” He cleared the documents on to the table. Christopher thanked him.

Worth sat down at the writing-table and rested his arms on it.

“Lady Frances Montgomery advised me of your coming today. A charming lady! Charming!”

Christopher suppressed a smile. Evidently Fanny had exerted herself to captivate Sir Richard.

Worth came back to earth.

===Chinese===
“好。嗯，请坐可好？哦，椅子上搁着文件！容我挪开！”他把文件拢到了桌上。克里斯托弗道了谢。

沃思在写字桌前坐下，两臂支在桌上。

“弗朗西丝·蒙哥马利夫人预先见告，说您今日来访。一位迷人的夫人！迷人极了！”

克里斯托弗忍住了笑。芬妮显是花过一番心思，去迷住这位理查德爵士的。

沃思这才回过神来。

---

===Original===
“Charming, yes. I understand you have been secretary to my Lord Roxhythe?”

“I have had that honour, Sir Richard, for nine years.”

“Well, well! May I ask why you left him? Do not think me impertinent! But it is just as well to know everything, is it not?”

This was almost amusing. Worth was indeed a contrast to Roxhythe. Christopher found himself thinking of another interview that had taken place at eleven at night in rooms overlooking the river. How typical of my lord that was!

“Er—certainly, sir. I left because I wanted a change. He will speak for me, I know.”

===Chinese===
“迷人，不错。我得知你做过罗克斯海斯勋爵大人的秘书？”

“这份荣幸是我的，理查德爵士——整整九年。”

“哦，哦！可否问一声，你为何离开了他？别当我多事！不过凡事打听个明白总是好的，是不是？”

这几乎有些可笑。沃思同罗克斯海斯确确实实是两个极端。克里斯托弗不由得想起另一次会面：夜里十一点钟，在一处俯临河水的屋子里。那才真是爵爷的作风！

“呃——当然，先生。我离开是因为想换一换。他会替我说话的，我知道。”

---

===Original===
“Ah, yes, yes, of course! That is excellent. You understand that this is rather different work from what you have been accustomed to?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Don’t think that I mean to infer that you are not capable of undertaking it! But I think it would be a great change. Is that not so?”

“Yes, sir. I have done little save manage the affairs of my lord’s estate.”

“Just as I thought. Exactly. No matter. Of course I need hardly say this to James Dart his son, but great discretion would be required of you an you worked for me. Here we handle State affairs which must not be talked of.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是，是，自然！那再好不过。你可明白，这份差事同你素来做惯的颇不相同？”

“是，先生。”

“别当我是在暗示你胜任不了！只是我想，这于你是个大变动。是不是？”

“是，先生。除了替爵爷经管田庄事务，我没做过什么。”

“果然如我所料。正是。不打紧。自然，这话对詹姆斯·达特（James Dart）之子几乎不消说得，可你若在我手下当差，便少不得要万分谨慎。我们这里经手的是国家大事，绝不可与外人道。”

---

===Original===
“I understand that, sir.”

“Ah, yes, yes, naturally. I am sure that you would prove discreet. You said, I think, that Lord Roxhythe would speak for you?”

“I did.”

“Yes. You’ll forgive me, Mr. Dart, but I marvel that you stayed so long in his service.”

Christopher stiffened.

===Chinese===
“这个我明白，先生。”

“啊，是，是，自然。我相信你会证明自己是谨慎的。方才你好像是说，罗克斯海斯勋爵肯替你说话？”

“我是这么说来着。”

“好。恕我直言，达特先生，你在他手下竟待了这么久，我不免纳罕。”

克里斯托弗身子一僵。

---

===Original===
“Indeed, sir?”

“I had thought that James Dart his son would not have been in the company of such as Roxhythe.”

“Sir, I think it as well to tell you that Lord Roxhythe commands my highest regards.”

“Dear, dear! Of course he has great fascination. I have heard of it. A powerful man.”

“Very,” said Christopher.

===Chinese===
“是么，先生？”

“我一向以为，詹姆斯·达特之子当不致同罗克斯海斯这等人厮混。”

“先生，我认为还是直言相告的好：罗克斯海斯勋爵受我最高的敬重。”

“啧，啧！他自有极大的魔力，我有所耳闻。一位有权势的人物。”

“极有权势，”克里斯托弗道。

---

===Original===
“Forgive me again, but do you realize that the atmosphere of my house is very different from Lord Roxhythe’s?”

Christopher glanced round the untidy room.

“Yes,” he said. The faintest of smiles flickered across his mouth.

“I live very quietly. I fear I am no brilliant courtier. I am but a patriot. I do trust you are not imbued with Lord Roxhythe his views.”

“I regret, sir, I cannot tell you what are his views.”

===Chinese===
“再次恕罪，可你可想见，寒舍的风气同罗克斯海斯勋爵府大不相同？”

克里斯托弗环顾了一下这间邋遢的屋子。

“想见，”他说。一丝极淡的笑影在他嘴边闪了一下。

“我过得十分清静。我怕自己算不得风光的廷臣。我不过是个爱国者。我切实指望你没有沾上罗克斯海斯勋爵那一套见解。”

“抱歉，先生，他的见解如何，我无可奉告。”

---

===Original===
“That is very well, very well. And so you desire to fill the post of secretary to me?”

Christopher sighed.

“That is my desire, sir.”

“Yes. Well, Mr. Dart, I will not disguise the fact that good—above all discreet—secretaries are not easily come by these days. Your name stands greatly in your favour. And of course Sir Jasper Montgomery’s recommendation is sufficient. With your permission I will write to Lord Roxhythe. And then, if you are agreeable, I should suggest a week’s trial.”

“Very well, Sir Richard. I shall try to satisfy you.”

===Chinese===
“很好，很好。那么说，你是想充任我的秘书了？”

克里斯托弗叹了口气。

“我正是此意，先生。”

“好。嗯，达特先生，我不瞒你说：如今得力的——尤其是谨慎的——秘书，可不是轻易寻得着的。你的家名于你大有裨益。贾斯珀·蒙哥马利爵士的荐举自然一言足矣。得你允准，我便致函罗克斯海斯勋爵。而后，你若同意，我提议先试用一星期。”

“好的，理查德爵士。我当尽力使您满意。”

---

===Original===
“Of course, of course! Let me see—have you not an elder brother?”

“Roderick, sir. He is with the Prince of Orange.”

“Is that so? Very interesting to be sure. Though we cannot afford to lose good patriots in these times.”

Christopher rose.

“Roderick has been with the Prince for many years, sir. He is very devoted.”

===Chinese===
“自然，自然！我想想看——你有一位兄长，不是么？”

“罗德里克，先生。他在奥兰治亲王麾下效力。”

“是么？着实有趣。只是当今时世，好的爱国者我们损失不起啊。”

克里斯托弗立起身来。

“罗德里克跟随亲王已多年了，先生。他极为忠诚。”

---

===Original===
“Ah yes, naturally. A remarkable young man, is he not? Remarkable.” He ushered his visitor out.

Christopher walked slowly down Bishopsgate Street. Suddenly he laughed mirthlessly, and his hand clenched on his glove. What a fool he was not to return to Roxhythe! Why should he enter the service of this uncongenial man? Why should he not go to his master and beg to be allowed to come back? But he knew that he would never do that. A fool he might be, but he knew that he was acting rightly. He thought how Roxhythe would have enjoyed the interview with Worth, and laughed again. There swept over him an overwhelming longing to see that tall, graceful figure again, to hear the lazy voice, to feel the pressure of those tapering fingers. He walked on, biting his lip.

Two days later came a letter from Roxhythe. Christopher’s hands trembled as he broke the seal.

===Chinese===
“啊，是，自然。一位出众的青年，是不是？出众。”他把客人送出门去。

克里斯托弗沿着主教门大街（Bishopsgate Street）缓缓地走。忽然他惨然一笑，攥着手套的手扣紧了。不回罗克斯海斯那里去，他是多傻！何苦投入这么一个气味不投的人手下当差？何苦不去他的主人跟前，求他容自己回去？可他知道，他断断不会那样做。傻瓜他或者算得上，但他知道自己行事不错。他想到罗克斯海斯若看见这场同沃思的会晤该多么受用，又笑了。一股铺天盖地的渴慕涌遍全身——再望一望那颀长优美的身影，再听一听那懒洋洋的嗓音，再感觉一回那几根纤细指尖的按握。他咬着嘴唇走下去了。

两天之后，罗克斯海斯来了一封信。克里斯托弗拆火漆封缄时，两手发着颤。

---

===Original===
> “**My Deare Chris.**—Who in God’s Name is Worth? Some Psalm-singing Puritan, I’ll be bound. Eschew his Company. I spoke of you Very Highly, though I was minded to Malign you when I saw who your Future Master was to be.
> “My secretary is a Fool. I implore you to take Pity on me. Or if Ye will not, at least Visitt me Some Day.—**Roxhythe**.”

===Chinese===
> “**我最亲爱的克里斯**——看上帝的份上，这个沃思究竟是哪一号人物？保管又是个唱圣诗的清教徒。切莫与他往来。论到你，我原是把你揄扬得至高至重的，只是待一见你未来的东家是何等样人，倒存心要糟蹋你几句了。
> “我的秘书是个蠢材。我求你垂怜于我。你若执意不肯，至少拣个日子来瞧瞧我。——**罗克斯海斯**”

---

===Original===
Christopher folded the missive tenderly and slipped it into his pocket. Every nerve urged him to go to Roxhythe who wanted him, but his will held him back. Once in my lord’s presence the spell would be cast over him again, and all the old agony would return.

He answered the letter at length, and told my lord that as yet he could not face an interview. He assured Roxhythe of his undying affection. It was a pathetic, wistful letter that tried hard to be cheerful.

My lord read it and laid it aside.

“A pity,” he reflected. “He was so much more restful than this dolt.”
===Chinese===
克里斯托弗温柔地把信笺折起，收进口袋。他周身的神经都在催他奔到要他的罗克斯海斯跟前去，可他的意志拉住了他。只消一立在爵爷面前，那道魔咒便会重新罩下，旧日的百般苦痛也将卷土重来。

他回了一封长长的信，告诉爵爷，他眼下还不敢面对一场会面。他向罗克斯海斯保证自己的爱慕至死不渝。那是一封凄惶惆怅的信，却竭力装出欢容来。

爵爷读罢，把信搁在了一旁。

“可惜，”他忖道。“比起这个蠢材，他可叫人省心多了。”

## II The Husband / 丈夫

===Original===
Lady Crewe was disconsolate. Out of the corner of her eye she watched my Lord Roxhythe paying his respects to Mlle. Charlotte d’Almond. Charlotte was of the Duchess of Portsmouth’s household, something of a virago, but undoubtedly fascinating. Lady Crewe hated her cordially. Lady Crewe sat alone, playing with her fan. Presently Mr. Dart appeared.

“All alone, Lady Crewe?”

She forced a smile.

“No, Mr. Dart; you are here.”

Christopher was fond of Millicent. He sat down beside her.

===Chinese===
克鲁夫人郁郁不乐。她用眼角的余光瞟着罗克斯海斯勋爵正向夏洛特·达勒蒙（Charlotte d’Almond）小姐殷勤致意。夏洛特是朴茨茅斯公爵夫人府上的人，颇有几分泼辣悍妇的做派，妩媚却是不容置疑。克鲁夫人打心底里恨她。克鲁夫人独坐在一旁，手里把玩着扇子。不多时，达特先生到了。

“就您一个人么，克鲁夫人？”

她强作笑颜。

“不，达特先生；您这不是来了。”

克里斯托弗很喜欢米莉森特。他在她身旁坐了下来。

---

===Original===
“Shall we stay on this very pleasing couch, or shall we dance?” he asked.

“I—I don’t think I will dance, thank you,” she answered. She was young, and she did not conceal her emotions well.

Christopher glanced round the room.

“All the world is here tonight,” he remarked. “What a gathering! I don’t see Sir Henry?”

“He is here,” she said listlessly. “Gaming belike.”

A year ago Sir Henry Crewe was never from his wife’s side. Christopher regarded Roxhythe across the room with tightened lips. He attempted another remark.

===Chinese===
“咱们是守着这张怪讨人喜欢的软榻呢，还是去跳舞？”他问。

“我——我不想跳了，多谢，”她答道。她还年轻，不大藏得住心事。

克里斯托弗朝屋里环视一周。

“今晚满城的人都到了，”他品评道。“好一场盛会！怎么没瞧见亨利爵士（Sir Henry）？”

“他在的，”她恹恹地说。“多半在赌钱。”

一年之前，亨利·克鲁爵士（Sir Henry Crewe）从不曾离开他妻子半步。克里斯托弗抿紧嘴唇，隔着屋子望向罗克斯海斯。他试着另起话头。

---

===Original===
“It is quite an age since we last saw each other, Lady Crewe. I looked for you at the Coventry rout last week but someone said you were in the country. Was that so?”

“No,” she answered. “I was not well. I do not think town air agrees with me. I tire so easily.”

Time was, reflected Christopher, when this had not been so. Her ladyship’s cheeks had been rosy then, and less thin.

“Why, I am sorry!” he said. “You must make your husband take you to the country for a while, though I vow we should miss you sadly.”

Lady Crewe was not attending. A lazy, cynical voice reached Christopher’s ears. He turned sharply. Lord Roxhythe stood beside them.

===Chinese===
“咱们好些日子没见了，克鲁夫人。上星期考文垂（Coventry）家的夜会上我还寻过您，可有人说您下乡去了。可是真的？”

“不是，”她答道。“我身子不适。我怕是过不惯城里的空气，动不动就乏得慌。”

克里斯托弗暗自思量，从前可不是这般光景。那时夫人的面颊还红扑扑的，人也不这样清瘦。

“哎呀，真叫人难过！”他说。“您得叫您丈夫带您下乡住上一阵才是，虽说我敢起誓，我们会想您想得厉害。”

克鲁夫人压根儿没在听。一个慵懒而讥诮的声音飘进克里斯托弗耳中。他霍然转身。罗克斯海斯勋爵已立在两人身旁。

---

===Original===
“My very dear Millicent! I had not seen you till this moment. Pray where have you been?” He kissed her hand. Christopher observed how the colour flooded her face.

“You have been otherwise engaged, my lord,” she replied. “I have been here some while.”

Christopher saw that he was not wanted. He faded away. Roxhythe took his seat.

“Child,” he said, “where are all your roses?”

===Chinese===
“我顶亲爱的米莉森特！直到此刻我才瞧见你。敢问你一直躲在哪里？”他吻了吻她的手。克里斯托弗看得分明：红潮霎时涌上了她的脸。

“您另有所约呢，大人，”她答道。“我在这儿坐了半晌了。”

克里斯托弗看明自己在此已是多余，便悄然隐去了。罗克斯海斯坐了下来。

“孩子，”他说，“你的血色都哪里去了？”

---

===Original===
“Am I so pale?” she smiled. “Perhaps I have lost my rouge.”

“Evidently,” he said. “And what ails you?”

Her eyes were troubled.

“My lord … my lord. …”

“But why so aloof?”

The coaxing tone brought the tears to her lashes.

===Chinese===
“我竟这样苍白么？”她浅浅一笑。“兴许是胭脂掉了吧。”

“显然是，”他说。“那你究竟是怎么了？”

她的眼里盛着愁绪。

“大人……大人……”

“可何必这样见外？”

这哄劝的声调叫她睫毛上登时挂了泪。

---

===Original===
“David—I am very unhappy.”

He rose.

“My dear, we must examine this more closely. I know a room where we shall not be disturbed.”

“Oh, no!” she cried. “Indeed, I must not!”

“Must not?”

“You—you know it is not seemly for me to be seen so much—with you. My—my husband—”

===Chinese===
“大卫——我很不快活。”

他站起身来。

“亲爱的，这事儿咱们得细细剖究。我知道有间屋子，不会有人来搅扰。”

“哦，不行！”她嚷道。“我真的不能去！”

“不能？”

“你——你知道，总同你叫人瞧见——不成体统。我——我丈夫——”

---

===Original===
“Fiend seize your husband. Come!”

“I ought not—I ought not—” Even as she said it she rose and laid her hand on his arm. Together they went out.

Roxhythe led her into a small, dimly lighted parlour. He shut the door, and took her in his arms.

“What is it, sweetheart?”

For a moment she tried to free herself; then her hands clung to his broad shoulders.

===Chinese===
“让魔鬼抓了你丈夫去。走！”

“我不该——我不该——”话犹如此，她已起了身，把手搭上他的手臂。两人一道出去了。

罗克斯海斯领她走进一间灯光昏昏的小客厅。他掩上门，把她搂进怀里。

“怎么了，甜心儿？”

一霎时她还想挣脱；随即双手便攀住了他宽阔的肩膀。

---

===Original===
“David, it is wrong! I—I am not this kind of woman! God help me, I wish I had never met you!” The cry was broken.

Roxhythe bent his head till his lips met hers. It was Mrs. Diana Shelton who had called Roxhythe’s kiss “divine intoxication.”

“Confess! ’Tis a lie?”

“No, no! Indeed, I wish it!”

He kissed her again.

“You do not love me?”

===Chinese===
“大卫，这是罪过！我——我不是这种女人！上帝救救我，但愿我从没遇见过你！”哭喊声碎成了几截。

罗克斯海斯低下头去，直到唇与唇相接。罗克斯海斯的吻，曾被黛安娜·谢尔顿夫人（Diana Shelton）唤作“神圣的迷醉”。

“招认！方才那话是假的吧？”

“不，不！我真的但愿如此！”

他又吻了她。

“你并不爱我？”

---

===Original===
“Oh, yes!—No! oh, what am I saying?” She broke away from him to a chair. “Before I—met you—before you—made love to me—I thought I cared so much for Henry. Now—now we hardly speak. You fill all my thoughts, and he looks at me—as though he hated me. I’m no court beauty. I cannot—play at love as they do. ’Tis—not in my nature.”

My lord knelt at her side, holding both her hands.

“Do you then care so much for Henry? Am I nothing?”

“Have I not told you? Oh, my heart is nigh breaking! You do not really love me; you only—pretend—and it means so much to me. I’m a fool; a silly, hysterical miss! I—” She tried to laugh, but her voice broke, and she buried her face on his shoulder, sobbing.

Roxhythe stared over her head at the wall. His expression was rather curious. Suddenly he bent over the bowed figure, clinging so desperately to his hands.

===Chinese===
“哦，爱！——不！哦，我这是在说什么呀？”她挣开他，一径逃到一张椅子旁。“在我——遇见你——在你——向我求爱——之前，我一直自以为深深爱着亨利。如今——如今我们几乎没有话讲。你占满了我全部的心思，而他看我的眼神——倒像恨我似的。我不是宫廷里那种美人儿。我学不会——像她们那样把爱情当戏来做。那——不是我的天性。”

爵爷在她身旁跪下，握住她的双手。

“这么说，你就这样爱着亨利？我便什么都不是么？”

“我不是同你说过了么？哦，我的心都要碎了！你并不是真心爱我；你不过——佯装——可这对我何等要紧。我是个傻瓜；一个痴傻癫狂的小丫头！我——”她想笑一笑，嗓音却哽住了，把脸埋在他肩头，啜泣起来。

罗克斯海斯的目光越过她的头顶，落在墙上。他的神情颇为古怪。忽然，他朝那副死死攥着他双手、深深俯伏的身影俯下身去。

---

===Original===
“My child, you distress yourself unduly. How old are you?”

“T-twenty-one. Why—why do you ask?”

My lord smiled whimsically.

“Twenty-one. And I am—forty-two.”

She lifted her head.

===Chinese===
“我的孩子，你这番苦楚是白白加给自己的。你今年多大？”

“二——二十一。你——你问这个做什么？”

爵爷唇边浮起一丝古怪的笑意。

“二十一。而我呢——四十二。”

她抬起头来。

---

===Original===
“What of it?”

“I seem to be rather too old for you, dear.”

“David—my lord—I do not—understand.”

“No? I think our little comedy has played itself out.”

Slowly she drew herself away from him.

===Chinese===
“那又怎样？”

“照我看，我这把年纪配你，嫌大了些，亲爱的。”

“大卫——大人——我不——明白。”

“是么？依我看，咱们这出小喜剧已经自己演到了终场。”

她慢慢地从他身边挣脱开来。

---

===Original===
“You—call it comedy. I—have another name for it. Mayhap ’twas indeed a—comedy to you. To me—to me—” she stopped, twisting her fingers.

“Oh, no!” said my lord, calmly. “You delude yourself, my dear. It was a pretty farce, and perhaps you were a little dazzled. But that is all.”

“You—make me—hate you.”

“Why, that is as it should be.”

“You—you made love to me; you—dazzled—me, and now you are tired of the—farce—you cast me off.”

===Chinese===
“你——管它叫喜剧。我——另有叫法。兴许在你，它当真是一出——喜剧。在我——在我——”她住了口，绞着手指。

“哦，不，”爵爷平静地说。“你这是哄骗自己，亲爱的。那是一出顶好看的闹剧，兴许你还被晃花了那么一点儿眼。仅此而已。”

“你——逼得我——恨你。”

“嗯，本该如此。”

“你——你向我求爱；你——晃花了——我的眼，如今你腻味了这出——闹剧——便把我一脚踢开。”

---

===Original===
“Not a whit. I am not tired of it. I think you are.”

She shook her head. Slow tears were creeping down her cheeks.

“I love you. I cannot let you go.”

“Well, my dear, I do not see how you are to keep the both of us on a tether if you take the matter so seriously.”

“I do not want both.”

===Chinese===
“一点儿也不。我没有腻味。要我说，腻味的正是你。”

她摇头。泪水缓缓爬下双颊。

“我爱你。我不能放你走。”

“好啦，亲爱的，你这般当真，我可看不出，你怎么能把咱们两个一总拴在同一根绳上。”

“我不要两个。”

---

===Original===
“Then choose your husband, my child.”

“I can’t, I can’t! I want you!” It was the cry of a child. Roxhythe bit his lip.

“It will pass.”

She raised her head.

“Are you saying—these things—for my sake, or is it—because of—Charlotte d’Almond?”

===Chinese===
“那就拣你丈夫，我的孩子。”

“我不能，我不能！我要你！”这是一声孩子的哭喊。罗克斯海斯咬住了嘴唇。

“会过去的。”

她抬起头。

“你说这些——是为着我好呢，还是——为了——夏洛特·达勒蒙？”

---

===Original===
“Oh lud!” said my lord. He rose to his feet. “Preserve me!”

She also rose.

“It is not? You love me, as you’ve so often vowed?”

Roxhythe looked at her serenely.

“My dear, I do not think I love anyone.”

Tragedy was in her blue eyes, and uncomprehending hurt.

===Chinese===
“我的老天！”爵爷说着站起身来。“饶了我吧！”

她也站了起来。

“不是？你爱我，就像你一再赌咒起誓的那样？”

罗克斯海斯安然自若地望着她。

“亲爱的，我想我谁也不爱。”

她的蓝眼睛里盛着悲剧，还有一份懵然不解的伤痛。

---

===Original===
“You thought me—just a—cheap woman!”

“No.”

“Then—then—Oh heavens, how dare you humiliate me so? And I—and I—please take me back to the ballroom!”

She stepped forward into the full light of the candles, erect, outraged. Roxhythe eyed her critically.

“Child, you must dry the tears.”

===Chinese===
“你一直当我——不过是个——下贱女人！”

“不是。”

“那——那么——哦，天哪，你怎么敢这样羞辱我？而我——而我——请把我带回舞厅去！”

她跨前一步，走进烛光通明处，身姿笔挺，怒不可遏。罗克斯海斯带着挑剔的眼光打量她。

“孩子，眼泪得擦干。”

---

===Original===
In spite of her forced calm something sparkled on the end of her long lashes.

“Oh, tut, tut, Millicent! You will forget all this madness. Come, let me wipe away the tears.”

Millicent pushed him from her with hands that trembled.

“No! Please—don’t try to—be kind to me! I cannot bear it. I have been in heaven and hell this past year, and now—and now—” She choked back a sob. “You were—very cruel, my lord. You made me play at love with you, and then—when I am no longer playing—you turn away, and—call it—a pretty comedy. And you talk to me—as if you were—my father!”

“Which I almost might be,” remarked his lordship. “My dear, you are too young for the game. I ought to have known it. I am sorry. Now won’t you let me dry your tears?”

===Chinese===
任她怎样强自镇定，长睫毛的梢上仍有泪光闪动。

“呸，呸，米莉森特！这一切痴狂你都会忘掉的。来，让我替你把泪擦了。”

米莉森特用颤抖的双手推开他。

“不！求你——别再——对我发善心！我受不了。这一年我上过天堂也下过地狱，如今——如今——”她硬生生咽下一声抽泣。“你——好狠心，大人。你叫我陪你把爱情当戏做，到头来——我假戏真做了——你却扭头就走，还——管它叫——一出好看的喜剧。你还这样同我说话——倒好像你是——我父亲！”

“论岁数，倒也差不离是了，”爵爷说。“亲爱的，这场游戏里你太年轻了些。我早该明白的。我很抱歉。这会儿还不许我替你擦擦眼泪么？”

---

===Original===
His voice was very gentle; all his fascination was to the fore. It swept over Millicent and would not be gainsaid. Pride was as nothing before it; at that moment she felt that only one thing mattered, and that was that he should not leave her. She allowed him to draw her closer, and to wipe her eyes with his scented handkerchief. A small pulse in her throat was throbbing madly; he was so inexpressibly dear, so strong, so wonderful. The tears welled up afresh; she heard him speak through a haze of misery.

“Dear child, I am not worth it. I am only an interlude.”

“That is all—to you. Oh, you are utterly, utterly ruthless! I amused you for the time, so—you have—broken my heart—for your pleasure, and brought me—as low as this! I was so happy before you came! So happy.”

“You will be happy again,” said Roxhythe philosophically. “Hearts are easily mended. Tell that husband of yours to take you away for a time.”

===Chinese===
他的声音十分温柔；浑身的魅力尽数使了出来，席卷了米莉森特，不容人抗拒。在它面前，骄傲一钱不值；那一刻她觉得天底下只余一件事要紧，便是他不能走。她由着他把自己拉近，由着他用那只薰香的手帕拭去泪痕。她喉间一处细小的脉息狂乱地跳动；他是那样难以言说的可亲，那样强壮，那样奇妙。泪水重新涌上来；隔着一片愁雾，她听见他说话。

“乖孩子，我不值得。我不过是一段插曲。”

“那只是——对你是。哦，你冷酷透顶，冷酷透顶！我一时解了你的闷，于是——你便为了自己的快活——碾碎了我的心，把我作践——到这般田地！你来之前我是多么快活！多么快活。”

“你还会快活起来的，”罗克斯海斯达观地说。“心是容易补缀的。叫你那位丈夫带你出去避一阵吧。”

---

===Original===
“My husband! We scarcely speak! He despises me! He thinks me—what I am—a cheap, faithless woman!”

“It seems your husband is a fool. There! The tears are gone?”

“Take me back to the ballroom, please. I—I have been mad. What will—Henry think—if he finds me gone? Oh, please take me back.”

Roxhythe smiled faintly.

===Chinese===
“我丈夫！我们几乎没有话！他看不起我！他当我——我就是那号人——一个下贱的、负心的女人！”

“敢情你丈夫是个傻瓜。好啦！眼泪擦净了？”

“请带我回舞厅吧。我——我是疯了。要是亨利——发现我不见了——该怎么想？哦，求你带我回去。”

罗克斯海斯淡淡一笑。

---

===Original===
“Yes. I did not think the passion was real. Console yourself, my dear. ’Tis Henry you love.” He held out his arm.

The door opened.

“Just as I thought!” The words came furiously, hissed across the room. With his back to the door, hands clenched at his sides, stood Sir Henry Crewe.

Millicent sprang away from Roxhythe’s side, her cheeks flaming. Roxhythe himself regarded the intruder pensively.

===Chinese===
“是啊。我原就没当那番痴情是真的。宽宽心吧，亲爱的。你爱的是亨利。”他伸出手臂。

门开了。

“果然不出我所料！”这声怒喝自屋子那头嘶嘶而来。亨利·克鲁爵士背抵着门立在那里，双手在身侧攥成了拳。

米莉森特从罗克斯海斯身旁猛地跳开，双颊烧得通红。罗克斯海斯本人则不慌不忙地打量着这不速之客。

---

===Original===
“Blue and rose-pink. …” he murmured. “Marvellous!”

Crewe walked forward, his dark velvet cloak hushing against the table as he brushed past.

“I have not sought you out to talk of my clothes, Lord Roxhythe!” he said. He did not glance in his wife’s direction.

“No?” answered Roxhythe. He met the angry young eyes amusedly. “What then?”

Crewe controlled his voice with difficulty. He was very pale, but his eyes burnt.

===Chinese===
“蓝的，又配玫瑰粉……”他喃喃道。“妙极了！”

克鲁走上前来，深色的天鹅绒斗篷擦过桌沿，发出窸窣的轻响。

“我不是来同你谈我衣服的，罗克斯海斯爵爷！”他说。他一眼也不朝妻子那边望。

“哦？”罗克斯海斯应道。他饶有兴致地迎着那双年轻的、烧着怒火的眼睛。“那为什么？”

克鲁艰难地按捺着嗓音。他脸色惨白，两眼却烧着火。

---

===Original===
“I have come to tell you that my friends will wait on yours, Lord Roxhythe!”

“Thank you very much,” said Roxhythe. “But may I point out to you that this is a somewhat inopportune moment?”

“I think not! I could scarce have chosen a more fitting time!” He laughed bitterly. “I trust I make myself clear?”

“Not at all,” said Roxhythe. “I am at a loss.”

“You are singularly dense if you do not understand me! Things have come to a pretty pass that you so brazenly take my wife apart! Is that explanation enough?”

===Chinese===
“我是来告诉你，我的朋友们自会去拜会你的朋友们，罗克斯海斯爵爷！”

“多谢美意，”罗克斯海斯说。“不过容我提醒一句，此刻行事，时机未免欠佳？”

“我看再合适不过！更合宜的时辰再挑不出来了！”他惨然一笑。“想必我把话说得够明白了？”

“全然没有，”罗克斯海斯说。“我一头雾水。”

“你若还听不懂，那可就是迟钝得少有！你竟敢厚着脸皮把我妻子带到一旁，可见事情烂到了什么地步！这般解释可够明白了？”

---

===Original===
Roxhythe stared at him in great hauteur. Then he turned to Millicent and bowed.

“Permit me to conduct you back to the ballroom, my dear.”

Crewe flung himself between them.

“Lady Crewe can stay to hear what I have to say! She will not again require your escort!”

My lord’s voice became a shade more languid.

===Chinese===
罗克斯海斯十足倨傲地瞪住他。随后他转向米莉森特，微微一躬身。

“容我送你回舞厅，亲爱的。”

克鲁猛地插到两人中间。

“克鲁夫人可以留下听我把话说完！她再不用你的护送了！”

爵爷的嗓音又慵懒了一分。

---

===Original===
“My good youth, you rave. You have my permission to stand back.”

Few had ever dared to withstand that note. Sir Henry stood firm.

“’Tis you who shall stand back, sir! You shall not touch my wife!”

Millicent clasped and unclasped her hands. She was very near to breaking point.

“You make a very fine melodramatic hero,” said Roxhythe. “But you forget with whom you have to deal.”

===Chinese===
“我的好小伙子，你这是说呓语。我准你退到一边去。”

敢顶撞这种腔调的人向来寥寥无几。亨利爵士纹丝不动。

“该退下的是你，先生！你不许碰我的妻子！”

米莉森特双手攥紧又松开。她已临近崩溃的边缘。

“你这出情节剧的悲情英雄扮得真不坏，”罗克斯海斯说。“只是你忘了自己是在同什么人打交道。”

---

===Original===
“You might be the devil himself and I’d not let you pass!”

“Child’s talk,” said my lord. His hand descended on Crewe’s shoulder and gripped hard. He gave a sudden twist, and Crewe fell back with a smothered exclamation. Roxhythe took Millicent’s cold hand in his.

“I’ll return to you,” he informed the furious young man. “Open the door.”

“Perhaps it is as well that Lady Crewe should withdraw,” sneered Sir Henry. He flung the door wide.

Roxhythe did not answer him. He led Millicent, tearless now, a creature of ice, to the deserted hall.

===Chinese===
“你就是魔王本人，我也不会放你过去！”

“小孩子的话，”爵爷说。他的手落在克鲁肩上，狠狠一攥，猛地一拧，克鲁一声闷哼，踉跄退开。罗克斯海斯把米莉森特冰凉的手拢进自己掌中。

“回头再来奉陪，”他知会那怒火中烧的年轻人。“开门。”

“克鲁夫人还是回避的好，”亨利爵士讥讽道。他把门豁然拉开。

罗克斯海斯没有理他。他领着米莉森特——此刻已不再流泪，一尊冰凝的人儿——走到空落落的门厅。

---

===Original===
“Will you wait here, child? I’ll send my cousin to you.”

Her lips moved.

“Oh—no! I cannot! I—”

“My dear, you are in no fit state to go back to the ballroom. Sit down.”

She sank down, unresisting. Roxhythe kissed her hand. “Let me reassure you, sweetheart; there will be no scandal. You can trust my cousin.” He strolled into the ballroom.

===Chinese===
“你在这里等一等，孩子？我这就打发表妹来陪你。”

她嘴唇动了动。

“哦——不！我不能！我——”

“亲爱的，你这副样子回不得舞厅。坐下。”

她没有推拒，颓然坐倒。罗克斯海斯吻了吻她的手。“让我安你的心，甜心儿；不会有什么风言风语。我表妹信得过。”他踱回了舞厅。

---

===Original===
Lady Frances was not dancing. When she saw Roxhythe she came quickly towards him.

“Where is Lady Crewe?”

“I want to take you to her. That young fool of a husband came plunging in upon us, and she is nigh fainting with fright.”

“Good God, Roxhythe! In my house! Could you not be decent for one evening? Where is the child?”

“In the hall. May I solicit your kindness for her? She should go home.”

===Chinese===
弗朗西丝夫人没有下场跳舞。一见罗克斯海斯，她便快步迎上前来。

“克鲁夫人在哪儿？”

“我正要带你去找她。她那个傻小子丈夫一头闯了进来，她险些吓晕过去。”

“上帝啊，罗克斯海斯！在我的府里！你就不能检点一个晚上么？孩子在哪儿？”

“在门厅。可否劳你的好心照拂她一二？她该回家了。”

---

===Original===
Lady Fanny swept out. Roxhythe, following more leisurely, saw her bend over the drooping figure in the chair. He half smiled, and went back to the little parlour.

Frances took the girl’s hands.

“My dear! Will you come upstairs with me?”

The great shamed eyes looked up.

“I—think—I had best—go home,” whispered Millicent.

===Chinese===
芬妮夫人飘然而出。罗克斯海斯跟在后头，步子从容些，望见她俯向椅中那个蔫蔫的身影。他若有所思地浅浅一笑，转身回了小客厅。

弗朗西丝握住姑娘的双手。

“亲爱的！随我上楼去好么？”

那双羞愧的大眼睛抬了起来。

“我——想——我最好——回家去，”米莉森特悄声道。

---

===Original===
Frances drew her to her feet.

“Presently, dear. Come with me now and tell me all about it.”

“Lady Frances—I am indeed sorry—to be the cause of a—disturbance in your house. I—”

“Nonsense! Come, we shall be private in my room.”

She bore the girl off to her boudoir, and put her into a chair.

===Chinese===
弗朗西丝把她拉了起来。

“待会儿再回，亲爱的。这会儿先随我来，把事情原原本本讲给我听。”

“弗朗西丝夫人——实在万分抱歉——在你府上——惹出这场——乱子。我——”

“傻话！来，到我房里去，没人搅扰。”

她把姑娘带进自己的闺房，安置在一张椅子里。

---

===Original===
“There! Poor little thing! Tell me what has happened.”

Millicent bowed her head.

“I’ve been so wicked—I suppose you know. And today—I let—Lord Roxhythe—take me to another room—and—and—my husband found us—and—oh, heavens, what must you think of me?”

“Why, that you are a silly child! No, no, don’t cry! There’s no harm done. My cousin will see to it that there is no scandal. But mercy on us, what induced you to play with Roxhythe, of all men?”

“I love him,” answered Millicent dully.

===Chinese===
“好了！可怜的小人儿！告诉我出了什么事。”

米莉森特垂下了头。

“我一向很不检点——想必你也知道。今天——我让——罗克斯海斯爵爷——把我带到另一间屋子——而——而我丈夫撞见了我们——哦，天哪，你该把我看成什么样的人？”

“嘿，只当你是个傻孩子！不，不，别哭！没出什么乱子。我表兄自会料理妥当，断不叫人嚼舌。可是老天爷，天下的男人你不挑，怎么偏偏去招惹罗克斯海斯？”

“我爱他，”米莉森特木然答道。

---

===Original===
Lady Frances opened her eyes to their widest.

“Love—my dear, foolish girl, you cannot.”

“I love him. And it’s all over—all over.”

“And a good thing too!” thought my lady. But she did not say that. She put her arms round Millicent.

“Won’t you tell me everything, dear?”

===Chinese===
弗朗西丝夫人把眼睛睁到了极大。

“爱——我亲爱的傻姑娘，你不会的。”

“我爱他。而一切都完了——全完了。”

“那样倒好！”夫人心中暗道。但这话她没有出口。她伸臂搂住米莉森特。

“不肯把什么都告诉我么，亲爱的？”

---

===Original===
The girl flushed.

“You are very, very kind, Lady Frances, but—oh, I expect you know all there is to know about me!”

“My child, I have seen Roxhythe often at your side, and I confess I have wondered what you were at—playing with fire.”

“I was not playing! Oh, at first, three years ago, yes. No one minded; my husband thought nothing of it. But lately—I have been so—unhappy, and when he was with me—so very happy! And he meant nothing; he did not love me. It was a—game. I suppose any other woman would have known, but I—I—oh, I think my heart will break!”

“I am quite sure it will not,” replied Lady Frances. “’Tis all midsummer madness. How could you think Roxhythe was in earnest? Was there no one to warn you?”

===Chinese===
姑娘红了脸。

“您真是，真是太好了，弗朗西丝夫人，可是——哦，我想，关于我的种种，您也都听说了！”

“我的孩子，我常见罗克斯海斯伴在你身边，不瞒你说，我一直纳闷你在闹什么——那是在玩火。”

“我没有玩火！哦，起初，三年前，算是玩。没人在意；我丈夫也不放在心上。可近来——我一直那么——不幸，而有他在身边——又那么快活！他并没存什么心思；他不爱我。那只是一场——游戏。换了旁的女人怕是早就看穿了，可我——我——哦，我的心要碎了！”

“我敢肯定碎不了，”弗朗西丝夫人答道。“不过是一场仲夏的痴狂。你怎么会当罗克斯海斯是真心的？就没有人提点过你？”

---

===Original===
“No. There is only Henry—and now he—hates me. What shall I do?”

“Start afresh,” said Fanny briskly. “Roxhythe is not worth one teardrop. You must forget him, and play no more with fire.”

“Forget! Ah, my lady, it is easy to speak so. I love him! I love him so much that were he to lift one finger I would go with him—anywhere!”

Lady Frances nodded over the bowed head.

“Well, my dear, he’ll lift no finger. He lives for himself alone. This is not his first affaire.”

===Chinese===
“没有。我只有亨利——如今他也——恨我了。我该怎么办？”

“从头来过，”芬妮干脆利落地说。“罗克斯海斯不值一滴眼泪。你必须忘掉他，再不许碰火。”

“忘掉！唉，夫人，说来容易。我爱他！我爱他爱到这般地步，只消他动一动小指头，我就跟他走——走到天边！”

弗朗西丝夫人俯身对着那颗低垂的头，点了点。

“好啦，亲爱的，他的指头是不会动的。他只为他自己一个人活着。这样的风流账，你也并不是头一笔。”

---

===Original===
Millicent shuddered.

“I thought he really cared for me. I knew there were—other women—but—”

Lady Frances proceeded to be cruel for kindness’ sake.

“I have known Roxhythe for—I won’t say how many years—and I know how much heart he hath. That is none. He has fascinated you until you think that you love him. But you do not. Ah, no, my dear, you do not!”

Millicent was silent. After a moment Fanny patted her shoulder.

===Chinese===
米莉森特打了个寒噤。

“我当他当真是待我好的。我知道还有——旁的女人——可是——”

弗朗西丝夫人跟着出诸好意，狠下心肠来。

“我认识罗克斯海斯——多少年就不说啦——我知道他有心肠多少。分毫皆无。他把你迷住了，迷到你自以为爱他。其实不然。唉，不，亲爱的，你并不爱他！”

米莉森特默然。过了一会儿，芬妮轻拍她的肩。

---

===Original===
“Come! Cheer up! Oh, I know ’tis hard, but you must bear a brave front. Never let him see that he has hurt you.”

“You do not know, Lady Frances.”

Fanny laughed irrepressibly.

“Why, do you think I have not been in love scores of times with those whom I should not have loved? Child, I have experienced all your feelings, and I assure you that you will recover.”

“I wish that I were dead!”

===Chinese===
“来！打起精神！哦，我知道这难过，可你必须撑起一副勇敢的门面。千万别叫他看出他伤着了你。”

“您不知道的，弗朗西丝夫人。”

芬妮忍不住笑出了声。

“嗳，你当我没为那些不该爱的人几十回地坠入情网么？孩子，你这般滋味我都尝遍，我向你担保，你会缓过来的。”

“我恨不得死了干净！”

---

===Original===
“Nonsense! You are overwrought tonight; tomorrow you will think differently. I am going to send you home now, and—if I may—I will come and see you in a few days’ time.”

“You—you will not care to. There will be some dreadful scandal—oh, I wish that I had never come to town!”

“There’ll be no vestige of scandal, my dear. Trust Roxhythe to see to that.”

“Oh, yes, yes! They are going to fight, and one of them will be killed—all for me who am—worthless!”

===Chinese===
“傻话！你今夜只是心绪过激；明日想法子自然两样。我这就打发人送你回家，还有——容我不揣冒昧——过几天我去瞧你。”

“您——您才不会乐意呢。会闹出天大的风波的——哦，我巴不得从没进过城！”

“一丝风影也不会有，亲爱的。这事交托给罗克斯海斯，万无一失。”

“哦，是了，是了！他们要决斗了，总有一个要送命——全为了我这个——一无是处的人！”

---

===Original===
“I’ll wager my best necklet no one is killed,” said Lady Frances.

“Henry is so angry! I have never seen him look so terrible! He—he will do my lord some injury.”

“Alas! There’s no likelihood of such a thing happening!” said Fanny, tartly.

===Chinese===
“我拿我最心爱的项链打赌，死不了人，”弗朗西丝夫人说。

“亨利气坏了！我从没见他神色那样吓人！他——他会伤着爵爷的。”

“唉！那种事才不会发生呢！”芬妮尖刻地说。

## III The Challenge / 挑战

===Original===

Roxhythe shut the door.

“And now what is it?”

Crewe was standing by the fire. At my lord’s words he swung round.

“It is this, sir! I’ll not have my wife’s name dishonoured by such as you! For nearly three years it has gone on! At first I thought nothing; she had her admirers, but she loved me. And then you gradually stole her from me, until she thinks of naught save when she shall next be with you! Oh, I’m not blind! I’ve watched and waited. But tonight I could no longer contain myself! One of us dies, my lord!”

“Very fine,” applauded Roxhythe. “But you make a deal out of nothing. Let us say that I fascinated Lady Crewe. We played at love, *bien sûr*. Now we have agreed to end the game. As to her good name, no harm is like to come to that.”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯关上了门。

“好了，现在又是什么事？”

克鲁（Crewe）立在壁炉边。听了爵爷这话，他霍地转过身来。

“是这么回事，先生！我不容你这样的人玷污我妻子的名声！这事情拖了将近三年了！起初我什么也没多想；她有她的爱慕者，可她爱的是我。后来你一点一点把她从我身边偷了去，偷到她如今心里除了几时能再见到你，什么都没有了！哦，我不瞎！我一直看着，等着。可是今晚我再也按捺不住了！咱们俩总得死一个，大人！”

“说得好，”罗克斯海斯抚掌赞道。“可你这是小题大作，无中生有。就算是我迷住了克鲁夫人吧。我们逢场作戏，*bien sûr*——自然喽。如今我们讲好了，这出戏就此收场。至于她的好名声，多半受不着什么损伤。”

---

===Original===

“No harm, you say? All the town will talk of this. How do I know that there is not more between you?”

The fine lips curled contemptuously.

“Faith, you have a good opinion of your wife!” said Roxhythe. Then he grew grave. “You foolish boy, what have you been about all this time? You say you have watched us? Then why a-God’s name did you not act? By heaven, I would let no man steal my wife’s heart!”

“If it can be stolen I do not want it! I’ll make no effort to win her from you, my lord! She﻿—she has earned my contempt! my hatred!”

===Chinese===

“不受损伤，你说？全城都会把这事挂在嘴上。我怎么知道你们中间没有更多的名堂？”

他那线条优美的嘴唇轻蔑地一撇。

“说实在的，你把你妻子看得也忒高了！”罗克斯海斯说。随后他正色起来。“你这傻孩子，这么些时候你都在干什么来着？你说你一直窥视着我们？那好，以上帝之名，你为什么不动手？上天在上，换了是我，谁也别想偷走我妻子的心！”

“既然偷得走，我也不要了！我不会费一丝力气把她从你手里夺回来，大人！她——她挣得了我的鄙夷！我的憎恨！”

---

===Original===

“The tragic hero, egad! One would think there was more to this affaire than a series of very mild flirtations.”

“I do think it!”

“Then you are a foolish child. Strive to be wiser. I suggest you take your wife away, and woo her afresh. She will very soon forget me.”

Crewe gripped a chair-back. His face was white with anger.

===Chinese===

“好一位悲剧主角，凭上帝起誓！旁人还当这桩风流案里有什么大文章，远不止一串再温和不过的调情呢。”

“我就是这么想的！”

“那你就是个傻孩子。学得聪明些吧。我劝你带着你的妻子走，重新向她求一回爱。她很快就会把我忘掉的。”

克鲁死死攥住一个椅背。他气得满脸煞白。

---

===Original===

“How dare you mock me? One would think that I was to blame for all this!”

“Most undoubtedly you are. Instead of freezing the girl you should have shaken her soundly and taken her away. *Mordieu*, you drove her to my arms, with your coldness and your scowls!”

“I thought her above﻿—this kind of intrigue! I﻿—heaven, what did I not think her? I have found that she is no better than the commonest trull that walks the streets!”

“La-la! What a fury! I begin to pity your wife.”

===Chinese===

“你竟敢讥嘲我？倒好像这一切都是我的不是！”

“千真万确，正是你的不是。你光是冷待那姑娘还不够，本该把她结结实实摇醒、把她带走。*Mordieu*——我的老天，正是你那副冷淡、你那副皱眉蹙额，把她推进了我的怀抱！”

“我以为她不至于——不屑于这一路的私情勾当！我——天哪，我什么没把她想过？如今我才发现，她同街上走动的最下贱的娼妇没有两样！”

“啦啦！好大的火性！我开始可怜你太太了。”

---

===Original===

Two hectic spots of colour burned on Sir Henry’s cheeks.

“We’ll have done, if you please, sir﻿—”

Roxhythe sighed with relief.

“Now God be thanked, here’s sense at last!”

===Chinese===

两片病热的红晕在亨利爵士（Sir Henry）的双颊上烧着。

“咱们到此为止吧，先生——”

罗克斯海斯如释重负地舒了口气。

“谢天谢地，总算听见句明白话！”

---

===Original===

“Will you name your friends?”

Roxhythe looked him up and down.

“Oh. You want satisfaction? Bethink you, you’ll damage your wife’s reputation as I have not done all this time.”

“I will take care of that, I thank you. Will you name your friends?”

===Chinese===

“你可肯报出你的决斗助手？”

罗克斯海斯把他上下打量了一番。

“哦。想要讨个了断？你想清楚，你这样只会糟蹋你太太的名声，这等糟蹋，我至今还未曾做过呢。”

“这就不劳费心了，多谢。你到底肯不肯报出你的助手？”

---

===Original===

“No,” said Roxhythe. “I will not.”

It seemed that Crewe was dumbfounded. He stared in amazement.

“You will not? You will not? Am I to call you coward then?”

“My dear boy, you may call me what you will if it eases you at all. Roxhythe does not fight with every fly that buzzes in his ear.”

===Chinese===

“不，”罗克斯海斯说，“不肯。”

克鲁似乎给惊呆了。他愕然瞪视。

“你不肯？你不肯？那我只有叫你一声懦夫了？”

“我亲爱的孩子，只要能叫你舒坦些，你爱叫我什么都使得。罗克斯海斯不同每一只在耳边嗡嗡的苍蝇开战。”

---

===Original===

Crewe sent a chair spinning across the room.

“Yet you will fight me, sir!”

“If you continue in this vein it seems very likely. I counsel you to calm yourself. It is no light matter to fight Roxhythe.”

“I am not afraid! Right is on my side!”

===Chinese===

克鲁一脚踢得一张椅子满屋乱转。

“可你必须跟我打，先生！”

“你若照这个调门闹下去，那多半免不了。我劝你镇定些。同罗克斯海斯交手，可不是小事一桩。”

“我不怕！公理在我一边！”

---

===Original===

“But in this world it is more often might that triumphs. My tragic hero, do you not realize that I could pink you within﻿—one minute?”

“I care not! And I believe that I can kill you!”

“The worse for you then. You were as effectually damned. You would have to reckon with King Charles. I’d not envy you that task. No, I will not fight you.”

“Then you are a coward! a coward! a coward! All the world will call you one!”

===Chinese===

“可在这世上，得胜的往往是强力。我的悲剧主角，你莫非不明白，不出——一分钟，我就能戳中你？”

“我不在乎！而且我相信我杀得了你！”

“那你更糟。你反正一样万劫不复。你还得去同查理国王算账。这份差事，我可不会羡慕你。不，我不跟你打。”

“那你就是个懦夫！懦夫！懦夫！普天下都会这么叫你！”

---

===Original===

“All the world will laugh at you for your pains, Crewe. The world knows what manner of man I am.”

“You insult me! Am I unworthy of your sword?”

“By no means. But I do not murder babes.”

Crewe looked up into the mocking eyes. His hand fumbled in the breast of his coat and came out. With one laced glove he struck my lord across the face.

===Chinese===

“普天下只会笑你白费气力，克鲁。世人知道我是何等样人。”

“你侮辱我！我不配你的剑么？”

“断然不是。只是我不杀婴孩。”

克鲁抬头望进那双含讥带讽的眼睛。他的手在外衣前襟里摸索了一阵，抽了出来。拿一只绦带手套，照爵爷的脸上就是一下。

---

===Original===

“Is that enough?” he panted.

The straight brows contracted swiftly.

“Almost enough to earn you a thrashing at my hands, Crewe,” said Roxhythe, a hint of grimness in his smooth voice.

Sir Henry fell back. A sob tore at his throat.

===Chinese===

“这够了吧？”他气喘吁吁地说。

那两道笔直的眉倏地蹙紧。

“差一点就够你挨我一顿好打了，克鲁，”罗克斯海斯说，圆润的嗓音里透着一丝狠意。

亨利爵士踉跄后退。一声呜咽撕裂他的喉头。

---

===Original===

“My God, are you made of stone? You’ll swallow that insult?”

My lord shrugged.

“I have already told you; I do not murder babes.”

“Damn you, am I to strike you again?”

===Chinese===

“我的上帝，你是石头做的么？这样的侮辱，你也当真咽得下去？”

爵爷耸了耸肩。

“我已经告诉过你了；我不杀婴孩。”

“遭瘟的，还要我再打你一下么？”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe smiled.

Crewe’s hand clenched on the glove, twisting it round and round.

“Can I say nothing to move you? What have I done that you should scorn to fight me? Do you not owe me at least that much?”

“My good child, no. I have not damaged Lady Crewe’s reputation; I am even preventing you from so doing.”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯微微一笑。

克鲁的手把那只手套攥得死紧，一圈又一圈地拧着。

“我说什么都不能叫你回心转意？我究竟做了什么，你竟不屑同我交手？这一点，你至少总欠着我吧？”

“我的好孩子，不欠。我不曾糟蹋克鲁夫人的名声；我此刻还在拦着你，免得你自己去糟蹋它呢。”

---

===Original===

“I will cry this shame against you! All London shall know how you refused to fight! were afraid to fight!”

“You would be very ill-advised. You would ruin your wife, and make yourself a laughingstock. Do you think I cannot afford to refuse to fight without injuring mine honour?”

Crewe stood still, seething with rage and impotence.

“Why will you not fight me? What reason have you?”

===Chinese===

“我要把这份耻辱当众揭出来！全伦敦都会知道你怎样拒绝应战！怎样不敢应战！”

“那你是极不明智。你会毁了你妻子，还把自己变成笑柄。你当我拒绝一战，就保不住自家的名誉么？”

克鲁僵在原地，一腔怒火与无力在胸中煎熬。

“你为什么不肯跟我打？你有什么缘由？”

---

===Original===

“I thought that I told you that,” said Roxhythe.

“Bah! ’Tis not from any desire to spare my life, I know!”

“Why then, we will say that it is not my will.”

“Do you think to put me off with that excuse? You treat me as though I were of no account! as though you had not ruined my happiness, disgraced my wife!”

===Chinese===

“我记得这个我已经告诉过你了，”罗克斯海斯说。

“呸！我知道，绝不是发什么善心要饶我的命！”

“那么好吧，我们就说：非我本意。”

“你想拿这样的托词搪塞我？你待我，就仿佛我毫无分量！仿佛你不曾毁掉我的幸福、玷污我的妻子！”

---

===Original===

My lord rearranged his cravat.

“I’ve no taste for heroics off the stage, my friend.”

The young man’s breath was coming short and quick. His hands trembled; his eyes burned dark in his pale face.

“Don’t mock at me! You﻿—you goad me to what I will not think of! I could kill you where you stand, you smiling devil!”

===Chinese===

爵爷理了理他的领巾。

“戏台子底下的英雄做派，我没有胃口，我的朋友。”

年轻人的呼吸变得又短又促。他两手发颤；一双眼睛在他苍白的脸上烧得乌黑。

“别拿话讥我！你——你把我往我不敢想的事情上逼！我能当场宰了你，你这笑面的恶魔！”

---

===Original===

My lord was still busy with his cravat. He stood with his back to Crewe looking into the mirror.

“*Eh bien!* Kill me.”

Crewe swung round on his heel. Up and down the room he paced, with white lips and trembling hands. He came at last to a standstill, facing my lord.

“Once more I ask: will you name your friends?”

===Chinese===

爵爷还在忙着他的领巾。他背朝克鲁立着，望着镜子。

“*Eh bien*——好啊！杀了我。”

克鲁猛地转过脚跟。他屋前屋后地踱步，嘴唇发白，双手打颤。末了他停住，正对着爵爷。

“我再问一遍：你肯不肯报出你的助手？”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe studied his reflection pensively.

“I will not.”

Crewe was almost hysterical with rage. He tore at his sword, wrenching it from the scabbard.

“You shall fight! If you will not have it in order, it shall be here and now! On guard, my lord!”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯若有所思地端详着镜中的自己。

“不肯。”

克鲁怒得几乎歇斯底里。他扑向佩剑，猛力把它拽出鞘来。

“你非打不可！你不肯按规矩来，那就现时现地！预备，大人！”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe gave a finishing touch to his laces, and turned.

“What have we now? *Corbleu!* A sword! Am I to fling myself on its point?”

“Draw, curse you!”

Roxhythe snapped his fingers scornfully.

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯把身上的绦带花边最后理了一理，这才转过身来。

“这又是哪一出？*Corbleu*——老天在上！一把剑！要我自个儿扑到剑尖上去么？”

“拔剑呀，遭瘟的！”

罗克斯海斯轻蔑地打了个响指。

---

===Original===

“So much for that pretty plaything! I do not fight you now or at any time. Body o’ me, am I to fight every young cockerel who fancies himself injured by me? Put up your sword and be thankful that I do not choose to take offence.”

The sword clattered to the ground.

“Devil! Devil!” gasped Sir Henry, and sprang at him.

There was a short struggle, a strangled oath from Crewe. Roxhythe had both the boy’s wrists in a vice-like grip. He did not appear to exert himself in the least, but Crewe could not break free. The pressure tightened relentlessly.

===Chinese===

“那件漂亮的小玩意儿就到此为止吧！眼下不打，任何时辰我也不跟你打。上帝的圣体，但凡哪一个自以为受了我委屈的愣头小公鸡，我都得陪他打上一场么？把剑收好吧，还得谢我不打算动气才是。”

剑哐啷一声跌落在地。

“恶魔！恶魔！”亨利爵士气噎道，纵身朝他扑去。

短促的一场扭打，克鲁迸出一声闷在喉头的咒骂。罗克斯海斯把那孩子的两只手腕一并攥进铁钳也似的掌握里。他看上去半点不曾用力，克鲁却挣脱不得。钳力无情地收紧。

---

===Original===

“Fool!” said my lord evenly. “I could ruin you ten times over for this. What madness has come over you that you dare to challenge me in such a fashion?”

Sir Henry was silent, clenching his underlip hard between his teeth. The grip on his wrists was agony. Roxhythe looked down at him contemptuously.

“If you like I will swear that at my hands Lady Crewe has received no ill, save, perhaps, a little heartache. Is that enough?”

“No, no! Damn you, let me go! I’ll allow no man﻿—to make love to﻿—my wife﻿—and go﻿—unpunished!”

===Chinese===

“蠢货！”爵爷语气平平地说。“单凭这一下，我就能把你毁上十遍。你中了什么魔，竟敢用这等方式向我寻衅？”

亨利爵士默不作声，把下唇死死咬在齿间。腕上的钳握痛得钻心。罗克斯海斯轻蔑地俯视着他。

“你若愿意，我可以起誓：克鲁夫人从我手里，不曾受过什么委屈，至多，或许，添了一点儿心疼。这够不够？”

“不够，不够！遭瘟的，放开我！我绝不容许随便哪个男人——向我妻子——献殷勤——还逍遥——法外！”

---

===Original===

“I applaud you. But yours would be the punishment an I met you.”

“I’ll take my chance of that! Let go my wrists! Do you think I care whether I live or die? Oh, name your seconds! Name them!”

“No.”

“For God’s sake forget that you are Roxhythe for one moment!”

===Chinese===

“我可要为你鼓掌了。不过倘若我同你交手，受惩罚的该是你。”

“这个我甘愿碰运气！放开我的手腕！你以为我在乎自己的死活么？哦，报出你的助手来！报出来！”

“不。”

“看在上帝份上，就这一会儿，忘了你是罗克斯海斯吧！”

---

===Original===

“It is as well that one of us should remember it.”

“ ’Sdeath! Are you a creature of flesh and blood? I’ve struck you! I have offered you every insult! Is it possible that you can still refuse me satisfaction?”

“In truth, I am very forbearing,” sighed Roxhythe.

Crewe struggled to be free of him.

===Chinese===

“好在我们两个里头，总还有一个记得。”

“上帝之死！你是血肉之躯么？我打了你！各样侮辱我都奉送过了！你竟还能拒绝给我一个了断？”

“说真的，我是极能容让的了，”罗克斯海斯叹道。

克鲁挣扎着要摆脱他。

---

===Original===

“Then again I call you coward! I’ll never rest until I have met you!”

Roxhythe released him.

“If you pester me it will be my painful duty to have you removed. I repeat: neither now or at any other time will I fight you. That is my last word.”

Crewe fell back. The marks of Roxhythe’s fingers were on his arms; dry sobs shook him. He collapsed into a chair, resting his head in his hands.

===Chinese===

“那我还是叫你懦夫！不同你交过手，我永无宁日！”

罗克斯海斯放开了他。

“你若再来纠缠，我就只好忍痛着人把你请出去了。我再说一遍：现在不打，往后任何时辰也不打。这是我最后一句话。”

克鲁踉跄后退。他的臂上留着罗克斯海斯的指痕；干涩的呜咽震得他浑身发抖。他瘫倒进一张椅子，双手抱住了头。

---

===Original===

Roxhythe shook out his ruffles.

The door was opened; Sir Henry heard the snap as it was closed again. He was alone.

Ten minutes later Roxhythe was at Mrs. Carthew’s side, drawling witticisms.

Lady Frances came up with Mr. Fletcher at her elbow. She smiled sweetly.

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯抖了抖他的褶皱花边袖饰。

门开了；门重又合上时那声轻响，亨利爵士也听见了。屋里只剩他一人。

十分钟后，罗克斯海斯已经坐在卡休夫人（Mrs. Carthew）身旁，拖着悠长的调子说着妙语。

弗朗西丝夫人由弗莱彻先生（Mr. Fletcher）陪着走了过来。她甜甜地一笑。

---

===Original===

“Mrs. Carthew, may I present Mr. Fletcher?”

The lady bowed.

“Will you dance, Madam?” simpered Fletcher.

Madam was uncertain. Plainly she liked Roxhythe’s company. But her hostess was already engaging his attention.

===Chinese===

“卡休夫人，容我引见弗莱彻先生？”

那位夫人欠身还礼。

“肯赏光跳个舞么，夫人？”弗莱彻媚声媚气地问。

夫人有些踌躇。分明她还愿意同罗克斯海斯作伴。可招待她的女主人已经把他的注意力占住了。

---

===Original===

“Thank you, sir.” She was led away.

Lady Frances sat down beside my lord.

“Roxhythe, why have you tampered with that poor child?” Her voice was very calm.

“My dear Fanny, need we pursue the subject? I do not care to dwell on my mistakes.”

===Chinese===

“多谢你，先生。”她给领走了。

弗朗西丝夫人在爵爷身旁坐下。

“罗克斯海斯，你何苦去招惹那可怜的孩子？”她的声音十分平静。

“我亲爱的芬妮，这话题还非追下去不可么？我不乐意细想自己的错处。”

---

===Original===

“You admit that it was a mistake? David, I implore you, let it end here!”

“It ended an hour since. I found myself growing paternal.”

“I am thankful for’t. The girl fancies herself in love with you. I pray heaven ’tis but a fancy. I have told her what manner of man you are.”

“Really? What manner of man am I?”

===Chinese===

“你承认这是个错处了？大卫，我求你，就叫它到此为止吧！”

“一个钟头前就完了。我发觉自己渐渐拿出父辈的架势来了。”

“那我真要谢天谢地。那姑娘自以为爱上了你。我祷告上天，但愿这只是痴想。我已经告诉过她，你是个什么样的人。”

“哦？我是个什么样的人？”

---

===Original===

She disregarded him.

“David, it was not right; it was not fair. I’d say naught if she were a Court miss, versed in these ways. She is not. She knew no harm until you came into her life. And now﻿—God and you know what harm has been wrought.”

“None.”

“That is true, Roxhythe?”

===Chinese===

她不理会他。

“大卫，这不对；这不公平。倘若她是个宫里见惯风月、深谙此道的姑娘，我便一个字也不提。她不是。在你闯进她的生活以前，她不识半点邪祟。而如今——酿下了什么伤害，上帝和你心中有数。”

“没有。”

“这话当真，罗克斯海斯？”

---

===Original===

“As I live. I believe I must always have felt paternal towards her. It was a very mild intrigue.”

Lady Frances heaved a sigh of relief.

“I feared﻿—she was so very overwrought﻿—If you say it is not so, I believe you. But, oh, David, why? Why try to break her poor, foolish little heart? Were there not enough women besides her? Women who knew you and your ways?”

“It was her sweet simplicity that attracted me,” said Roxhythe.

===Chinese===

“拿我的性命起誓。我相信我对她自始至终都是一片父辈心肠。那不过是段再温和不过的风流小戏。”

弗朗西丝夫人如释重负地舒了一口气。

“我怕来着——她当时那样心神激荡——你既说不是，我就信你。可是，哦，大卫，何苦呢？何苦要碎她那颗可怜、痴傻的小小心？除了她，别的女人还不够多么？那些深知你和你那套做派的女人？”

“吸引我的，正是她那份甜美的天真，”罗克斯海斯说。

---

===Original===

“So you broke her for your pleasure. Sometimes I think that you are utterly without heart, David.”

“Mayhap. However, you’ll agree that I am not without forbearance when I tell you that for the past hour I have been closetted with the husband, refusing to fight him.”

“Ah! You will not fight him?”

“Certainly not. Why should I?”

===Chinese===

“于是你为了自己的快活把她毁了。有时候我想，你这人简直没有心，大卫。”

“也许。不过，等我告诉你——过去这一个钟头，我一直同她丈夫关在一间屋里，拒不同他决斗——你总得承认，我并非没有容让之德。”

“啊！你不肯跟他打？”

“断然不打。我为什么要打？”

---

===Original===

“I know why you should not! ’Twere Lady Crewe’s ruin an you did.”

“So I thought. Unhappily he did not. He did all in his power to provoke me to wrath.”

“He failed?”

“Can you ask? I have told him that I will not meet him now or at any other time.”

===Chinese===

“我知道你为什么不该打！你若打了，克鲁夫人就毁了。”

“我也是这么想的。可惜他不这么想。他使出浑身解数撩拨我的火气。”

“他没成？”

“这还用问？我已经告诉他，现在不同他交手，往后任何时辰也不。”

---

===Original===

“David, promise me that you will not go back on that!”

“I promise.”

She touched his hand, smiling a little tremulously.

“You’re not all bad, David. I believe that you are sorry for this﻿—mistake.”

===Chinese===

“大卫，答应我，这话你决不反悔！”

“我答应。”

她碰了碰他的手，笑意微微发颤。

“你还不算全坏，大卫。我相信你为这桩——错事，心里是懊悔的。”

---

===Original===

“I regret it with all my heart. The child took me more seriously than I knew.”

Lady Frances dabbed surreptitiously at her eyes.

“My dear,” said Roxhythe, “if you cry, I shall depart. I have had naught but tears and ravings all the evening.”

“Poor David! Oh me! I should be angry with you, I suppose. Somehow I cannot. You had best make love to me next time. Then neither of us will be hurt.”

===Chinese===

“我打心底里懊悔。这孩子把我的话当了真，比我自知的还认真。”

弗朗西丝夫人偷偷按了按眼角。

“我亲爱的，”罗克斯海斯说，“你再哭，我可要走了。整整一晚，我领教的不是眼泪就是疯话。”

“可怜的大卫！哎呀！按说我该生你的气。不知怎么，就是气不起来。下回你还是来向我献殷勤吧。那样咱们俩谁也不受伤。”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe kissed her hand.

“Two women there are, Fanny, whom I esteem above all others. One is now a memory.”

Frances looked up.

“Who was she?”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯吻了吻她的手。

“世上有两个女人，芬妮，我把她们看得高过一切旁人。其中一个，如今只剩一段记忆了。”

弗朗西丝抬起眼睛。

“她是谁？”

---

===Original===

“She was Madame.”

“Madame! Roxhythe, you loved her?”

“I respected and admired her above all women. The other is your sweet self. If ever I love, or have loved a woman, you are she.”

“How dear of you!” sighed my lady. “To how many women have you said that?”

===Chinese===

“她是夫人（Madame）。”

“夫人！罗克斯海斯，你爱过她？”

“我敬她重她，高过一切女子。另一个，就是你这可人儿。倘若我正爱着、或是曾爱过一个女人，那人就是你。”

“你这话叫人多受用！”夫人叹道。“这话你同多少个女人说过啦？”

## IV Progression / 层层进逼

===Original===

At Drury Lane Christopher met Harcourt. They sat side by side in the Pit, and during the intervals, exchanged confidences. After the play they went together to partake of supper. When the dishes had been set before them Harcourt shook his head at Christopher.

“Oh, Chris, you are very wily!”

Christopher sampled a pasty.

“Am I? Why?”

“You led me to think that your master was no plotter.”

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗在德鲁里巷剧院（Drury Lane）遇见了哈考特。两人并肩坐在池座里，幕间休息时互吐衷曲。散了戏，他们又一道去用夜宵。菜肴摆上桌，哈考特朝克里斯托弗直摇头。

“噢，克里斯，你好狡猾！”

克里斯托弗尝了一口馅饼。

“是么？怎么说？”

“你叫我以为你家主人不是玩弄阴谋的人。”

---

===Original===

It was a bold attack, but it failed.

“Nor is he.” Christopher went on with the pasty.

Harcourt laughed long and low.

“Why, Chris, have you heard none of the rumours current in town?”

“I hear a good many lies. Which one is this?”

===Chinese===

这一击颇为大胆，却落了空。

“他本来就不是。”克里斯托弗继续对付他的馅饼。

哈考特低低地笑了好一阵。

“咦，克里斯，城里这些流言你竟一件也没听说？”

“谎话我倒听得多了。你说的是哪一句？”

---

===Original===

“That the King made a treaty with France some time back﻿—secretly.”

“Oh, that!” Christopher was scornful. “I wonder you give ear to these rumours, Harcourt.”

“But my dear boy, men say that it was for that reason that we went to war with Holland!” He was watching Christopher closely.

“Men will say anything,” replied Dart. It was a very fair imitation of Roxhythe’s style.

===Chinese===

“说国王前些日子同法兰西订了盟约——偷偷摸摸订下的。”

“噢，那个！”克里斯托弗满脸不屑。“哈考特，这种流言你也肯侧耳去听，我真纳闷。”

“可我亲爱的孩子，人家都说，我们同荷兰开战就为的是这个！”他的眼睛紧紧盯住克里斯托弗。

“人们什么都敢说，”达特答道。这一答学罗克斯海斯的腔调，学得惟妙惟肖。

---

===Original===

Harcourt’s eyes narrowed.

“Are you fencing with me, I wonder?”

Christopher looked up, smiling.

“Odso! I? No.”

It was impossible to look into his clear, honest eyes and to disbelieve his word. Harcourt was puzzled.

===Chinese===

哈考特眯起了眼睛。

“我倒想问一句，你是不是在同我兜圈子？”

克里斯托弗抬起头来，面带微笑。

“上帝的真言！我？才不会呢。”

望着那双清澈、老实的眼睛，谁也无法不信他的话。哈考特心里犯了嘀咕。

---

===Original===

“I have heard it said also that Roxhythe worked the intrigue. You remember how often he was in Paris?”

“Ay. And I know why. It was not intrigue.”

“Oh! Then you do not think that the King allied himself with France behind our backs?”

“Of course I do not. Is that a wild duck?”

===Chinese===

“我还听人说，那场阴谋就是罗克斯海斯穿针引线。你可记得他在巴黎待过多少回？”

“记得。我也知道缘故。那不是阴谋。”

“哦！那你是不信国王背着我们同法兰西结了盟？”

“当然不信。那是野鸭吗？”

---

===Original===

Harcourt pushed the dish towards him. Christopher had a fine, healthy appetite.

“I don’t trust the King,” said Harcourt profoundly.

“You must always be mistrusting someone, Sydney,” said Christopher, amused. “What’s to do now?”

“Why were we deprived of Shaftesbury? Why have we this Danby?”

“God knows. I don’t meddle in politics. You had best ask Shaftesbury himself. I hear he is much with your master.”

===Chinese===

哈考特把那盘菜推到他面前。克里斯托弗胃口极好，一身健旺。

“我不信国王，”哈考特意味深长地说。

“西德尼，你总得疑心着谁才踏实，”克里斯托弗被逗乐了。“这回又是哪一桩？”

“好端端的，凭什么革了沙夫茨伯里（Shaftesbury）的职？又凭什么给我们塞来一个丹比（Danby）？”

“天晓得。政治我一概不掺和。这你顶好去问沙夫茨伯里本人。听说他如今同你家主人过从甚密。”

---

===Original===

Harcourt frowned.

“Perhaps I shall. Is it possible that you can trust Danby?”

“I hardly know him,” said Christopher. He attacked the wild duck with some vigour.

“But his policy! It is all cringing to the Court.”

===Chinese===

哈考特皱起眉头。

“兴许我真会去问。你难道信得过丹比？”

“我同他谈不上认识，”克里斯托弗说。他起劲地对那只野鸭发动了进攻。

“可他那一套政策！一味的向宫廷摇尾乞怜。”

---

===Original===

“Is it?”

“I mislike his distribution of money. It smacks of bribery.”

“Sydney, I recommend this bird﻿—oh, I beg your pardon! yes, bribery. Certainly.”

“Associating with Roxhythe has made you very careless,” reproved his friend.

“I have already told you that I do not meddle in what I do not understand. I have abundant faith in His Majesty’s discretion﻿—and that is all there is to it. How is Madame Harcourt?”

===Chinese===

“是么？”

“我不喜欢他分发钱款的手法。一股贿赂的气味。”

“西德尼，我劝你尝尝这只鸭——噢，失敬失敬！对，贿赂。可不是嘛。”

“同罗克斯海斯厮混，把你也混得这样心不在焉了，”他的朋友数落道。

“我已经同你说过，不懂的事我不掺和。我对陛下的分寸有十二分的信心——如此而已。哈考特夫人近来可好？”

---

===Original===

Harcourt gave it up, and Christopher promptly forgot the conversation.

Later in the week he called on Lady Frances to whom he was more than ever attached.

She greeted him gaily. With her was Lady Crewe, and Christopher saw that Millicent had been crying.

“Do I intrude?” he asked, smiling.

“By no means!” answered Frances. “We are delighted to see you, are we not, Millicent?”

===Chinese===

哈考特只得作罢；克里斯托弗转眼便把这场谈话忘到了九霄云外。

那个星期晚些时候，他去拜望弗朗西丝夫人——他如今对她依恋更甚于往日。

她欢欢喜喜地迎他。克鲁夫人也在座，克里斯托弗看得出米莉森特哭过。

“我来得不凑巧吧？”他笑着问。

“哪里的话！”弗朗西丝答道。“见到你高兴还来不及呢，是不是，米莉森特？”

---

===Original===

Lady Crewe assented. Christopher kissed both their hands, and sat down. For a short space he entertained them with snatches of gossip. Lady Crewe was palpably ill-at-ease and anxious to be gone. Before very long she rose, murmuring excuses.

Lady Frances took her hand.

“Must you go? Well, I’ll not press you to stay, as I know how busy you are. Chris, wait for me!” She went out with Millicent.

===Chinese===

克鲁夫人应承了一声。克里斯托弗吻过两位夫人的手，坐了下来。有那么一小会儿，他说些零碎闲话给她们解闷。克鲁夫人分明局促不安，一心只想脱身。没过多久，她便起身，嘴里支吾着道歉。

弗朗西丝夫人拉住她的手。

“非走不可？好吧，我知道你事多，便不强留你了。克里斯，等我一下！”她同米莉森特一道出去了。

---

===Original===

When she returned, Christopher looked at her, one eyebrow raised inquiringly.

“Well?”

“Well what?” asked her ladyship, swinging her brocades.

“I want to know.”

“Inquisitive child! Again what?”

“Is it all at an end between Lady Crewe and Roxhythe?”

===Chinese===

她回来时，克里斯托弗望着她，一根眉毛探询似地扬起。

“怎么样？”

“什么怎么样？”夫人摆动着锦缎裙裾问道。

“我想知道嘛。”

“刨根问底的孩子！又想打听什么？”

“克鲁夫人和罗克斯海斯之间，是不是全完了？”

---

===Original===

Fanny sat down beside him.

“Thank heaven, yes! You noticed, then?”

“Since March he has hardly ever been at her side.”

“And she mopes and lies awake nights thinking of him. It’s a sad coil, Chris.”

“So I always thought. Lady Crewe looks very sick.”

===Chinese===

芬妮在他身旁坐下。

“谢天谢地，完了！这么说你也看出来了？”

“三月以来，他几乎不曾到过她身边。”

“她呢，终日郁郁，夜里想着他想得睡不着。一团乱麻哟，克里斯。”

“我向来就是这么看的。克鲁夫人气色坏得很。”

---

===Original===

Frances tapped her fan against the table.

“Because she hath a fool for husband! ’Pon rep, Chris, I’ve no patience with the man! Oh, I’ll tell you the whole story! You can be discreet, I know. In March I gave a ball; you remember? Well, they were both present. Roxhythe took Millicent into my little parlour and as far as I can gather there was something of a fracas. He discovered that he felt fatherly towards her and I suppose that he saw that she was too much in earnest for peace and quiet. To do him justice, I believe he meant to be kind then. They bade one another farewell, or some such nonsense, and the child wept very grievously. Roxhythe is too fascinating. At that moment in walked the husband! Conceive the tableau! Roxhythe brought Millicent to me, and went back to Sir Henry. According to him, Sir Henry was all for a duel, but he’d have none of it, and left the poor man disconsolate. No doubt he was very rude. Since then he has eschewed Millicent’s society. *Tant mieux.* All would then have been well had it not been for Crewe’s heroics. So Roxhythe calls it. Instead of treating the matter tactfully, he first raved at the child, and then turned a cold shoulder to her. They scarcely speak; each goes his own road, and each is very properly unhappy.

“I told Sir Henry he was a fool﻿—yes, was it not brave of me?﻿—and I told him to take Millicent away and be kind to her. Oh, he could have won her back! Instead he took her down into the country where she fretted herself to death. Now she thinks that she hates Crewe. I’ve talked to the man till I am tired, and to no avail. In fact, he sent me about my business. And so they go their ways. Millicent yearns for Roxhythe, because she wants love and Henry seems to have none for her. She sees David at all the houses they visit, and in that way the wound is kept open.”

===Chinese===

弗朗西丝拿扇骨轻叩着桌面。

“谁叫她嫁了个傻瓜丈夫！凭名誉起誓，克里斯，这个人我实在受不了！噢，我把整件事原原本本讲给你听！我知道你嘴严。三月里我办过一场舞会，你记得吧？喏，他们两位都在场。罗克斯海斯把米莉森特带进我的小客厅，据我打听来的消息，两人在那里起了场小小的风波。他发觉自己对她已只剩父亲般的心肠，我猜他也看出她太认真了，这么下去难得安生。平心而论，我想他当时原是一片好意。两人互道了珍重——或是诸如此类的痴话——那孩子哭得很伤心。罗克斯海斯这人魅力太盛。偏赶在那当口，做丈夫的闯了进来！你想想那副画面！罗克斯海斯把米莉森特送到我跟前，自己回头去应付亨利爵士。据他说，亨利爵士一门心思要决斗，可他偏不接这个茬，撇下那可怜人独自懊丧去了。想必他是极无礼的。打那以后，他便避开米莉森特，不与她来往。*Tant mieux.*——这样再好不过。若不是克鲁那套英雄做派——罗克斯海斯就这么个叫法——事情本来到此也就顺当了。他不肯把事情处置得圆通些，先是冲那孩子大发雷霆，随后又给她一副冷肩膀。两人几乎不交一语；各走各的路，也各自不痛快——而且不痛快得恰到好处。

“我对亨利爵士说，他是个傻瓜——是啊，我胆子大不大？——我叫他带米莉森特走，好好待她。噢，他本可以把她赢回来的！可他倒好，把她带下乡去，听凭她在那里把心思熬煎成灰。如今她只道自己恨透了克鲁。我同那个人磨破了嘴皮，一无用处。说真的，他索性打发我别多管闲事。于是两人就这么各过各的。米莉森特一心痴念着罗克斯海斯，因为她要的是爱，而亨利对她似乎一点也没有。他们夫妇出入的宅门，处处都碰得见大卫，这伤口便一直敞着不合。”

---

===Original===

“I see,” said Christopher. “I had some notion of this, of course, but I did not know all. One does not question Roxhythe.”

“No,” agreed her ladyship. “One does not. I am very worried over this affaire. I must say that since the fracas Roxhythe hath not paid much heed to Millicent. But they meet everywhere﻿—and Roxhythe is all too magnetic. The child fancies herself madly in love with him.”

“I had not thought that. True, she does not look well, and she is less gay, but she scarce glances in Roxhythe’s direction.”

“Oh, she hath her pride!” said Frances. She sighed a little, and fell silent. After a few moments she smiled reminiscently.

===Chinese===

“原来如此，”克里斯托弗说。“这里头的情形我自然约略知道一些，只是不全。罗克斯海斯的事，谁也不好刨根问底。”

“可不是，”夫人附和道。“不好问。这件事我愁得很。说句公道话，打那场风波之后，罗克斯海斯确实不大理会米莉森特了。可他们偏偏处处碰面——罗克斯海斯那股魔力又实在撩人。那孩子自以为爱他爱得发了狂。”

“这个我倒没想到。不错，她气色不好，兴致也淡了，可她连朝罗克斯海斯那边望都不大望一眼。”

“噢，她有她的骄傲！”弗朗西丝说。她轻轻叹了口气，默然片刻，随即带着回想的笑意莞尔一笑。

---

===Original===

“Chris, who do you think waited on me yesterday?”

Christopher shook his head.

“Who?”

“Our new Earl!”

“What, Danby?”

“No less. Was it not amusing? I barely know him, and now, suddenly, he comes to see me!”

===Chinese===

“克里斯，你猜昨天谁来登门拜我？”

克里斯托弗摇了摇头。

“谁呀？”

“咱们的新伯爵大人！”

“什么，丹比？”

“正是他老人家。你说滑稽不滑稽？我跟他素昧平生，如今忽然登门来看我！”

---

===Original===

“Perhaps he has long been an admirer,” said Christopher, twinkling.

Her lightening smile flashed out.

“No such thing. He wanted to prove me concerning Jasper’s political sentiments.”

“Did he? What are his sentiments?”

“If I knew I do not suppose that I should tell you, my dear boy.”

===Chinese===

“兴许他仰慕夫人已久了，”克里斯托弗眨眨眼睛说。

她脸上倏地绽出一朵明快的笑。

“哪有的事。他是来探我的口风，想摸贾斯珀的政见。”

“哦？他的政见究竟如何？”

“就算我知道，想来也不会告诉你，我的好孩子。”

---

===Original===

“As I am aware that you do know, I take that as a very decided snub!”

“*Soit!* I’ll tell you: Jasper belongs to no party.”

“Wise man.”

“So I think. I mislike this Danby.”

===Chinese===

“我明白你准知道，那我就把这话当作一记干脆利落的闭门羹了！”

“*Soit!*——罢了，说给你听：贾斯珀不属任何一党。”

“聪明人。”

“我也这样想。这个丹比我不喜欢。”

---

===Original===

“That is curious,” remarked Christopher. “I met Harcourt the other day and he said much the same thing.”

“Harcourt is very often right. Why does he object to Danby?”

“I forget. Something concerning bribery, I think. He mistrusts everyone. Even the King is not above reproach.”

“Oh?” Lady Frances studied her fan. “Of what does he suspect the King?”

“Some tittle-tattle about selling England to France. Harcourt swallows every wild rumour that is current and firmly believes in it. ’Tis the way of his party.”

===Chinese===

“这就奇了，”克里斯托弗道。“前几天我遇见哈考特，他的说法同你如出一辙。”

“哈考特往往是对的。他为什么看不上丹比？”

“忘了。好像是关于贿赂什么的。他谁都信不过。连国王也逃不脱他的挑剔。”

“哦？”弗朗西丝夫人端详着手里的扇子。“他疑心国王什么？”

“什么把英格兰卖给法兰西的流言蜚语。眼下流行什么荒唐谣言，哈考特一概囫囵吞下，深信不疑。他那路人都是这个做派。”

---

===Original===

“I don’t think that, Chris. Harcourt usually has grounds for his suspicions.”

“Oh, he hath for this one, the war with Holland, and Shaftesbury’s resignation.”

“Ah! By the way, Chris, is not Shaftesbury your friend?”

“Hardly. He was a friend of my father’s and he has been very kind to me. Lately I have eschewed his company as he cannot meet me without deploring my regard for Roxhythe.”

===Chinese===

“我不这么看，克里斯。哈考特的疑心通常是有根有据的。”

“噢，这回他的疑心也有根有据——同荷兰的战事，还有沙夫茨伯里的辞职。”

“啊！对了，克里斯，沙夫茨伯里不是你的朋友么？”

“谈不上。他是我父亲的朋友，待我素来很好。近来我避开他，因为他一见了我，必要为我敬慕罗克斯海斯这件事哀叹连连。”

---

===Original===

“I see. That regard is as strong as ever?”

“An hundred times more strong!” said Christopher warmly.

Lady Frances said nothing.

===Chinese===

“原来如此。这份敬慕还同从前一般深？”

“深上百倍不止！”克里斯托弗热切地说。

弗朗西丝夫人没有作声。

---

===Original===

Not ten minutes after Christopher had departed, Montgomery came quickly into the room. Lady Frances laid down her embroidery.

“Well, Jasper?”

Montgomery flung himself into a chair. His face was overcast.

“Has His Majesty been at the House today? Is the dispute ended?”

“He has ended it very summarily.”

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗去了还不到十分钟，蒙哥马利便快步走进房来。弗朗西丝夫人放下手里的绣活。

“怎么了，贾斯珀？”

蒙哥马利一头栽进椅子里，面色阴沉。

“陛下今日可曾驾临议院？那场争执了结了没有？”

“他了结得非常干脆。”

---

===Original===

“Oh? What has happened?”

“We are prorogued.”

Lady Frances started. Her eyes crinkled at the corners; she laughed beneath her breath.

“My dear Jasper, he is a marvellous man!”

Montgomery shrugged despairingly.

===Chinese===

“哦？出了什么事？”

“议会叫他给休会了。”

弗朗西丝夫人一怔。她眼角漾起细纹，压着嗓子低低一笑。

“我亲爱的贾斯珀，这个人真是了不得！”

蒙哥马利无可奈何地耸耸肩。

---

===Original===

“It passes all bounds. We were all in a turmoil over this question of privilege﻿—Shaftesbury’s doing, of course. Had it to do with the Test, or had it not? The Houses were at one another’s throats; the King could do naught to settle the dispute. So he prorogued us. I tell you, Fanny, he’ll o’er-reach himself ere long. First we had Danby foisted upon us. By sheer force we made peace with Holland. That was February of last year. Did Charles recall the troops? No! He gives us shuffling answers. ’Tis my belief he is in French pay. There was dissension. Then Danby employs a little bribery, and all is quiet. The House turns against Lauderdale, as well it might. There was talk of impeachment. More bribery. No more talk of impeachment. Next we have the No-Popery cry, Danby heading it, the King﻿—ostensibly﻿—seconding.

“Then the bill offered to the Lords﻿—no person to sit in either House, or to hold any office without declaring all resistance to the King’s power criminal, or without swearing never to attempt to alter the government of Church or State.
