“Did you come here to quarrel with me?” asked Roxhythe. “Sit down, and talk of something else.”

===Chinese===

“我压根儿没留意过他们。想必是吧。”

“那您如今的行径，是我做梦也想不到会出在您身上的。怯懦！残忍！”

“你是专程来跟我吵架的么？”罗克斯海斯问。“坐下，说点别的。”

---

===Original===

“I came to implore you to help in the cause of right! I see I might as well talk to a stone!”

“My good child, you excite yourself over nothing.”

“Was it nothing that Father Coleman was murdered? That good man!”

“It was necessary. The King deplored it, but the people would have it.”

“I suppose you advocated it?” said Christopher bitterly.

===Chinese===

“我是来恳求您为公道出一把力的！如今看来，我还不如去对一块石头说话！”

“我的好孩子，你这是无端自扰。”

“科尔曼神父惨遭杀害，这也算不得什么？那样一位好人！”

“那是必需的。国王深以为憾，可百姓非如此不可。”

“想必还是您从中力促的罢？”克里斯托弗辛酸地说。

---

===Original===

“Certainly. I thought you knew that nothing counts with me save His Majesty’s safety and peace?”

“I﻿—I cannot answer you, sir. Oh﻿—oh, heaven, how I wish that I had never set eyes on you!”

Roxhythe stretched out his hand.

===Chinese===

“自然。我一直当您知道：除了陛下的安全与安宁，万事在我这里概不占分量。”

“我——我没法回答您，先生。哦——哦，上天，我多希望从来没有见过您！”

罗克斯海斯伸出手去。

---

===Original===

“Chris, dear boy, you are demented. Calm yourself.”

Christopher ignored his hand.

“Then ’tis you have driven me so! You did your best to break my heart﻿—and now you reveal yourself to me﻿—callous, ruthless! It﻿—hurts damnably, my lord.”

Roxhythe turned away. He said nothing.

===Chinese===

“克里斯，好孩子，你失心疯了。定一定神。”

克里斯托弗没有理会那只手。

“那么，把我逼成这样的正是您！您千方百计要叫我心碎——如今又向我现出这副真面目——冷漠，无情！这——疼得万分的厉害，大人。”

罗克斯海斯转过身去。他一言不发。

---

===Original===

“I﻿—I can’t rest! I﻿—oh, there’s no truth anywhere! no honour! I thought Russell and Worth were irreproachable; I thought Shaftesbury above suspicion! I was wrong, wrong, wrong! I’ve done with Englishmen! Each works for his own ends and cares not what means he employs to obtain them. Even you, my lord!”

“I suppose I should be grateful for the ‘even,’ ” said Roxhythe wearily.

Christopher went quickly to his side.

“Ah, no, sir! I﻿—didn’t mean it! I am distraught﻿—I﻿—never meant to say those things﻿—to you. Forgive me!”

Roxhythe laid a hand on his shoulder.

===Chinese===

“我——我静不下来！我——哦，放眼哪儿都没有真！没有义！我当罗素和沃思无可指摘，当沙夫茨伯里白璧无瑕！我错了，错了，错了！英国人我算是看穿了！个个为己谋私，用什么手段全然不顾。连您也一样，大人！”

“照这么说，对这个‘连’字，我该领情才是，”罗克斯海斯有气无力地说。

克里斯托弗快步走到他身旁。

“啊，不，先生！我——不是那个意思！我方寸已乱——我——绝没有想说那些话——对您。饶恕我！”

罗克斯海斯把一只手搭上他的肩头。

---

===Original===

“Chris, you are distraught because you are rudderless. Come back to me!”

“No﻿—no! I cannot! Less than ever now. I﻿—I think I shall go out of my mind soon!”

“Chris, you were happy with me. Come back!”

“Ah, so happy! It could never be the same again. I must go﻿—right away, where I shall not see you.”

===Chinese===

“克里斯，你方寸大乱，是因为没了掌舵的人。回到我身边来吧！”

“不——不！我不能！如今更是万万不能。我——我怕我快神志失常了！”

“克里斯，你跟着我是快活的。回来吧！”

“啊，是那样的快活！可是一切再不能照旧了。我必须走——远远地走，走到看不见您的地方去。”

---

===Original===

“Even though I beg you to stay?”

“Yes﻿—even then, my lord. Don’t try to persuade me! It is hard enough as it is.”

“So you’ll go away? Where?”

“Holland, sir. To join my brother, I think.”

“Orange,” said Roxhythe quietly. “That will be the end, Chris.”

===Chinese===

“即便我求你留下，也是这样？”

“是的——即便那时也是一样，大人。别想劝动我！眼前已经够难的了。”

“这么说，你是要走？去哪儿？”

“荷兰，先生。我想是去投奔我哥哥。”

“奥兰治，”罗克斯海斯淡淡地说。“那就意味着尽头了，克里斯。”

---

===Original===

“Yes, sir﻿—the﻿—end.”

“And all in search of﻿—what?”

“In search of honesty and truth. I will not sacrifice my honour for love of man.”

===Chinese===

“是的，先生——那——尽头。”

“而这一去，寻的又是——什么？”

“寻的是诚与真。为了爱一个人而牺牲我的名节，我不干。”

---

===Original===

“So instead you’ll sacrifice your happiness for that vague thing called patriotism?”

“I’ll find happiness in my patriotism!”

“You are like to be disappointed,” said Roxhythe.

===Chinese===

“那么你反倒要把自己的幸福，牺牲给那虚飘飘的所谓爱国心？”

“我在爱国之中自会寻得幸福！”

“你多半要失望的，”罗克斯海斯说。

## VI The Decision / 抉择

===Original===

Christopher wrote to Roderick, advising him of his coming to the Hague, and quickly made all his arrangements. Now that he had made his decision he was almost glad to be going. He longed to leave England behind him, and with it, all his uncertainties. Two days before his departure he visited Lady Frances.

She received him in her drawing-room. She thought she had never seen him look so old.

“Well, dear Chris?”

He sat down beside her, trying to smile.

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗写信给罗德里克，告知自己即将前往海牙，并很快把诸事一一安排停当。主意既定，此行反叫他近乎觉得庆幸。他一心盼着把英格兰抛在身后，连同满心的彷徨无着一并抛开。动身前两天，他去探望了弗朗西丝夫人。

她在客厅里接见他。她觉得从未见过他这般显老。

“怎么样，亲爱的克里斯？”

他在她身旁坐下，想勉强一笑。

---

===Original===

“I have come to﻿—say farewell, Lady Fanny.”

She sat very still.

“Ah﻿ ﻿… Holland.”

“Yes, Holland. You understand that I cannot remain in London?”

===Chinese===

“我是来——向您告别的，芬妮夫人。”

她端坐着，一动不动。

“啊……荷兰。”

“是的，荷兰。您明白，我是不能再留在伦敦的，对么？”

---

===Original===

“I suppose so,” she sighed. “Poor Chris!”

“Don’t﻿—pity me! I can’t bear it. There’s no peace for me in England, and no work. Always I think of Roxhythe, longing only to see him﻿—to hear his voice﻿—feel his hand in mine﻿—.” He stopped, biting his lip. “I am sorry. I have no right to weary you with such﻿—foolishness.”

She took both his hands.

“Chris, are we not friends? How could I be wearied? Won’t you﻿—tell me everything?”

“You are so kind,” said Christopher. “You’ve always been so kind﻿—I﻿—oh, to be able to talk to someone!”

“I know. You won’t go back to Roxhythe?”

===Chinese===

“我想也是，”她叹了口气。“可怜的克里斯！”

“别——可怜我！我受不了。在英格兰，我没有安宁，也没有差事。我时时刻刻想着罗克斯海斯，只想见他一面——听听他的声音——握一握他的手——”。他止住话头，咬着嘴唇。“抱歉。我没有权利拿这等——傻话来烦您。”

她把他两只手都握住了。

“克里斯，我们难道不是朋友吗？我怎么会嫌烦呢？你不愿意——把一切都讲给我听？”

“您真好，”克里斯托弗说。“您一直都这么好——我——啊，能对一个人倾诉，该有多好！”

“我明白。你不回罗克斯海斯那里去了？”

---

===Original===

“I cannot. You know what happened. You have heard all the tales concerning my lord. I should be acting falsely to all that I hold most sacred if I gave way to my longing to be with him.”

Again she sighed.

“And he does not﻿—care as I care. It is not to be expected. Lady Fanny﻿—I am not﻿—complaining, but﻿—I loved him so greatly! I trusted him so! And he tricked me. It’s﻿—all over. I’ve to forget it all. I’ve to forget Roxhythe, and all that he meant to me. I must go right away, where I shall not be so constantly reminded of him.”

She stroked his hand gently.

===Chinese===

“我不能。事情的原委您是知道的。那些关于大人的流言蜚语，您也都听遍了。倘若纵容自己那股想与他厮守的渴望，便是对我心中最神圣的一切背信弃义。”

她又叹了口气。

“而他并不——像我这样把他放在心上。这本也是奢望不得的。芬妮夫人——我并不是——在抱怨，可是——我那样深切地爱过他！我那样信任他！他却骗了我。一切都——完了。我必须把这一切忘掉。我必须忘掉罗克斯海斯，忘掉他曾之于我的一切意义。我必须远远走开，到一个不至于时时被他牵动心绪的地方去。”

她轻轻抚着他的手。

---

===Original===

“So you are for Holland? Perhaps it is best after all. But I shall miss you sadly, Chris.”

“Please﻿—don’t speak of it! I’ve so loved your friendship! But I must go.”

“I know you must, Chris. And I know how hard it is.”

“Hard!” he whispered. “It is﻿—tearing my heart out of my body. I﻿—” he smiled crookedly. “I leave it﻿—with him. I suppose I shall be at peace again﻿—one day. But I shall always remember these wonderful years﻿—when I was﻿—so happy. I should﻿—be grateful for them﻿—for the memory of them. Sometime I shall look back on it all calmly﻿—but just now﻿—I daren’t let myself think!”

“Dear boy, I am sorry from the depths of my heart! But you are right; this great, great ache will fade﻿—you’ll only remember the happiness and be glad that you were happy. And you’ll be happy again. You have your brother.”

===Chinese===

“这么说你是要去荷兰了？兴许这终究是桩好事。只是我会狠狠想你的，克里斯。”

“求您——别说了！您的情谊我是何等珍爱！可我必须走。”

“我知道你必须走，克里斯。我也知道这有多难。”

“难！”他低声道。“这是——把我的心从胸膛里活活撕出来。我——”他歪着嘴苦笑了一下。“就把它留在——他那里吧。想必有一天，我总会重归安宁的。可这些美好的岁月我会永远记得——那时候我——何等幸福。对此我应当——心存感激——感激这份记忆。总有一天我能平静地回望这一切——只是眼下——我不敢容自己多想！”

“可怜的孩子，我打心底里为你难过！可你说得对；这道深深、深深的创痛终会淡去——你只会记得那些幸福，并为曾经幸福而欢喜。你还会再幸福的。你还有你哥哥呢。”

---

===Original===

“Yes. He﻿—doesn’t count, you know. I﻿—never cared for him greatly, and since I have been with﻿—Roxhythe﻿—he has had all my love. He has it still. There will never be another in his place. I’m a weak fool﻿—but﻿—oh, Lady Frances, I want him so much!”

She tugged at his bowed shoulders.

“Don’t, Chris! Ah, don’t! He’s not worth it! Oh, why, why did he catch you in his net?”

“God knows. I don’t really regret it. He has been responsible for so much that was wonderful in my life. And now﻿—I hate all other masters. I compare them, you see﻿—and they don’t bear comparison. Roxhythe was﻿—I hardly know﻿—incomparable.”

===Chinese===

“是啊。可他——您知道，算不得什么。我——从来没有怎么疼爱过他，自从我跟了——罗克斯海斯——我全部的爱就都给了他。如今依然如此。那个位置上永远不会有第二个人。我是个没出息的傻瓜——可是——哦，弗朗西丝夫人，我是那么想要他！”

她拽了拽他低垂的双肩。

“别这样，克里斯！啊，别这样！他不值得！哦，为什么，为什么他偏偏要把你擒进他的网里去？”

“天晓得。我其实并不后悔。我生命里那么多美好的东西，都是拜他所赐。而如今——别的主子我都看不上了。你瞧，我总拿他们跟他比——他们根本不配比。罗克斯海斯是——我自己也说不清——无可比拟的。”

---

===Original===

“He is just Roxhythe,” said Fanny sadly.

Christopher caught his breath in a half-sob, half laugh.

“ ‘I am Roxhythe.’﻿ ﻿… I can hear him say it﻿—in that soft voice! So haughtily! Yes, he is just Roxhythe.” He stood up, biting his lip. “I leave the day after tomorrow, Lady Frances. You’ll not﻿—quite forget me?”

===Chinese===

“他就是罗克斯海斯，”芬妮怏怏地说。

克里斯托弗倒抽一口气，半是呜咽，半是轻笑。

“‘我是罗克斯海斯。’……我还能听见他说这话——用那种柔柔的嗓音！那么倨傲！是啊，他就是罗克斯海斯。”他站起身，咬着嘴唇。“我后天动身，弗朗西丝夫人。您不至于——把我忘得一干二净吧？”

---

===Original===

“Forget my Chris? Is it likely? One day you will come back. I am going to wait till then. You won’t forget me, I hope?”

He kissed her hands.

“It were impossible. I shall never forget﻿—all your kindness. You’ll let me write to you?”

===Chinese===

“忘掉我的克里斯？有可能吗？总有一天你会回来的。我就等到那一天。你不会忘了我的，我希望？”

他吻了她的双手。

“那决不可能。您的——种种恩情，我永远不会忘。您许我给您写信的，是么？”

---

===Original===

“You must write,” she said. “I should be so sad if you did not.”

“I can’t thank you enough﻿—Goodbye, Lady Fanny!”

My lady put her hands on his shoulders and lifted her face.

“You may kiss me, Chris. My poor, poor, Chris!”

===Chinese===

“你一定要写，”她说。“你不写，我会很难过的。”

“我感激不尽——再见了，芬妮夫人！”

夫人把双手搭上他的肩头，仰起了脸。

“你可以吻我，克里斯。我可怜的、可怜的克里斯！”

## VII The Revenge / 报复

===Original===

It was very cold. Outside a drizzling rain fell on the bleak gardens. The gaunt tree branches were wet and shining. Charles sat by the fire in his room, nursing a spaniel. His dark eyes were brooding, his fingers restless.

“You heard what Danby had to say, David?”

Roxhythe was gazing out into the rain.

“Ay. Montague has been elected member for Northamptonshire.”

“Danby tells me they quarrelled some time ago. God’s life, why must he quarrel with my French Ambassador of all people?”

“Does Danby think he means harm?”

===Chinese===

天很冷。外头细雨绵绵，落在一园子萧瑟的草木上。瘦削的枝桠湿漉漉地闪着微光。查理坐在房里的炉火边，怀里搂着一只长毛垂耳犬。他一双黑眼睛沉沉地思虑着，手指烦躁不安。

“丹比那番话，你可听说了，大卫？”

罗克斯海斯正凝望着窗外的雨。

“是啊。蒙塔古（Montague）选上了北安普敦郡（Northamptonshire）的议员。”

“丹比告诉我说，他们前些日子闹翻了。上帝的生命，天下人物那么多，他偏偏要同我的法国大使过不去，这是何苦来？”

“丹比认为他存心为害？”

---

===Original===

“Ay. He spoke of incriminating documents. You know what that means, Roxhythe.”

“Letters to Louis. I always said it was unwise.”

The King was peevish.

“If you had not fallen out of favour with Louis those letters need never have gone through Montague. Now we shall have Danby impeached.”

“I think I see the hand of Shaftesbury. Montague is a tool.”

“Shaftesbury or others. He hates Danby most.”

===Chinese===

“是啊。他说起有可以构陷定罪的文书。你明白那意味着什么，罗克斯海斯。”

“写给路易的信。我一向就说，此举不智。”

国王愠怒起来。

“你若不曾失了路易的欢心，那些信原犯不着经蒙塔古转手。这会儿，我们可要眼见丹比遭弹劾了。”

“我看得出沙夫茨伯里的手笔。蒙塔古不过一件工具。”

“沙夫茨伯里，或是旁人。他恨丹比最深。”

---

===Original===

“And Danby, being your tool, will turn on you.”

“Another tax on my ingenuity! Danby intends to strike at Montague before Montague has time to strike at him.”

“Better still to dispose of Montague.”

“No, David! I have had enough blood.”

Roxhythe shrugged.

===Chinese===

“而丹比既是你的工具，自会回过头来咬你。”

“又要给我的机变课一道税了！丹比打算抢在蒙塔古下手之前，先去打蒙塔古。”

“顶好是干脆把蒙塔古料理掉。”

“不成，大卫！我见的血已经够多了。”

罗克斯海斯耸了耸肩。

---

===Original===

“How does Danby think to strike at Montague?”

“Some talk of Montague’s conferring with the Papists without my knowledge. Danby plans to seize his papers.”

“Why, that is very well! He is to act in your name?”

“He says so. He is to inform the House of it tomorrow.”

There was a long silence. Presently the King turned his head.

===Chinese===

“丹比打算怎么个打法？”

“外间有人传说，蒙塔古瞒着我同天主教徒来往。丹比打算抄没他的文书。”

“咦，这倒好得很！他要假你的名号行事？”

“他是这么说。明天就要向下议院申告此事。”

半晌没有言语。过了一会儿，国王转过头来。

---

===Original===

“By the way, Davy, the Crewes are in town again.”

“Crewes?”

The King smiled faintly.

“Forgotten already? The man you fell afoul of two years ago.”

“That man! Yes, I remember. I told him to absent himself for a year.”

“Well, he has been gone for two. He dared to appear at Whitehall.”

===Chinese===

“对了，达维，克鲁夫妇又上伦敦来了。”

“克鲁夫妇？”

国王淡淡一笑。

“这就忘了？两年前跟你结怨的那一位。”

“那个人！哦，记得记得。我叫他出门避开一年。”

“哼，他这一去倒去了两年。他还敢上白厅来露面。”

---

===Original===

“Oh? What did you say?”

“Remembering your request I said nothing. But it was gross presumption on his part.”

“What of the wife?”

“She was there. I believe she has become most devoted.”

“I thought she would.” Roxhythe came to the fireplace. “King Louis hath his revenge on me, Sir.”

===Chinese===

“哦？你怎么说？”

“记着你的嘱咐，我什么也没说。不过他这么着，也未免太放肆了。”

“那位夫人呢？”

“她也在场。我相信她如今是顶忠贞的了。”

“我早料到了。”罗克斯海斯走到壁炉跟前。“路易王这就在向我报复了，陛下。”

---

===Original===

Charles raised his heavy brows.

“Louis? Why?”

“He conceived that I had promised to act in his interests. He was furious with me when you married the Lady Mary to the Prince.”

“A pity. What is his revenge?”

“I take it he has warned the Country Party against me.” Roxhythe smiled rather wearily. “No longer can I intrigue privately.”

“It’s a plaguey nuisance. Faith, Louis is no gentleman to turn informer in that fashion!”

===Chinese===

查理扬了扬浓重的眉毛。

“路易？为什么？”

“他认定我应承过事事顾着他的利益。您把玛丽小姐许给那位亲王的时候，他对我大为光火。”

“可惜。他怎么个报复法？”

“据我料想，他已告诫乡村党，教他们提防我。”罗克斯海斯笑了，笑得颇为倦怠。“往后再不能暗地里耍阴谋了。”

“这可真是一场遭瘟的麻烦。凭良心说，路易这般充当告密人的行径，可算不得君子！”

---

===Original===

“ ’Tis unkind of him, I admit. I am the less useful to you, Sir, in consequence.”

Charles stretched out his hand quickly.

“Don’t speak like that, Davy! Always you are my dearest friend!”

Roxhythe went on his knee.

“Always,” he said, and kissed the King’s hand. “Always.”

===Chinese===

“他这份无情，我认了。如此一来，我对陛下也就更没用了。”

查理赶忙伸出手来。

“别这么说，达维！你永远是我最亲爱的朋友！”

罗克斯海斯屈膝跪下。

“永远。”他说着，吻了吻国王的手。“永远。”

---

===Original===

Danby’s attack on Montague failed, for Montague carefully secreted the two most important documents in his possession. One of them was the letter written by Charles’ order before the Peace of Nimeguen. Backed by the Treasurer’s bitterest enemies he brought charges against Danby. Impeachment followed. There was fresh uproar in the House.

Danby narrowly escaped imprisonment, but the majority was small. Public feeling was against him.

Then Charles prorogued his unruly Parliament, and in January, scarcely a month after, dissolved it.

===Chinese===

丹比攻打蒙塔古的一仗落了空，因为蒙塔古早把他手中最重要的两份文书细细密藏起来。其中之一，便是奈梅亨和约签订之前奉查理之命写下的那封信。他倚仗财政大臣最狠的死敌们，反过来控告丹比。弹劾随之而来。下议院里又掀起一片新的喧腾。

丹比仅以身免，逃过下狱之灾，只是多数甚微。舆情尽于他不利。

随后查理便勒令他那不服管束的议会休会；不出一月，到得正月，索性把它解散了。

## VIII The Haven / 避风港

===Original===

After ten years Christopher returned to Holland. Much of it he had forgotten, much brought back old memories, poignant in their nearness to him. He had travelled from Harwich to Rotterdam, spending only a night in that town. He visited 19, Prinsen Straat, hoping to see de Staal again. It had been a shock to find the house in other hands and to hear that de Staal had gone to his rest four years ago. He had hardly realized how much he wanted to see the old man. He went away with lagging steps, guided along that very road which they had walked that evening, now so long ago. He visited the inn at which he had stayed, and looked up at the window of his room. It was just the same. Nothing seemed to have changed: not even the fat landlord.

Christopher wandered into the coffee-room. Here he had seen the spy who had dogged their steps. He remembered, smiling a little, how excited he had been, and how placid he had found Roxhythe. My lord had been dozing in his chair; he had refused to be roused.

He tore himself away from the inn, knowing that it was foolishness to have come. It was with relief that he left Rotterdam behind.

===Chinese===

十年之后，克里斯托弗重返荷兰。那里的许多情形他已经淡忘，许多却唤回旧日记忆，历历宛在，痛切得很。他从哈里奇到鹿特丹来，在那城里只歇了一夜。他寻到亲王街 19 号（Prinsen Straat），指望再见到斯塔尔。谁知宅子已易他主，又听说斯塔尔四年之前便已长眠，这叫他心头猛地一沉。他自己都不曾料到，竟是这样想再见那位老人一面。他拖着滞重的步子离开，脚下不觉又走上那个晚上——如今已隔了那许多年——他们一同走过的那条路。他又去了当年下榻的客栈，抬头望了望自己住过的那间屋的窗。一切如旧。仿佛什么都不曾变过：连那胖掌柜也没有。

克里斯托弗信步踱进咖啡室。当年他就在这里见过那个一路钉他们梢的密探。他微微含笑，想起自己那时怎样的激动，而爵爷又何等的平静。爵爷正靠在椅中打盹，怎么也不肯让人唤醒。

他恋恋不舍地离开了客栈，心里明白此行原属痴愚。把鹿特丹抛在身后时，他倒觉出一阵轻松。

---

===Original===

The Hague seemed yet more packed with memories. The Poisson d’Or had changed no more than the inn at Rotterdam. Christopher looked up at the window, almost expecting to see Roxhythe standing there with the inevitable Milward at his elbow.

It was with an effort that he turned away. He had always remembered the Hague as a cheerful, happy town. Now it seemed dark, forlorn, a place of ghosts.

Roderick had grown kinder, and less harsh. He made no reference to Roxhythe. He was unfeignedly glad to see Christopher again; he wanted to present him to the Prince, but Christopher refused. Later he would go to the Huis ten Bosch, but for the present he wanted to be quiet.

===Chinese===

海牙更是满城都装着记忆。金鱼旅店同鹿特丹那家客栈一样，分毫未改。克里斯托弗抬头望向那扇窗，几乎指望能看见罗克斯海斯站在那儿，肘边照例伴着米尔沃德。

他费了好大一番气力才转过身去。记忆里的海牙，向来是一座欢快宜人的城。此刻却显得幽暗凄清，成了个幽灵出没之地。

罗德里克待弟弟比从前厚道了些，也不那么苛刻了。他绝口不提罗克斯海斯。重逢克里斯托弗，他是真心欢喜，要把他引见给亲王，克里斯托弗却辞谢了。豪斯登堡宫（Huis ten Bosch）他日后自会去，眼下他只想清静。

---

===Original===

He bought a horse shortly after his arrival and one day rode out to Scheveningen. The peaceful fishing-village took his fancy. Nearly every day he rode there, sometimes talking to the fishermen, mostly sitting by the sea alone, undisturbed by any uncouth sound, watching the silver-backed gulls swirling and diving against the intense blue sky.

Slowly the ache within him died, already it was less acute. Sitting on the shore, listening to the cry of the gulls and the continuous break of the waves on the sand, all that had passed during the last year seemed to fade away to a memory. It was no longer the never-ceasing pain; it was still there; it was still a great sadness, but it had softened and was not ever-present.

Bit by bit he began to take an interest in what went on around him. He watched the fishermen draw in their hauls, interested in the slippery, gleaming fish that floundered in the bottom of the net. Once he went out in a boat, helping the fishermen. He grew stronger, more virile, less morbid.

===Chinese===

到达后不久，他买了一匹马，一天骑到了斯赫弗宁恩（Scheveningen）。那座安宁静谧的渔村正合他的心意。此后他几乎天天骑马去那里，有时同渔夫闲谈，多半时候独自坐在海边，没有半点粗浊的声响来搅扰，只看银背的鸥鸟在湛蓝的天色里盘旋、俯冲。

他心头的创痛慢慢地钝了，早已不似先前那般尖锐。坐在岸边，听鸥鸟的啼叫，听海浪在沙滩上无休无止的碎响，过去一年间的种种，仿佛都淡成了一场旧忆。它已不再是那种无休无止的剧痛；痛还在那里；依然是深重的哀伤，只是磨得温软了些，不再须臾不离。

他渐渐对周遭的动静有了兴致。他看渔夫们收拢渔获，对网底里翻腾蹦跶的滑溜溜、亮闪闪的鱼看得出神。有一回他还随船出海，帮着渔夫们下力气。他渐渐强健起来，添了生气，褪了病态。

---

===Original===

Roderick seldom accompanied him to Scheveningen. Christopher did not wish it. His brother’s presence disturbed him, disturbed the great peace of the village. He would spend all the day there, rejoicing in the vast loneliness, feeling the rough spray on his face, and the wind blowing strongly about him. At sundown he would ride back to the Hague, tired and hungry. Sometimes he dined with Roderick, sometimes by himself.

After a while he went less often to Scheveningen. Desire for company was coming to life again. Roderick saw it, and introduced him to Mynheer Heenvliet and various other members of the Prince’s household. Other friends Christopher made for himself, all Dutchmen. One of these, Jan Van den Busch, showed him some of the countryside. Christopher visited Rijswijk and Loosduinen. It gave him a taste for sightseeing, and he went away for a time, travelling north. When he returned, Roderick was surprised at the change in him. His eyes were brighter, his bearing more brisk, his spirits lighter. His laugh still lacked its old spontaneity, his smile was not so joyous, but it came more often and less forcedly.

Roderick realized that Christopher had aged more in one year than in all the others of his life. He had no youthful illusions, no youthful impetuosity. He was calmer, more shrewd. He had a knowledge of men and of the world. Roderick realized that in some ways Christopher was older than he.

===Chinese===

罗德里克很少陪他去斯赫弗宁恩。克里斯托弗并不愿意他来。兄长在场总叫他心神不定，搅扰了村中那一派大安详。他常在那里消磨一整天，贪恋那浩瀚的孤寂，任粗粝的水沫扑上脸颊，任长风在周身劲吹。日落时分，他又乏又饿，骑马回海牙去。有时同罗德里克用饭，有时独自一人。

过了一些时候，他去斯赫弗宁恩的次数少了。与人为伴的愿望重新活了过来。罗德里克看在眼里，把他引见给海恩弗利特先生和亲王座下的其他几位人物。另一些朋友是克里斯托弗自己结交的，都是荷兰人。其中有个扬·范登博斯（Jan Van den Busch），带他去看了些乡野风光。克里斯托弗游了赖斯韦克（Rijswijk）和洛斯代嫩（Loosduinen）。这叫他尝到了游历的趣味，便出门小游，往北边去了一程。等他回来，罗德里克惊异地发觉他变了。眼睛亮了些，举止爽利了些，心绪也轻快了些。他的笑依旧不及当年自然，微笑也不似从前那般欢悦，可来得勤了，也不再那样勉强。

罗德里克意识到，克里斯托弗一年之间老成的程度，胜过此前生平所有的年月。少年人的幻梦他没有，少年人的血性他也没有。他沉静了，精明了。他识得人心，也识得世故。罗德里克意识到，论某种深浅，克里斯托弗比他还老成。

---

===Original===

Once again he broached the question of an introduction to the Prince. This time Christopher consented.

So one day the brothers rode out of the Hague through the wood that led to the Huis ten Bosch. Christopher was rather silent for the most part but when they reached the gardens of the palace he expressed his admiration.

“His Highness will be pleased to hear that you like his flowers,” said Roderick. “He takes great pride in them.”

===Chinese===

他又一次提起引见亲王的事。这一回，克里斯托弗应允了。

于是有一天，兄弟二人骑马出海牙，穿过那片通往豪斯登堡宫的树林。克里斯托弗一路大半沉默着，到了宫中花园，却称赏起来。

“殿下听说你喜欢他的花，必定高兴，”罗德里克说，“这些花是他极得意的东西。”

---

===Original===

“What does the Princess here?” asked Christopher suddenly. “I had not thought that the solitude was congenial to her.”

“At first Her Highness conceived herself very homesick. She is different now.”

“Poor Lady Mary! She was such a gay princess! She took such delight in the life at Whitehall.”

Roderick spoke stiffly.

===Chinese===

“王妃在这儿做什么？”克里斯托弗忽然问，“我原以为幽居的日子不合她的性情。”

“起初王妃殿下想家想得厉害。如今不同了。”

“可怜的玛丽小姐！她从前是那样快活的一位王妃！白厅的日子她过得多有滋味。”

罗德里克话说得生硬。

---

===Original===

“There is no need to pity her. She is the Prince his wife.”

“Poor lady!” said Christopher again. “I do pity her.”

“Her Highness should be happy enough,” replied his brother. “She has changed.”

The Prince was not in the house. One of the lackeys had seen him walking in the gardens not long since. They found him at length on a terrace, basking in the sunlight.

===Chinese===

“用不着可怜她。她是亲王的夫人。”

“可怜的夫人！”克里斯托弗又说，“我实在是可怜她。”

“王妃殿下总该够幸福的了，”他兄长答道，“她变了。”

亲王不在府里。有个仆役说，不久前还见他在园中散步。弟兄俩寻了半晌，才在一片露台上寻见他，正惬意地晒着太阳。

---

===Original===

Roderick swept him a low bow.

“Highness, I have brought my brother. May I present him?” It was a triumphant moment for him. At last Christopher had been brought face to face with this Prince whom he had affected to despise. At last he could show Christopher how wonderful was his master.

William turned. Christopher thought he had never met such a piercing scrutiny. Then the Prince smiled and rose.

===Chinese===

罗德里克深深打了一躬。

“殿下，我把舍弟带来了。可容我引见？”这一刻于他实在扬眉吐气。克里斯托弗终究被领到了这位他素来佯作鄙夷的亲王面前。他终于可以向克里斯托弗表明，自家主人是何等了得的人物。

威廉转过身来。克里斯托弗觉得，自己从未领教过这样锐利的审视。随即亲王微微一笑，站起身。

---

===Original===

“I have long desired to make your acquaintance, Mr. Dart. Rodrigue has often spoken to me of you.” He extended his hand.

Christopher went on one knee to kiss it.

“Your Highness is very kind,” he said.

===Chinese===

“久已想结识你了，达特先生。罗德里格常常向我谈起你。”他伸出手来。

克里斯托弗单膝跪下，吻了吻那只手。

“殿下太客气了，”他说。

---

===Original===

“Christopher greatly admires the gardens, Sir,” said Roderick.

William looked pleased.

“They are beautiful!” said Christopher warmly. “I do indeed admire them, Sir.”

“I love them,” answered the Prince. “I could not live without my flowers.” He waved his hand towards a bed of tulips. “Those are my flowers.”

===Chinese===

“克里斯托弗把园子夸个不停呢，殿下，”罗德里克说。

威廉面有喜色。

“美极了！”克里斯托弗热诚地说，“我是真心赞叹，殿下。”

“我疼爱这些花，”亲王答道，“离了我的花，我可活不成。”他朝一畦郁金香挥了挥手。“那些便是我的花。”

---

===Original===

Christopher smiled.

“Even we in England know which are your favourites, Sir!”

“So? They are my favourites because they are part of Holland. You do not grow such flowers in England.”

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗微微一笑。

“连我们英格兰人都知道您的花中至爱是哪一样，殿下！”

“哦？它们是我的至爱，因为它们是荷兰的一部分。这样的花，你们英格兰种不出来。”

---

===Original===

“No,” said Christopher. “But we have our roses.”

“Yes, you have your roses. Rodrigue, he must be shown the west side!”

So the Prince of Orange showed Mr. Dart the west side of his gardens.

===Chinese===

“种不出来，”克里斯托弗说，“可我们有我们的玫瑰。”

“不错，你们有你们的玫瑰。罗德里格，一定得领他看看西边！”

于是奥兰治亲王领着达特先生，把花园的西边看了一遍。

---

===Original===

The visit to the Huis ten Bosch was the first of many. Christopher made more friends in the Prince’s household, and the Princess desired his acquaintance.

He hardly recognized the Lady Mary in the quiet, soberly dressed woman to whom he was presented. The Mary he knew had sparkling eyes and a roguish smile. The eyes were calm now, almost sad; the smile was full of dignity. He thought that she seemed unhappy, and later he found that there was a coldness between the Prince and his wife.

Mary was anxious to hear all the London news. More than once Christopher saw her eyes fill, but the tears did not well over. He could not tell her much, but she was grateful for very little. She asked after various people, lingering over their names as over a pleasant memory. Christopher pitied her from the bottom of his heart.

===Chinese===

豪斯登堡宫之行是头一遭，此后常来常往。克里斯托弗在亲王府中又结交了几位朋友，王妃也愿意结识他。

被人引见时，他几乎认不出眼前这位衣着素净、神色安静的妇人便是玛丽小姐。他记忆里的玛丽，眼波流转，笑意顽皮。如今那双眼睛沉静了，近乎忧伤；那笑容端凝持重。他觉得她似乎并不快活，后来又发觉，亲王夫妇之间存着一段冰冷的距离。

玛丽急切地想听伦敦的种种新闻。不止一回，克里斯托弗看见她眼里蓄满了泪，却始终没有涌出来。他能讲的并不多，她却点滴都领情。她问起这个那个人，把那些名字久久含在口边，像含着一段甜美的旧忆。克里斯托弗打心底里怜惜她。

---

===Original===

Desire for work was growing apace. The indolent life he was leading had become irksome. Christopher realized that he must find some occupation.

The more he saw of William, the more convinced he was that he had found one who was honest and a patriot. He watched the Stadtholder’s adroit management of affairs with growing admiration.

Roderick was surprised that his brother did not fall at the Prince’s feet, worshipping. He was still more surprised that Christopher should feel no desire to become one of the Prince’s household. He could not understand that all Christopher’s love remained with Roxhythe.

===Chinese===

做事的愿望飞快地滋长。眼前这怠惰闲散的日子，渐渐叫他腻烦了。克里斯托弗明白，他必须寻一份营生。

威廉见得越多，他越深信自己找到了一个诚实的人、一个爱国者。他眼看执政娴熟地斡旋诸事，敬服与日俱增。

叫他吃惊的是，弟弟竟没有拜倒在亲王脚下顶礼膜拜。更叫他吃惊的是，克里斯托弗竟毫无进亲王府中当差的意思。他不明白，克里斯托弗全部的爱，依然系在罗克斯海斯身上。

---

===Original===

“You do not desire to be near the Prince his person?”

“I would rather join his army,” answered Christopher.

“Join the army! You had better enter his household.”

===Chinese===

“你不愿意在亲王本人跟前当差？”

“我宁愿去投他的军队，”克里斯托弗答。

“投军！你不如进他的内府。”

---

===Original===

“I do not wish to serve any man﻿—personally.”

“Odds life! Not even His Highness?”

“No one.”

Roderick stared.

===Chinese===

“我不想侍奉任何人——贴身侍奉。”

“上帝的生命！连殿下也不例外？”

“谁也不侍奉。”

罗德里克瞪直了眼。

---

===Original===

“You are no soldier, Chris!”

“I can learn.”

“You were better advised to turn your hand to politics.”

===Chinese===

“你可不是当兵的坯子，克里斯！”

“我可以学。”

“依我说，你还不如把心思转到政治上去。”

---

===Original===

“Never! I want not to hear the word again!”

“But that is ridiculous! Because you found Shaftesbury acting questionably is no reason to think that﻿—”

“I will not hear of politics. They mean intrigue and covert dealing; bribing and tricking. I’ll none of it.”

===Chinese===

“决不！这个字眼我不要再听见！”

“可这就荒唐了！你见沙夫茨伯里行止可疑，总不能由此便认定——”

“政治二字我不要听。政治便是阴谋诡计、暗地里的勾当；便是行贿使诈。我决计不沾。”

---

===Original===

Roderick shook his head in amazement. But he broached the subject to his master.

Thus it came about that Christopher had audience with the Prince one sunny morning at the Huis ten Bosch.

William sat at his desk, chin in hand. He regarded Christopher thoughtfully for some moments.

“Rodrigue has been speaking to me of you, Mr. Dart. You desire to serve under my standard?” He spoke in Dutch.

===Chinese===

罗德里克愕然摇头。可他还是把这事对主人提了。

于是，一个晴朗的早晨，克里斯托弗在豪斯登堡宫谒见了亲王。

威廉坐在书案前，一手托着下巴。他凝神端详了克里斯托弗半晌。

“罗德里格向我谈起过你，达特先生。你愿在我的旗下效力？”他说的是荷兰语。

---

===Original===

“If your Highness permits, I ask nothing better.”

Christopher saw the hazel eyes twinkle suddenly.

“Yet if I offer you a post about my person you will refuse it?”

===Chinese===

“只要殿下恩准，我别无他求。”

克里斯托弗看见那双榛色的眼睛倏地一亮。

“可若是我赏你一个贴身的差事，你会推掉？”

---

===Original===

There was something disconcerting about the Prince. Christopher stammered a little.

“I hope﻿—Your Highness﻿—will not﻿—offer that.”

“Sit down,” said William. “Why do you hope that?”

“Sir, I desire to serve no man personally. I want to serve as a soldier.”

===Chinese===

这位亲王自有叫人心慌的地方。克里斯托弗说话结巴起来。

“我希望——殿下——不会——开这个口。”

“坐下，”威廉说，“为什么希望我不开这个口？”

“殿下，我不想贴身侍奉任何人。我要以军人的身份效力。”

---

===Original===

William smiled.

“You want to serve an ideal, *hein*?”

“Perhaps that is true, Sir. I do not want to serve﻿—a man.”

“Are you afraid that I should betray your trust?”

===Chinese===

威廉微微一笑。

“你想效忠的是一个理想，*hein*？”

“殿下，或许正是如此。我不想效忠——一个人。”

“你是怕我把你的信赖辜负了？”

---

===Original===

The swiftness of the attack got behind Christopher’s guard.

“I﻿—don’t understand, Sir.”

“I think you do. Am I the man to betray a trust?”

“No, Sir. Why do you ask?”

===Chinese===

这一击来得太快，直绕到克里斯托弗的防线背后。

“我——不明白，殿下。”

“我看你明白。我可是那种辜负信赖的人？”

“不是，殿下。您为何这样问？”

---

===Original===

“Because I will not be served by any man who does not place in me his whole confidence.”

Christopher hesitated.

“I believe that you at least are honest, Sir,” he said at last.

“But you will not accept a post about my person.”

===Chinese===

“因为谁若不能把全副信赖交托给我，我便不要他侍奉。”

克里斯托弗踌躇了。

“至少我相信您是诚实的，殿下，”他终于说。

“可你不肯接受贴身的差事。”

---

===Original===

Christopher grew hot under the steady scrutiny.

“No, Sir.”

“Why not?”

===Chinese===

在那纹丝不动的逼视下，克里斯托弗脸上发起烫来。

“不肯，殿下。”

“为什么不肯？”

---

===Original===

For a moment Christopher did not answer.

“Highness, if you must know, it is this:﻿—For nine years I have served my Lord Roxhythe. He has all my love, all my devotion. I cannot serve another man in the same way. I have tried and failed. Twice failed.”

“Mr. Dart, you say that Roxhyt’e has your love and your devotion. What then have you to offer me?”

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗半晌没有作声。

“殿下，您既然非知道不可，我便直说：——九年来，我侍奉的是罗克斯海斯爵爷。他占有我全部的爱、全部的赤诚。换一个人，我无法这样侍奉。我试过，失败了。两回都失败了。”

“达特先生，你说罗克斯泰占着你的爱与赤诚。那么你还拿什么给我？”

---

===Original===

“Faithful service, Sir, and loyalty.”

“So!” William tapped his fingers lightly on a sheet of parchment. His face grew harsh. “We will have plain speaking, if you please, Mr. Dart. I have some knowledge of milor’ Roxhyt’e’s life, and of his dealings. How am I to know that you are not imbued with his morals﻿—or lack of morals?”

Christopher straightened.

===Chinese===

“忠实的效力，殿下，还有忠诚。”

“是这样！”威廉的手指在一张羊皮纸上轻轻叩着。脸色沉了下来。“咱们就打开天窗说亮话吧，达特先生。米洛·罗克斯泰的为人行止，我略知一二。教我怎么知道，你没有浸透了他的德行——或者说，他的无德？”

克里斯托弗把腰板挺直了。

---

===Original===

“Highness, may we leave Roxhythe out of the discussion? I trust my morals are above reproach.”

“I trust so, Mr. Dart. Yet in ’77 there was some question of that.”

“Will your Highness explain?”

===Chinese===

“殿下，这场谈话可否不提罗克斯海斯？我相信我的德行无可指摘。”

“我也愿意相信，达特先生。可 77 年，你在这上头原惹过疑心。”

“请殿下明示。”

---

===Original===

“It is necessary? In ’77 you bore letters to Cherrywood; letters that we believe to have been addressed to the French King from King Charles. You will admit that gives food for thought, Mr. Dart.”

Christopher met his eyes bravely.

“I give Your Highness my word that if that was so I knew nothing of it when I did take the letters. I thought them innocent dispatches to His Grace of Monmouth.”

===Chinese===

“有必要么？77 年，你给切里伍德捎过信；我们相信，那是查理国王写给法王的信。达特先生，你总得承认，这够人寻思一番的吧。”

克里斯托弗坦然迎住他的目光。

“我向殿下担保：即便实情如此，我接信的时候也毫不知情。我只当那是给蒙茅斯公爵大人的寻常公文。”

---

===Original===

“So your brother assures me. He tells me you were tricked. It is because you fear that I might trick you that you will not enter my﻿—personal﻿—service?”

“N-no, Sir. I think not. It is because I could not serve you with wholehearted affection. It is true that I have grown suspicious of late, but I believe that I do trust Your Highness.”

“Thank you. That is your only reason?”

===Chinese===

“你兄长也是这样向我担保的。他对我说，你是受了骗。你所以不肯进我——贴身——的门下，就是因为怕我也许会骗你？”

“不——不是的，殿下。我想不是。是因为我无法全心全意地侍奉您。近来我确实变得多疑了，可我相信，殿下我是信得过的。”

“多谢。这就是你唯一的缘由？”

---

===Original===

Again Christopher hesitated.

“No, Sir, there is another. However honest you be there must always be intrigue. I desire to know nothing of intrigue. I want to﻿—be outside all the inner workings of politics. I want to﻿—forget everything.”

William coughed a little.

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗又踌躇了。

“不，殿下，还有一个。您再诚实，阴谋总是免不了的。阴谋的事，我一概不想知道。我要——站到政治这一切内情的外头去。我要——忘掉一切。”

威廉轻轻咳了两声。

---

===Original===

“Then I think you would be better advised to seek employment with someone who lives not a public life.”

“I have thought of that, Sir, but it does not appeal to me.”

“In truth, Mr. Dart, you do not know what you want.”

===Chinese===

“那么依我看，你还是另投一位不抛头露面的主家当差，更明智些。”

“这个我想过了，殿下，可我提不起兴致。”

“说实在的，达特先生，你自己也不知道要什么。”

---

===Original===

“Indeed, Sir, I do! I want to fight France﻿—our common enemy.”

“I do not fight France. There is peace.”

Christopher looked at him strangely.

“Your Highness will not always be content with this peace.”

===Chinese===

“我知道的，殿下！我要打法国——打咱们共同的敌人。”

“我不打法国。现在是和局。”

克里斯托弗怪异地望着他。

“这样的和局，殿下不会永远安于的。”

---

===Original===

William’s brows rose perceptibly.

“Oh! So you will join the army﻿—and wait? Dull work, Mr. Dart.”

“I have yet to learn a soldier’s duties, Sir.”

===Chinese===

威廉的眉毛分明挑了一挑。

“哦！这么说，你要去投军——然后等着？闷差事啊，达特先生。”

“军人的职分，我正待从头学起呢，殿下。”

---

===Original===

The Prince tapped the parchment again.

“Well.﻿ ﻿… Perhaps I can find employment for you. We shall see.”

He took up a quill. For some moments he wrote swiftly. Then he dusted the parchment and folded it.

===Chinese===

亲王又叩了叩那张羊皮纸。

“好吧。……或许我能给你安插个差事。再看吧。”

他提起一支鹅毛笔，唰唰写了一阵。随后撒上细砂吸干墨迹，把羊皮纸折起。

---

===Original===

“You are quite sure that you wish to serve the Orange? Remember that you are an Englishman; remember that once you have entered the army you cannot leave it at will.”

“I have considered all that, Sir. It is no hasty decision that I have made. I have thought long, and﻿—pardon me﻿—I have observed Your Highness closely. I believe that at last I have found a master who is above bribes; who does not work for himself but for his country.”

William bowed.

“I admire plain speaking, sir. In my turn I believe that you too are honest. I doubted it at one time, but when I was told that you had quitted Lord Roxhyt’e I concluded that I was wrong.”

===Chinese===

“你当真拿得稳，要效忠奥兰治家？记住，你是英格兰人；记住，一入了军伍，可不能想走就走。”

“这些都思量过了，殿下。我这并不是仓促的决定。我想了很久，而且——恕我直言——我也一直细察着殿下的为人。我相信，我到底寻到了一位贿赂买不动的主人；他不为自己谋，而为他的国家谋。”

威廉欠了欠身。

“我喜欢直话直说，先生。那么换过来，我也相信你是诚实的。我从前疑心过，可一听说你离开了罗克斯泰爵爷，我便断定自己看错了。”

---

===Original===

“Thank you, Sir. I may enter your service?”

William handed him the parchment.

“You will convey that to Bentinck. Rodrigue will direct you. Bentinck will give you a commission, and it will remain for you to prove yourself.”

Christopher went down on one knee.

===Chinese===

“多谢殿下。我可以投效您了？”

威廉把羊皮纸递给他。

“你把这个送到宾廷克那里去。罗德里格会给你引路。委任状由宾廷克发给你，往后就得看你自己争气了。”

克里斯托弗单膝跪下。

---

===Original===

“I will serve Your Highness faithfully,” he said. “I have to thank you for your kindness.”

William held out his hand.

“That is very well,” he said.

===Chinese===

“我必忠心耿耿侍奉殿下，”他说，“多谢您的恩典。”

威廉伸出手来。

“那就很好，”他说。

---

===Original===

Christopher went quietly out of the room. Roderick was awaiting him, all eagerness.

“Well, Chris?”

“I am to go to General Bentinck.”

Roderick was disappointed.

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗悄悄退出了房间。罗德里克正眼巴巴地等着他。

“怎么样，克里斯？”

“我去宾廷克将军麾下。”

罗德里克好生失望。

---

===Original===

“I had thought﻿—that when you had spoken with His Highness﻿—you would have desired to be always at his side.”

Christopher smiled faintly.

“I am no longer twenty-one, Dick. The age of illusions is past.”

“Have you no enthusiasms?”

===Chinese===

“我还以为——等你同殿下谈过之后——你便会想永远待在他身边了。”

克里斯托弗淡淡一笑。

“我不是二十一岁了，迪克。幻梦的年纪过去了。”

“你就没有半点热忱了么？”

---

===Original===

Christopher sighed.

“I hardly know. Perhaps. But not for man.”

“I don’t understand you, Chris. I had thought that the Prince would have captured your devotion.”

“You have yet to realize, Dick, that my devotion lies elsewhere.”

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗叹了口气。

“我也说不清。或许有。但决不是为了人。”

“我懂不了你，克里斯。我还当亲王会赢得你的赤诚呢。”

“你还没明白过来，迪克，我的赤诚在别处。”

---

===Original===

“Still?” Roderick was incredulous.

“Always.”

“But after all that has happened! after his treatment of you﻿—”

“If you think that any harm done to me could kill my love for Roxhythe, you do not understand love.”

===Chinese===

“还在？”罗德里克难以置信。

“永远在。”

“可经过这一切之后！他那样待你——”

“你若以为什么伤害都能扼死我对罗克斯海斯的爱，那你就是不懂得爱。”

---

===Original===

“You are infatuated! Pray heaven it will pass!”

“For my peace of mind I hope it will. You’ll dine with me tonight? I shall leave the Hague early tomorrow.”

“As soon as that? Yes, I’ll dine with you. I wish you were to be of the Household, though. I wanted you near me after all these years.”

===Chinese===

“你这是鬼迷心窍！但愿上天叫它快些过去！”

“为了我心神安宁，我也巴望它过去。今晚同我一起用饭？明天一早我就离开海牙。”

“这么快？好，我陪你用饭。只是你要是能进王府就好了。这些年过去，我原想把你留在身边的。”

---

===Original===

Christopher spoke rather cynically.

“No, Roderick. You had been jealous of me an I had joined the Prince his Household.”

“Really, Christopher!” Roderick was inclined to be offended. Then he smiled. “Perhaps you are right. But I shall miss you.”

“It will pass,” replied Christopher easily.

===Chinese===

克里斯托弗答话时颇带了些讥诮。

“不，罗德里克。倘使我进了亲王的内府，你早嫉妒上我了。”

“真是的，克里斯托弗！”罗德里克险些恼了。随即又笑了。“或许你说得对。可我会想你的。”

“会过去的，”克里斯托弗轻描淡写地说。

---

===Original===

Roderick went back to his master.

“Well, Rodrigue? You have seen your brother?”

“Yes, Sir. I cannot understand his attitude.”

“No?”

===Chinese===

罗德里克回到主人那里。

“怎么样，罗德里格？见着你兄弟了？”

“见了，殿下。他的心思我弄不懂。”

“哦？”

---

===Original===

“He is so cold! so unlike his old self.”

“My dear Rodrigue, your brother has suffered. I understand him.”

“But then, Highness, you understand all men,” said Roderick softly.

===Chinese===

“他那么冷！全然不是从前的他了。”

“我亲爱的罗德里格，你兄弟受过苦。我懂得他。”

“可话又说回来，殿下，世人您是个个都懂得的，”罗德里克轻声说。

## Book
 V The Other Part

## Book V The Other Part / 第五部 其中之另一

## I The Triple Game / 三重博弈

===Original===

“Trouble, trouble, naught but trouble!” Charles flung out his hands hopelessly. “Shaftesbury, Russell, Cavendish! What is to be done?”

Roxhythe smiled.

“Do you despair, Sir?”

“Do I ever despair? But this combination means endless toil, endless dissension. Shaftesbury is mine enemy.”

“To counteract Shaftesbury you have Sunderland.”

“Whom I would not trust.”

===Chinese===

“麻烦，麻烦，除了麻烦还是麻烦！”查理无可奈何地摊开两手。“沙夫茨伯里、罗素、卡文迪什！这可怎么办？”

罗克斯海斯微微一笑。

“陛下这是灰心了？”

“我几时灰心过？只是这么一伙人凑在一处，往后有的是操不完的心、平不完的争执。沙夫茨伯里是我的对头。”

“要牵制沙夫茨伯里，陛下还有桑德兰（Sunderland）。”

“那人我信不过。”

---

===Original===

“Nevertheless he may prove useful. And there is Halifax.”

“He blows hot and cold.”

“But mostly cold.”

“What do you mean, David?”

“I wonder that you have not observed Halifax more closely, Sir. When the greater party blows hot, he blows cold. You’ll find him opposed to Shaftesbury.”

“It may be so. You think he’ll support me?”

===Chinese===

“话虽如此，他兴许还有几分用处。何况还有哈利法克斯。”

“那人忽冷忽热。”

“冷的时候居多。”

“你这是什么意思，大卫？”

“臣只是奇怪，陛下怎么不曾把哈利法克斯看得更仔细些。哪一派人多得势、吹得正热，他便偏要吹冷风。您会发现他同沙夫茨伯里是对着干的。”

“或许是这样。你以为他会支持我？”

---

===Original===

“If you are the losing side, Sir, yes. If you are the stronger he will not matter.”

“True. But that will not help us now. I see trouble stirring for James. The people wax unruly.”

“His Grace acts very imprudently. You would be wise to remove him, Sir. While he remains in England the Protestant cause will keep fresh in England’s mind.”

“Remove him﻿ ﻿… ay, but where?”

“Does it signify? Send him where he cannot stir up agitation by his foolish behaviour.”

Charles sat up.

===Chinese===

“倘若您是势弱的一方，是的，陛下。倘若您占着上风，他便无足轻重。”

“不错。可眼下这话于事无补。我瞧得出来，有人要跟詹姆斯过不去了。民心正闹得不安分。”

“殿下行事实在太欠斟酌。陛下若肯把他打发开，方是明智。只要他还留在英格兰，新教这桩事在英格兰人心里就一日新鲜一日。”

“打发开……唔，打发到哪儿去？”

“那有什么要紧？只消打发到他那点儿蠢行搅不起风浪的地方去便是。”

查理坐直了身子。

---

===Original===

“I believe you are right, David. I’ll send him to Brussels.”

“It will suffice. At least he will be out of harm’s way.”

“Yes. But I do not think he will thank me.”

“Perhaps not.”

“He’ll be greatly incensed. It may be that he will suspect your hand in the matter.”

“Probably. It does not worry me.”

===Chinese===

“我想你是对的，大卫。我打发他到布鲁塞尔（Brussels）去。”

“那便尽够了。至少他远离是非之地。”

“是啊。只是想来他不会领我的情。”

“或许不会。”

“他准要大为光火。说不定还会疑心这事里有你的手笔。”

“多半会的。我不放在心上。”

---

===Original===

Charles stroked one of his dogs reflectively.

“Do you think that by doing this I shall avert the storm against his succession? I do not.”

“No, Sir. You will modify it.”

“It will still mean a fight. Shaftesbury is determined to exclude him.”

“Sire, most men are determined. Nearly all your new ministers are at one on the question. But I think that there will be dissension.”

“Why?”

===Chinese===

查理若有所思，抚着爱犬里的一只。

“你以为这么一办，就能替他挡开冲击他继承大统的那场风暴么？我看不能。”

“不能，陛下。但您能叫它缓和几分。”

“终究少不了一场争斗。沙夫茨伯里铁了心要把他摈除在外。”

“陛下，铁了心的人多着呢。您新近任用的大臣们，在这桩事上几乎众口一词。可臣想，他们中间自会生出分歧来。”

“为什么？”

---

===Original===

“They will not all want the same successor.”

“You think some will stand for Monmouth?”

“I do expect it, Sir. Prince William is not every man’s choice.”

“No. And Monmouth is popular. He would be the people’s choice, but I cannot believe that the Cabinet would consent to it.”

“We shall see. In the meantime, Sir, I propose to act.”

Charles leaned back in his chair.

===Chinese===

“他们属意的继承人未必是同一个。”

“你以为会有人拥立蒙茅斯？”

“臣确实作如是想，陛下。威廉亲王可不是人人都中意的人选。”

“可不是。何况蒙茅斯得人心。民意属意于他，可我不能相信内阁会点头应允。”

“走着瞧吧。眼下呢，陛下，臣打算先动起来。”

查理往椅背上一靠。

---

===Original===

“I were not King without you, Davy. You’ll help me to overthrow the coming cry for exclusion?”

“I will.”

The King looked at him curiously for a moment.

“Roxhythe, what are your own sentiments?”

“I’ve none. I care not what happens after you are gone. England may have James, or Mary, or Monmouth. It is all one to me. All that matters is your pleasure.”

“I would I had more of your mind about me! What do you think of doing?”

===Chinese===

“没有你，我这国王做不成，达维。你肯帮我压下去那阵将要起来的排斥风潮？”

“臣肯。”

国王好奇地打量了他片刻。

“罗克斯海斯，你自己心里是怎么个想法？”

“臣没有想法。陛下百年之后的事，臣一概不放在心上。英格兰交给詹姆斯也好，玛丽也好，蒙茅斯也好，于臣都是一个样。要紧的只有陛下的心意。”

“我身边要是多几个你这样心思的人就好了！你打算怎么办？”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe sat down on the nearest chair.

“I shall throw myself into the cause against His Grace of York. Secretly.”

The King’s brow contracted in bewilderment.

“Go on.”

“His Grace of York’s dislike for me is well known. That adds colour to my attitude. I approach Shaftesbury when the time comes, with great caution. I am a thought fearful of discovery, you understand. I think that it were best for me to act secretly for fear of incurring Your Majesty’s displeasure.”

“I do not think that they will trust you.”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯就近拣了把椅子坐下。

“臣要投身进去，为反对约克公爵殿下的一派奔走。暗地里。”

国王困惑地蹙起眉头。

“说下去。”

“约克公爵殿下厌恶臣，这是人所共知的。这倒给臣的举动添了几分真切的颜色。到时候臣去接近沙夫茨伯里，须得十二分的小心。臣略微怕事情败露，您明白的。臣想，臣还是暗中行事为妙，免得惹陛下不快。”

“我看他们未必信得过你。”

---

===Original===

“They will undoubtedly have misgivings. But my support in the matter would be invaluable. They would count on my exerting my influence to sway you ’gainst the Duke.”

“Ay, but what then?”

“When I have convinced the worthy Shaftesbury of my wholehearted sincerity I shall enter deep into the inner workings of the affair.”

“Which you will impart to me?”

“Which I shall impart to you. I think I may be instrumental in bringing about the fall of our friend Ashley.”

“You are clever enough for anything,” admitted Charles. “But this is a big risk.”

===Chinese===

“他们无疑会满腹狐疑。可臣在这桩事里的助力，对他们来说是无价之宝。他们会指望着臣施展影响，把您说动来反对公爵。”

“唔，然后呢？”

“等臣让那位可敬的沙夫茨伯里信实了臣的一片赤诚，臣便能深入这桩事的堂奥了。”

“内里的动静，你会说给我听？”

“臣自会一五一十说与您听。臣想，咱们那位朋友阿什利的倒台，臣或可助一臂之力。”

“你这颗脑袋，什么鬼主意都转得出来，”查理承认道。“可这一注风险不小。”

---

===Original===

“No. They can but disbelieve in me, and I do not think they will do that. They will see that if the Duke succeeds you I must fall. It is the popular belief that I work primarily for my own ends.”

Charles nodded.

“If all this should come to James his ears you are ruined﻿—when I die, my David.”

“That matters not at all, Sir.”

“I might confide in James.﻿ ﻿…”

“I beg you will not, Sir! He is so incautious. And he mistrusts me. He would not believe that I was working in his interests.”

===Chinese===

“算不得。他们至多不信臣罢了，而臣想他们不至于。他们会明白，公爵若承继了大统，臣必然失势。世人本就认定，臣行事先为自家打算。”

查理点了点头。

“这番把戏若传进詹姆斯耳朵里，你就完了——等到我百年之后，我的大卫。”

“那全不相干，陛下。”

“我也可以把话对詹姆斯交个底……”

“求陛下万万不要，陛下！他那嘴上没有把门的。何况他本就疑忌臣。他断不肯相信臣是替他出力。”

---

===Original===

“I do not suppose he would. Especially if he guesses by whose advice he is sent to Brussels.”

“He’ll guess that, of course. He suspects my hand in everything. His mistrust will but further my machinations.”

“Very well, Roxhythe, I consent.”

The favourite laughed.

“Did you mean to withhold your consent, Sir?”

“I’ve no wish to ruin you, David.”

===Chinese===

“想来他也不会信。尤其是他若猜出，把他打发去布鲁塞尔是听了谁的主意。”

“那个他自然猜得着。他事事都疑心有臣的手笔。他的猜忌，只会替臣的算计再添一把火。”

“好罢，罗克斯海斯，我准了。”

宠臣朗声一笑。

“陛下原本还打算不准么？”

“我并不想毁了你，大卫。”

---

===Original===

“Why, I am ruined already. What happens after your death is no matter at all.”

“Well, I do not think I shall die yet,” said Charles placidly.

===Chinese===

“嗳，臣早就毁了呀。陛下身后的事，根本无关紧要。”

“这个么，我想我还死不了呢，”查理心平气和地说。

---

===Original===

After welcoming the new Parliament with wild enthusiasm, England settled down to enjoy a panic concerning Papists and Papist heirs. This panic my Lord Shaftesbury fostered lovingly. He was a brave man, but the rest of the Council were not. They hesitated at bringing in an Exclusion Bill. But they agitated with the rest.

For a short space Shaftesbury supported the King’s suggested Bill of Securities, but he decided at last that it was not strong enough, and laid it aside. He prevailed upon the Council to bring in a Bill excluding James from the throne and devolving it upon the next Protestant heir. The Commons liked the Bill, and passed it. My Lord Shaftesbury anticipated trouble in the other House, and he instructed the Commons to prepare a Remonstrance.

Charles deemed it prudent to prorogue his Parliament.

The trouble fermented. My Lord Shaftesbury held meetings and discussions. So did my Lords Halifax, Essex, and Sir William Temple, the Secretary of State. Into these meetings was introduced the magic name of Roxhythe.

===Chinese===

英格兰以狂热的劲头欢迎过新一届议会之后，便安顿下来，消受起一桩有关天主教徒与天主教继承人的恐慌来。这场恐慌，沙夫茨伯里勋爵怜爱有加，蓄养得肥壮日盛。他是个有胆色的人，枢密院（the Council）里其余诸公却不是。提出一纸排斥法案（Exclusion Bill），他们迟疑不决。但旁人鼓噪，他们也跟着鼓噪。

一时之间，沙夫茨伯里也支持过国王提议的《担保法案》（Bill of Securities），但末了他认定这法案力度不够，把它撇在了一边。他说动枢密院提出一纸法案，将詹姆斯摈于王位之外，把王位传给下一位新教继承人。下议院喜欢这法案，予以通过。沙夫茨伯里勋爵料定上议院那一头不会太平，便授意下议院拟具一纸陈情书（Remonstrance）。

查理认为，让议会休会才是稳妥之计。

风潮愈酿愈烈。沙夫茨伯里勋爵频频集会、商议。哈利法克斯勋爵、埃塞克斯勋爵（Lord Essex），还有国务大臣威廉·坦普尔爵士（Sir William Temple），也都如是。这些集会之中，渐渐掺进了一个有魔力的名字——罗克斯海斯。

---

===Original===

Lord Holles mentioned my lord first. He was dining with Shaftesbury.

“I believe I have set my finger on a weak spot in the King’s armour,” he remarked. He peeled a nut, and ate it.

The Earl was all attention.

“What have you discovered, Holles?”

Holles ate another nut.

“I have reason to think that his favourite stands against him.”

===Chinese===

头一个提起爵爷的，是霍利斯勋爵。他正同沙夫茨伯里用饭。

“我想我算是摸着了国王甲胄上的一处软肋，”他说着，剥了一颗榛子，吃了。

伯爵立时凝神细听。

“你发现了什么，霍利斯？”

霍利斯又吃了一颗榛子。

“我有由头认定，他的那位宠臣正站在他的对面。”

---

===Original===

“Roxhythe? Impossible!”

“On the contrary. If you think for a moment you will see that it is more than probable.”

“You think that Roxhythe realizes that the accession of James would be his downfall?”

“Well, he is no fool.”

Shaftesbury pushed back his chair, frowning.

“I would never trust Roxhythe.”

===Chinese===

“罗克斯海斯？决无此事！”

“恰恰相反。你略想一想，便知这事再可能不过了。”

“你是说，罗克斯海斯心里明白，詹姆斯一朝继位，他就要垮台？”

“呃，他可不是傻子。”

沙夫茨伯里把椅子往后一推，眉头紧锁。

“罗克斯海斯这人，我是断断不肯信的。”

---

===Original===

“Except when he works for himself.”

“Less than ever then.”

“I disagree. I discern signs of uneasiness in my lord.”

“I can’t believe that Roxhythe would ever betray his feelings.”

“They were very slight signs, I admit. I fancy he is working for the exclusion.”

Shaftesbury sat biting his nail, his face in worried lines.

===Chinese===

“除非他为自己打算的时候。”

“那时候更信不得。”

“我不以为然。我在爵爷身上看出几分不安的苗头。”

“我不信罗克斯海斯会让自己的心思露出来。”

“苗头确实极细微，这我承认。我猜想他是在为排斥案出力。”

沙夫茨伯里坐着咬指甲，满脸愁纹。

---

===Original===

“If it were so it would help the cause more than anything else.”

“So I think. I know that he dined with Savile twice last week.”

“With Halifax! That means he favours the accession of Mary!”

“It is more likely that he has not thought of Monmouth. Monmouth should be more to his taste.”

“Holles, I wish that I might be sure of this! If one could trust him he would be invaluable. He has so much influence.”

“Why not sound him?”

===Chinese===

“果真如此，那对咱们这桩事业可是天大的助力。”

“我也是这么想。我知道上礼拜他同萨维尔（Savile）吃过两回饭。”

“跟哈利法克斯！那就是说他属意玛丽继位！”

“多半是他还没想过蒙茅斯。蒙茅斯才更对他的口味。”

“霍利斯，但愿我能拿得准这个！倘使信得过他，他便是无价之宝。他的影响实在太大了。”

“何不探一探他的口风？”

---

===Original===

“How?”

“Invite him to dinner.”

“Quite impossible. I do not visit him.”

“Then let me. I’ll also invite you.”

Ashley bit his nail again, irresolute.

“If he would come﻿—”

===Chinese===

“怎么探？”

“请他吃顿饭。”

“断然不行。我同他没有往来。”

“那便由我来。连你一并请上。”

阿什利又咬起指甲，拿不定主意。

“他若肯来——”

---

===Original===

“Oh, he will come! He often dines with me.”

“I do not think that he would ever work for a party.”

“It remains to be seen. It is just possible that our great Roxhythe is a little apprehensive.”

===Chinese===

“嗳，他肯来的！他常在我家吃饭。”

“我看他断不肯为哪一派出力。”

“那要走着瞧。也未可知咱们那位了不起的罗克斯海斯，正有几分忐忑呢。”

---

===Original===

Two days later Roxhythe exhibited a letter to his master.

“I am bidden to Holles tomorrow, Sir.”

“Really?” Charles took the letter. “How amiable he is! They mean to probe you, David.”

My lord smiled serenely. He accepted the invitation.

During dinner at Lord Holles’ house he excelled himself. He talked on every subject but one, and that one politics; witticisms flowed from his tongue, and if they annoyed Shaftesbury, they delighted his host.

When the servants had at last left the room, Lord Holles filled up the glasses, and, not without regret, brought the conversation round to home affairs. He began cautiously, for Ashley had implored him to be very circumspect in what he said before Roxhythe. He leaned back in his chair, tilting it slightly.

===Chinese===

两天之后，罗克斯海斯把一封信呈给他的主子看。

“霍利斯下帖请臣明天过去，陛下。”

“是么？”查理接过信。“他可真和气！他们这是要探你的底，大卫。”

爵爷安然一笑，应下了这份请帖。

在霍利斯勋爵府上的席间，他妙语如珠，平生所学发挥得淋漓尽致。什么话头他都谈，唯独政治一桩避而不谈；隽语妙言从舌底滔滔而出，若说惹恼了沙夫茨伯里，却也逗乐了主人。

待仆人们终于退出饭厅，霍利斯勋爵把各人的杯子斟满，怀着几分不舍，把话头引向国内政务上去。他开口小心翼翼，因为阿什利再三恳求过他，在罗克斯海斯面前说话务须十二分谨慎。他往椅背上一靠，椅子微微翘了起来。

---

===Original===

“We are all idle since our prorogation, Roxhythe﻿—and somewhat disgruntled!” He grimaced ruefully. “I should not say that to you, I suppose.”

Roxhythe stared into his glass.

“Yes, the Bill seems to have failed.”

“The poor Bill! But we must not weary you with it. You understand it is something of an obsession! However, I know you are not interested. Shaftesbury, a little Burgundy?”

“Why should I not be interested?” asked Roxhythe. “Of course﻿—it really does not affect me.﻿ ﻿…” He left a pause.

Holles shot a look at the Earl.

===Chinese===

“自打议会休会，咱们全都闲得发慌，罗克斯海斯——还有几分怏怏不乐哩！”他做了个苦相。“这话怕是不该对你说的。”

罗克斯海斯盯着自己的酒杯出神。

“是啊，那法案眼看是黄了。”

“可怜的法案！只是不该拿它来烦你。你要体谅，这桩事于我们简直是着了魔的！不过我知道你没兴趣。沙夫茨伯里，来点儿勃艮第葡萄酒？”

“我怎么会没兴趣？”罗克斯海斯道。“自然——这桩事其实与我无干……”他顿了一顿。

霍利斯朝伯爵飞快地递了个眼色。

---

===Original===

“Why I rather thought ye were above our discussions! But﻿—well, you are not always at one with his Grace of York, are you?”

He achieved a roguish smile.

Roxhythe touched his lips with his napkin.

“Not always,” he said.

Holles thought it as well to change the subject. He was an artist, he flattered himself. Presently he would let the conversation glide back to politics. He was annoyed when Shaftesbury, always impatient, came abruptly back to the all-important topic.

“Of course, if we have James we are assured of Papist successors.”

===Chinese===

“嗳，我原当这些商议用不着你屈尊俯就哩！不过——呃，你同约克公爵殿下也并非处处投契，是不是？”

他挤出一个促狭的笑脸。

罗克斯海斯拿餐巾按了按嘴唇。

“并非处处，”他说。

霍利斯觉得还是换换话题为妙。他自诩是此中妙手，少顷自会让话头滑回政治上去。不想沙夫茨伯里素来性急，劈头又把话扯回那桩头等要紧的正题，惹得他好不懊恼。

“自然罗，詹姆斯一日登基，天主教徒继承人便一日跑不了。”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe looked up quickly.

“Oh, ’tis not the successors﻿—” he stopped. “Do you think so?”

Holles replenished his glass. Since Shaftesbury had so tactlessly reintroduced the subject it had best be continued.

“With both parents Catholic, what would you?” he asked. “We ought to have a Protestant heir.” Out of the corner of his eye he could see Shaftesbury’s apprehensive gaze, full of warning.

Roxhythe was gloomy.

“Yes, but Mary means the Prince of Orange.”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯霍地抬起眼来。

“哦，倒不在继承人——”他收住了口。“你是这么想的？”

霍利斯给自己续了酒。沙夫茨伯里既已这般笨拙地重提旧话，索性谈下去为好。

“爹娘两个都是天主教徒，你说还能怎样？”他问道。“咱们该有一位新教继承人才是。”他眼角的余光，正瞟见沙夫茨伯里那惶惶然的目光，满是警示。

罗克斯海斯面色阴沉。

“是啊，可玛丽一继位，便是奥兰治亲王当政。”

---

===Original===

“True.” Holles returned Shaftesbury’s look steadily. “You do not like the thought?”

Roxhythe sipped his wine, of a sudden languid.

“In truth it concerns me not.”

There was no more political talk that evening.

When Roxhythe had gone, Holles returned to Shaftesbury, triumphant.

“What did I say?”

===Chinese===

“不错。”霍利斯稳稳地迎住沙夫茨伯里的目光。“你不喜欢这个念头？”

罗克斯海斯呷了口酒，倏地有气无力起来。

“说实话，这与我何干。”

那天晚上，政治的话题再没有提起。

罗克斯海斯去后，霍利斯得意洋洋地回到沙夫茨伯里跟前。

“我怎么说来着？”

---

===Original===

“Yes,” agreed the Earl. “But he is not desirous of joining us. I think he still ponders.”

“Evidently. And you see that he does not relish the idea of the Orange. We must secure him, my lord.”

“If we can﻿—if ’tis safe. He does not give much away.”

“Except that he wants the exclusion.”

“I wonder.﻿ ﻿…” Shaftesbury frowned uncertainly. “It may have been that he wished us to infer that.”

Holles was derisive.

===Chinese===

“不错，”伯爵附和道。“可他并不热心入伙。依我看他还在盘算。”

“明摆着。你也瞧见了，奥兰治那一头他不待见。咱们务必把他拉拢过来，大人。”

“若能拉得动——若保得稳的话。他这人轻易不露口风。”

“除了一条：他要排斥案通过。”

“这可难说……”沙夫茨伯里狐疑地皱起眉头。“说不定那正是他有意要让咱们这么琢磨的。”

霍利斯嗤之以鼻。

---

===Original===

“My dear Ashley! One could see that he was perturbed by his manner. Did you not think so?”

“Yes﻿—and no.”

“It was palpable! He must be cajoled to our side.”

“I do not like it!” Shaftesbury spoke curtly. “I do not trust Roxhythe. He might ruin us.”

“But will he? Do you not see that he must at all costs exclude James? He knows that the Duke hates him.”

“I do not expect him to work against the King.”

===Chinese===

“我亲爱的阿什利！单看他那副神气，便知他心里乱得很。你不觉得么？”

“也对——也不对。”

“明明摆在脸上！非把他哄到咱们这边来不可。”

“我不喜欢这买卖！”沙夫茨伯里说得干脆。“我信不过罗克斯海斯。他会坏咱们的事。”

“他会么？你还看不出来么，他无论如何也要把詹姆斯排斥掉？公爵恨他，他心里有数。”

“我可不指望他去跟国王作对。”

---

===Original===

“Rest assured that he would never do so openly. So much the better.”

“If we invite him to be one of us we take too great a risk.”

“I do not agree. If we do not snare him he may go over to Temple’s party. He has too much influence in the Upper House to be counted lightly. You do not want the Orange.”

“No, damme! But could he influence the House to that extent?”

“I think it more than likely. And if we set Monmouth up as the heir Roxhythe could very easily influence the King to ruin him.”

===Chinese===

“你放心，他断不会明着来。这反倒更好。”

“咱们若邀他入伙，冒的险可就太大了。”

“我不以为然。咱们若不把他收进网里，他兴许倒去投坦普尔那一派。他在上议院的影响大得很，万万轻视不得。奥兰治那一门，你总不愿意罢。”

“自然不愿意，见鬼！可他有本事把上议院说动到那份儿上？”

“我看八成有。再说，咱们若拥蒙茅斯为储，罗克斯海斯要撺掇国王毁了他，易如反掌。”

---

===Original===

“If he became one of Temple’s party that is what he would do, of course. Well.﻿ ﻿… But I do not like it!”

“Leave it to me!” said Holles.

===Chinese===

“他若投了坦普尔那一派，自然就会这么办。也罢……可我总不喜欢！”

“这事交给我！”霍利斯说。

---

===Original===

My Lord Roxhythe repaired to Whitehall. The King went apart with him.

“We progress,” said my lord tranquilly. “I am advocate for Mary, I am advocate for Monmouth.”

“ ’Sblood, David, does Shaftesbury really think to set Monmouth on the throne when I am gone?”

“So I gather. Temple wishes to bring Prince William to England to accustom the mind of England to the idea of his succession. But Shaftesbury will have none of it.”

“And you?”

“Very secretly I am with Temple﻿—say Halifax. Not wholly. They are still in doubt about me. Shortly I shall be one of Shaftesbury’s band. Then we shall see.”

===Chinese===

罗克斯海斯爵爷径往白厅去了。国王携他避到一旁。

“事情有进展，”爵爷安然道。“臣如今既鼓吹玛丽，也鼓吹蒙茅斯。”

“上帝的血，大卫，等我百年之后，沙夫茨伯里当真以为能把蒙茅斯扶上宝座？”

“臣听那意思正是如此。坦普尔想把威廉亲王接来英格兰，好叫英格兰的人心先习惯他继位这个念头。可沙夫茨伯里说什么也不肯。”

“那你呢？”

“臣暗地里是坦普尔一路的——姑且说，哈利法克斯一路。也不尽然。他们对臣尚有疑心。要不了多久，臣便要归入沙夫茨伯里那一伙了。到那时，且看分晓。”

---

===Original===

“It must be damned entertaining!” exclaimed the King.

“It is damned hard work!” retorted Roxhythe.

===Chinese===

“这差事准该死的有意思！”国王嚷道。

“也是该死的苦差事！”罗克斯海斯回敬道。

## II The Schemers / 阴谋家们

===Original===

Cautiously did my Lord Holles set about the business of snaring Roxhythe. It took some little time to win this trump card to his side, but he did it at length, marvelling at his own sagacity and cunning. At last Roxhythe allowed himself to be persuaded, and then he entered into the cause, as he put it, heart and soul. Shaftesbury still had misgivings; in Roxhythe’s presence his conversation was always guarded, yet he could not but see the truth in what Holles said: Roxhythe must at all costs work for the Duke of York’s exclusion. Reluctantly he invited Roxhythe to a discussion at his house.

The only other schemers there that day were Holles and one Lord Roberts. Roberts was entirely of Holles’ mind concerning Roxhythe. He clasped my lord warmly by the hand.

“I am glad to know that you are one of us, my lord!”

“I am honoured to be one of you,” smiled Roxhythe. “This is a serious matter.”

“It is indeed, my lord! It is indeed!”

===Chinese===

霍利斯勋爵小心翼翼地着手罗致罗克斯海斯入彀的勾当。要把这张王牌赢到自己这边，颇费了些时日，但他终究办成了，对自己这份精明乖觉暗暗称奇。罗克斯海斯终于肯听劝就范，从此便如他自己所说，全心全意投身这项大业。沙夫茨伯里仍存疑虑；罗克斯海斯在场时，他言谈总是存着几分戒备，然而霍利斯那番话他不能不觉得在理：罗克斯海斯无论如何得为排斥约克公爵出力。末了他还是勉为其难，请罗克斯海斯到府上共商一事。

当日在场的其他密谋者，便只有霍利斯和一位罗伯茨勋爵。在罗克斯海斯一事上，罗伯茨与霍利斯所见全同。他热忱地握住爵爷的手。

“很高兴得知您已是我们自己人，大人！”

“能与诸位同列，不胜荣幸，”罗克斯海斯笑道。“这可是桩正经事。”

“正是正经事，大人！正是！”

---

===Original===

Shaftesbury drew forward a chair.

“I need hardly say, Lord Roxhythe, that we trust to your discretion.”

“Certainly,” bowed my lord.

He listened to the discussion with interest. It appeared that the worthy gentlemen did not know how to win my Lords Halifax and Essex to their side. It also appeared that not many of the Council desired Monmouth for King.

In the middle of the argument my lord upraised his smooth voice.

“It seems, gentlemen, that the opposing side think his Grace would be an unpopular King.”

===Chinese===

沙夫茨伯里拉过一把椅子。

“无须我多说，罗克斯海斯勋爵，此事全赖您谨慎守密。”

“那是自然，”爵爷欠身道。

他饶有兴致地听着这场议论。看来这几位好先生还不知道怎样才能把哈利法克斯勋爵和埃塞克斯勋爵拉到自己一边。看来枢密院里巴望蒙茅斯登基的也为数不多。

议论正酣，爵爷把那副柔滑的嗓音提了起来。

“依我看，诸位先生，对面那一派似乎认定殿下会是个不得人心的国王。”

---

===Original===

“That is true!” cried Roberts. “They do not think that he would ever be received. I believe it is for that reason alone that they will not join us. Many of them do not really want William.”

“Then they should be shown how popular is the Duke,” said my lord.

“You mean that we should thrust him to the fore?”

“Present him to the people.﻿ ﻿… H’m!” Shaftesbury was dubious.

“He has been in the background of late,” remarked Holles. “It might be well to parade him.”

“Where is his Grace?” blandly asked my lord.

===Chinese===

“正是如此！”罗伯茨嚷道。“他们认定他永远得不到拥戴。我想单为这一条，他们便不肯入伙。他们当中许多人其实也并不想要威廉。”

“那就该让他们瞧瞧，公爵是何等得人心，”爵爷道。

“您是说，我们该把他推到台前去？”

“把他呈给民众瞧瞧嘛。……嗯！”沙夫茨伯里将信将疑。

“近来他一直屈居幕后，”霍利斯插话道。“让他出来亮亮相，或许不坏。”

“殿下此刻何在？”爵爷淡然问道。

---

===Original===

“He could not be present today,” answered Roberts, before Shaftesbury could intercept him.

“A pity,” said Roxhythe. He shrugged, and brought out his comfit-box.

“Why?” Shaftesbury it was who shot the question.

“He might have had some suggestion to put forward,” replied my lord.

“Oh, no!” Roberts shook his head. “He will be advised by us.”

“Why, that is better still,” said my lord, very urbane.

===Chinese===

“他今天没能到场，”罗伯茨抢在沙夫茨伯里拦阻之前答了话。

“可惜。”罗克斯海斯说着耸了耸肩，取出他的蜜饯盒。

“可惜什么？”发问的正是沙夫茨伯里。

“他原本兴许也能提些主意，”爵爷答道。

“哦，那倒不必！”罗伯茨摇头。“自有我们为他拿主意。”

“哎呀，那更妙了，”爵爷说，一派雍容。

---

===Original===

“Lord Roxhythe’s suggestion has merit,” observed Holles slowly. “It might be well to bring the Duke before the people’s eyes once more. You remember how popular he was during the war?”

“The people admired his courage﻿—why not send him to Scotland?” Lord Roberts started forward. “If the King might be induced to put him at the head of the troops!”

“To quell the rising? I do not know that His Majesty would do that.” Roxhythe spoke disparagingly. “He desires to keep the Duke at his side.”

“Could you not prevail with the King?” asked Holles.

Roxhythe seemed to consider.

“It is difficult. I do not want to become a suspect.”

===Chinese===

“罗克斯海斯勋爵这话有道理，”霍利斯缓缓道。“让公爵重新回到民众眼前，或许不坏。您可还记得战时他是何等得人心？”

“民众钦佩他的胆勇——何不派他去苏格兰？”罗伯茨勋爵探身向前。“若能劝得国王让他统兵，岂不甚好！”

“去平定叛乱？陛下未必肯这样办。”罗克斯海斯不以为然地说。“他要把公爵留在身边。”

“您就劝不动国王么？”霍利斯问。

罗克斯海斯似在思忖。

“难哪。我可不想惹上嫌疑。”

---

===Original===

“Surely you could do it in such a way that the King should suspect naught?”

“I might. I do not know.”

“It should not be so difficult. The King trusts in you.”

“Yes. Well, I will think on it. If I may safely do so I will use my influence. But the suggestion should come from Shaftesbury.”

“I agree with that,” said Roberts decidedly. “You could well suggest it to His Majesty, Ashley.”

“I am not sure that I approve of the scheme. Better that we should wait for a time.”

===Chinese===

“您总有法子办得叫国王瞧不出半点破绽吧？”

“或许有。我说不好。”

“这不该有多难。国王信重您。”

“也罢。容我想想。若保得万全，我便进言试试。不过这话头须得由沙夫茨伯里来提。”

“这我赞成，”罗伯茨斩钉截铁地道。“阿什利，这话由您向陛下提去，最合适不过。”

“我可不敢说赞成这条计策。不如再等些时日。”

---

===Original===

“No, no! If we wait we lose ground,” replied Holles. “If Monmouth quells the rising in Scotland the people will laud him once more. Then he can be paraded as much as you please. My Lords Halifax and Essex will see that he would be very easily the people’s choice.”

Still Shaftesbury hesitated.

“It is a bold step.”

“A sure step.”

“I think Holles is right,” said Roxhythe gently. “Halifax and Essex are uncertain. If they were clearly shown which way the people look they would be more likely to join us.”

“That is so, of course. On the other hand they may take fright at so bold a move.”

===Chinese===

“不，不行！再等下去，我们只会落于下风，”霍利斯答道。“蒙茅斯若平了苏格兰的叛乱，民众便会重新为他喝彩。到那时，随您怎么让他亮相都成。哈利法克斯勋爵和埃塞克斯勋爵自会看出，民心所属，非他莫属。”

沙夫茨伯里仍旧踌躇。

“这是步险棋。”

“是步稳棋。”

“我看霍利斯说得不错，”罗克斯海斯柔声道。“哈利法克斯和埃塞克斯本就摇摆。若让他们清清楚楚看见民心所向，他们入伙的可能便大得多。”

“这自然不假。可话说回来，如此大胆的一着，也兴许会把他们吓退。”

---

===Original===

“If you think that I should keep Monmouth in the background,” said Roxhythe.

“No. The step is worth taking,” said Roberts. “Do you, Roxhythe, think that Halifax and Essex will take fright?”

“It is hard to say,” fenced his lordship. “I had not thought so, I confess, but I may have been wrong.”

“There!” Roberts turned to Shaftesbury. “You hear?”

“And I still hesitate.”

Roxhythe smoothed his ruffles.

===Chinese===

“您若是觉得我该让蒙茅斯暂居幕后，”罗克斯海斯道。

“不。这一着值得走，”罗伯茨道。“罗克斯海斯，你看哈利法克斯和埃塞克斯会不会被吓退？”

“这就难说了，”爵爷闪避道。“老实讲，我原不曾这么想，可我也许看走了眼。”

“瞧见没有！”罗伯茨转向沙夫茨伯里。“您可听见了？”

“我仍旧踌躇。”

罗克斯海斯抚了抚袖口的褶边。

---

===Original===

“I do advise you to be guided by Shaftesbury. I know very little of these matters.”

“You underrate yourself, my lord!” cried Roberts. “I advocate the scheme.”

“And I,” said Holles.

Shaftesbury sighed.

“Very well, gentlemen. Since you are determined.”

===Chinese===

“我劝诸位务必听凭沙夫茨伯里主张。这些门道我懂得很少。”

“大人太自谦了！”罗伯茨嚷道。“我赞成这条计策。”

“我也赞成，”霍利斯道。

沙夫茨伯里叹了口气。

“好吧，诸位先生。既然你们主意已定。”

---

===Original===

Roxhythe visited the King in his closet that evening. Charles laughed at him.

“Well, my plotter?”

“I am deep in intrigue,” said Roxhythe. He sat down. “I have attended a meeting of our dear friends Ashley, Holles and Roberts.”

“I would give much to see you in their company,” chuckled the King. “What have you gleaned?”

“Several things. One that will grieve you, Sir.”

“Monmouth?”

===Chinese===

当晚，罗克斯海斯便到国王私室里觐见。查理拿他打趣。

“怎么样，我的阴谋家？”

“臣已然深陷阴谋之中了，”罗克斯海斯说着坐了下来。“臣方才列席了咱们亲爱的朋友们——阿什利、霍利斯、罗伯茨三位——的一场聚会。”

“能亲眼瞧瞧你混在他们当中的样子，给多大代价我都愿意，”国王呵呵直笑。“你都探到了些什么？”

“好几桩。有一桩要叫您伤心，陛下。”

“蒙茅斯？”

---

===Original===

“Monmouth.”

“He is privy to it?” Charles’ voice was anxious.

“I am afraid so, Sir.”

For a moment the King did not speak. He fingered his curls, his face overcast.

“I had not thought it of him,” he said at last. “This is ill hearing, David.”

“Not so ill as it might be, Sir. Monmouth would appear to be little more than a puppet in Shaftesbury’s hands.”

===Chinese===

“蒙茅斯。”

“他也知情？”查理的声音透着急切。

“恐怕正是，陛下。”

国王半晌没有言语。他捻着鬈发，满面阴霾。

“我竟没想过他会这样，”他终于开口。“这不是好消息啊，大卫。”

“也还不算到最坏的地步，陛下。蒙茅斯瞧着不过是沙夫茨伯里手里的一个傀儡罢了。”

---

===Original===

Charles pulled down the corners of his mouth.

“I wish he were not so weak!”

“Well, Sire, you always knew that he was﻿—easily led.”

“You said so from the first. What more?”

===Chinese===

查理撇了撇嘴角。

“他要是没那么软弱就好了！”

“这个嘛，陛下，您向来知道他是——好摆布的。”

“你打一开始就这么说。还有什么？”

---

===Original===

“I played with these worthy gentlemen. It was most amusing. They debated as to how they were to further Monmouth’s cause. I suggested that he should be brought to the people’s notice again. They liked my suggestion. All but Shaftesbury. He has sense but not sufficient faith in himself. The next suggestion came from Roberts. Why not send Monmouth to quell the Scottish rising? Eventually they decided that this was a brilliant step. I am to prevail upon Your Majesty to consent. Shaftesbury is to suggest it to you.”

===Chinese===

“臣同这几位好先生周旋了一番，有趣得很。他们商量着怎样推进蒙茅斯的大业。臣提议该让他重新回到民众眼前，他们很赞成臣这个提议，只有沙夫茨伯里例外。此人有见识，只是对自己信心不足。第二条主意出自罗伯茨：何不派蒙茅斯去平定苏格兰的叛乱？末了他们认定这是一步妙棋，要臣来劝请陛下俯允，再由沙夫茨伯里向陛下提起。”

---

===Original===

“Shaftesbury is very daring!”

“Very. Now, Sir, the point is this: by exhibiting Monmouth and by circulating the cry that he is the rightful heir, Shaftesbury will undoubtedly excite the people. I have insinuated that Halifax and Essex will also be won over.”

“They will not.”

“Most certainly they will not. They are hot for the Orange. And they would never stand for Monmouth on account of his birth. Your Majesty will pardon me if I speak too plainly.”

Charles smiled.

“Ay, I pardon you. Go on.”

===Chinese===

“沙夫茨伯里好大的胆子！”

“正是。眼下要紧的是这一层，陛下：只消把蒙茅斯抬出来示众，再四下里放风说他是正统嗣君，沙夫茨伯里必定能把民众鼓动起来。臣又暗暗递了话，说哈利法克斯和埃塞克斯也会给争取过来。”

“他们不会的。”

“断然不会。他们一门心思想着奥兰治那头。况且看在蒙茅斯那个出身份上，他们也断断容不得他。臣若有话说得太直的地方，还望陛下恕罪。”

查理微微一笑。

“唔，恕你无罪。说下去。”

---

===Original===

“When they see Monmouth blazoning in Scotland, and, later, blazoning through England, they will be the more alienated from Shaftesbury. And I rather think that the more timorous members of the Council, still wavering, will be shocked at Shaftesbury’s sudden move, and will either join the Orange party, or withdraw from the combat. Especially if Your Majesty shows signs of annoyance.”

“Very wise, Roxhythe. But are you sure of Halifax and Essex?”

“Perfectly. And I am moderately sure of our dear Sunderland.”

===Chinese===

“待到他们眼见蒙茅斯在苏格兰大出风头，往后又在英格兰全境招摇过市，他们便会同沙夫茨伯里愈发离心。臣还想着，枢密院里那些仍在首鼠两端的胆小角色，见了沙夫茨伯里这般突然出手，多半要吓一跳，不是投向奥兰治一派，便是索性抽身退出。陛下若再露出几分恼怒之色，就更是如此。”

“妙极，罗克斯海斯。可哈利法克斯和埃塞克斯那头，你有几成把握？”

“十成。至于咱们亲爱的桑德兰，也总有几分。”

---

===Original===

“Sunderland! Is he an Orangist?”

“Tentatively. If the Orange cause seems likely to prosper, he will become an ardent member. If not﻿—he will be properly indignant at the Exclusion Bill.”

“He does not cast his eyes in Monmouth’s direction?”

“He is too astute. Monmouth could never be King.”

“H’m! Well, I always thought him a man of brain.”

“He is very wily. I advise you, Sir, to consent to Monmouth’s generalship of the troops. Let him quell the rising; he has shown himself to be an able soldier. When the talk circulates that he is to be King after you, I shall be shaken with doubt. It may be that I shall affect others of Shaftesbury’s persuasion. It may even be that these eminently temperate gentlemen will draw back a little. Thus you have Shaftesbury standing alone. Then you may strike, and be sure of Essex and Halifax and Temple their approval.”

===Chinese===

“桑德兰！他是奥兰治派么？”

“试探着来。奥兰治这边眼见势旺，他便做一员热忱的干将；眼见不然——他自会对排斥法案做出十足义愤的样子。”

“他没打过蒙茅斯的主意？”

“他精明得很。蒙茅斯是永远做不成国王的。”

“嗯！我一向当他是个有脑子的。”

“此人工于权变。臣劝陛下准了蒙茅斯统兵一事。且让他去平叛，他到底显过一手段军旅才干。待到外面风传他要在您之后践祚，臣便要大起疑惧；这一疑惧，兴许还会波及沙夫茨伯里一派的旁人；说不定连这几位极有分寸的先生也要退上一步。如此一来，沙夫茨伯里便孤立无援。到那时陛下只管出手，埃塞克斯、哈利法克斯连同坦普尔那边的首肯，都是有把握的。”

---

===Original===

Charles stopped fingering his curls. His eyes brightened.

“*Cordieu*, David, I believe you are right! Essex and the rest of them are afraid of Shaftesbury since they supported the prorogation in May. If Shaftesbury wins they fall. Why, I shall have Shaftesbury in the hollow of my hand!”

“It will mean a struggle,” warned Roxhythe. “He is a dangerous man.”

“Whatever I do means a struggle. When I dismiss him Shaftesbury will move heaven and earth to defeat me, but it is the first step. And then﻿—an end to our brave Earl!”

===Chinese===

查理停住了捻发的手，眼睛亮了起来。

“*Cordieu*——上帝在上，大卫，我想你是对的！埃塞克斯那班人，打五月里附和了那场休会起，就一直怕着沙夫茨伯里。沙夫茨伯里要是赢了，他们便要跟着栽。嘿，到那时我还不把沙夫茨伯里攥在手心里！”

“那少不得有一场恶斗，”罗克斯海斯提醒道。“此人是个危险人物。”

“我做什么都少不得一场恶斗。真到罢黜他的那天，沙夫茨伯里定要翻天搅地跟我作对，可这总是头一步。再往后——咱们这位勇敢的伯爵，就该收场了！”

---

===Original===

“So I think, Sir.”

Charles relaxed again. Presently he frowned.

“Heigh-ho! I am disappointed in Monmouth. I did not think he would work behind me.”

“You would not like him to step into your shoes, Sir?” Roxhythe glanced at him curiously.

Charles was genuinely surprised.

===Chinese===

“臣也这么想，陛下。”

查理又松弛下来。过了一会儿，他皱起眉头。

“唉！蒙茅斯真叫我失望。我没料到他会在背地里算计我。”

“陛下是不愿意他接您的位子了？”罗克斯海斯好奇地瞟了他一眼。

查理是真吃了一惊。

---

===Original===

“I know that I have accorded him many rights and favours, but surely you cannot think that I would set him above James? Why, he is illegitimate!”

Roxhythe nodded.

“I wondered.”

“My moral sense is not so perverted, David!”

“No. I am glad of it.”

Charles opened his eyes lazily.

===Chinese===

“我知道我给过他许多体面恩宠，可你总不至于当我肯把他抬到詹姆斯之上吧？啊呀，他可是个私生子！”

罗克斯海斯点点头。

“臣不过有些疑心。”

“我的天良还没败坏到那步田地，大卫！”

“没有。臣深以为慰。”

查理懒懒地睁大了眼睛。

---

===Original===

“Do you care, then? I thought it was all one to you?”

“It is really. But I would sooner have James than the son of Lucy Walters.”

“Of course. God’s Body, but I should be a pretty Stuart if I connived at that!”

Roxhythe took up his hat.

“But you would not connive at it.﻿ ﻿… Well, Sir, I must be gone. I am due at Lord Essex his house in an hour.”

===Chinese===

“这么说，你倒真在意？我还当你觉得怎么都一样呢？”

“实在也当真无所谓。不过两下里挑，臣宁可要詹姆斯，不要露西·沃尔特斯（Lucy Walters）的儿子。”

“那是自然。上帝的圣体，我要是容纵了这个，可就真成了一位体面的斯图亚特了！”

罗克斯海斯拿起帽子。

“您自然不会容。……好了，陛下，臣该告退了。一个钟头后还得到埃塞克斯勋爵府上赴约呢。”

---

===Original===

“Poor David! Have you ever led so strenuous a life before?”

“Seldom,” answered Roxhythe. He smiled a little.

“I believe you like the game!” cried Charles, much amused.

“It is not without interest,” admitted his lordship. Then he sighed. “They are all so easy to trick,” he deplored. He went out languidly.

===Chinese===

“可怜的大卫！你从前可曾过过这样劳碌的日子？”

“少之又少，”罗克斯海斯答道。他微微一笑。

“我看你是喜欢上这局棋了！”查理乐不可支地嚷道。

“倒也不是全无兴味，”爵爷承认道。跟着叹了口气。“这些人统统不费吹灰之力就能骗倒，”他慨叹着，懒懒地走了出去。

## III Agitations / 风潮激荡

===Original===

So the Duke of Monmouth went to Scotland.

A mysterious tale arose. It was rumoured that the King had married Lucy Walters. There was much talk of a marriage certificate sealed in a certain box. Roxhythe attributed the tale to Shaftesbury, and affected dismay. He told the Earl that he had gone too far. He implored him to do nothing rash. Shaftesbury almost believed in his honesty.

As soon as he had put down the rising, the Duke of Monmouth returned triumphant to London.

===Chinese===

于是，蒙茅斯公爵去了苏格兰。

一桩神秘的传闻不胫而起。谣传国王早已娶了露西·沃尔特斯（Lucy Walters）。人言啧啧，都说某只匣子里封着一份结婚证书。罗克斯海斯把这说法安在沙夫茨伯里头上，做出一副惶惑之态。他告诉伯爵，这一步走得未免过火。他央他切莫轻举妄动。沙夫茨伯里几乎信了他的一片赤诚。

蒙茅斯公爵一经平定叛乱，便凯旋回到了伦敦。

===Original===

Then the King fell ill. Monmouth showed himself everywhere on the strength of it, and my Lords Sunderland, Halifax and Essex implored Charles to recall the Duke of York. They were very much afraid that if Charles grew worse and died, Monmouth would succeed at once.

Back came the Duke of York, sore at what he termed his banishment. From Sunderland he learned that Roxhythe was all for his exclusion. He thanked the pious Lord Sunderland for this information, and confessed that it in no way surprised him. He raved at Charles. Charles, convalescent, told him that he was a fool, and sent him to Scotland. Acting partly on Roxhythe’s advice, and partly from his own disgust at his son, he deprived Monmouth of his generalship, and ordered him to leave the country.

Doggedly Shaftesbury clung to his cause, deserted by all but a few. Supported by Lords Russell and Roxhythe, he pushed on the persecution of the Catholics in the country. Several entirely innocent men were put to death, including eight priests. The terror of the Popish plot was fanned into fresh flame. Roxhythe watched carefully, and, at length, solemnly warned Shaftesbury that he was going beyond all bounds. He counselled prudence, but by now my Lord was violent.

The King entered into the conflict and dismissed him from his post of Lord President of the Council. As Roxhythe had predicted, he had the Council’s full support.

===Chinese===

接着，国王病倒了。蒙茅斯仗着这势头四处抛头露面，桑德兰、哈利法克斯与埃塞克斯诸位勋爵恳请查理召回约克公爵。他们极为忧惧：万一查理病势转重、就此不起，蒙茅斯会立刻嗣位。

约克公爵回来了，对他口中的这场流放满怀怨愤。他从桑德兰处得知，罗克斯海斯一心主张把他排斥出去。他谢过虔诚的桑德兰勋爵通此消息，坦言此事毫不令他意外。他冲着查理大发雷霆。查理病体初愈，只说他是个傻子，把他打发去了苏格兰。他一半依着罗克斯海斯的进言，一半出于对亲生儿子的厌憎，革去蒙茅斯的统帅之职，命他离境。

沙夫茨伯里顽强地守着他的事业，众人尽去，只剩寥寥数人未弃他而走。有罗素勋爵与罗克斯海斯撑腰，他把国内对天主教徒的迫害一路推向深入。数名全然无辜的人被处死，其中有八名神父。天主教阴谋的恐怖之火又被扇得死灰复燃。罗克斯海斯冷眼细察，终于郑重警告沙夫茨伯里：他已越出了一切的界限。他劝他持重，可到这时，勋爵已然狂暴难遏。

国王亲自投入这场争斗，罢免了他的枢密院议长（Lord President of the Council）之职。正如罗克斯海斯所料，枢密院全力站在国王一边。

===Original===

Then he summoned Roxhythe.

“There is danger, David.”

“Great danger, Sir. Shaftesbury is determined to win.”

“And so am I. We shall see. I have appealed to France.” He frowned.

“France has answered?”

“Ay. Offering me degrading terms! This means I must call a Parliament. Heigh-ho!”

===Chinese===

随后，他召来罗克斯海斯。

“有大的风险，大卫。”

“大的风险，陛下。沙夫茨伯里志在必得。”

“我也志在必得。且看分晓。我已向法国求告。”他皱起眉头。

“法国回话了？”

“回了。开给我的是些屈辱条款！这就是说，我非召集一届议会不可。唉！”

===Original===

“You would be wise to wait before you allow it to meet, Sir.”

“I must gain time. I shall prorogue its assembly until November.”

“November of ’80. If you can.”

“I know that I can.”

In spite of all petitions he stood firm. Parliament was not allowed to meet.

===Chinese===

“容它开会之前先拖一拖，方为上策，陛下。”

“我必须争取时间。我要让它休会，休到十一月。”

“80 年的十一月。只要您做得到。”

“我知道我做得到。”

任凭百般请愿，他寸步不让。议会终究未获准开会。

===Original===

Shaftesbury grew still more daring. Again Roxhythe was closeted with the King.

Charles was worried.

“Shaftesbury exceeds all bounds, David. I am fearful for the result.”

“Give him rope, Sir,” advised my lord. “He’ll hang himself yet.”

“I don’t doubt it. But in the meantime he is working much harm. What is this tale of pamphlets?”

===Chinese===

沙夫茨伯里愈发胆大妄为。罗克斯海斯又同国王闭门密谈了一回。

查理忧心忡忡。

“沙夫茨伯里越出了所有的界限，大卫。我很担忧后果。”

“给他绳子，陛下，”爵爷进言。“他早晚拿它吊死自己。”

“这我不疑心。可与此同时，他正为祸不浅。小册子的传闻又是怎么回事？”

===Original===

“Our gentle Earl has a brain, Sir. He has formed a body. I am one of the body. We promote agitation. In time Essex will join us.”

“Roxhythe, this is serious!”

“Not as serious as it would seem, Sir. The public is tired of the Popish plot. Instead of executions, we now have acquittals.”

“But if Essex joins Shaftesbury it will mean great trouble!”

“It will bring matters to a head. There will indeed be trouble, but if you stand firm you will win. Monmouth is to return.”

===Chinese===

“咱们这位温文的伯爵有的是脑筋，陛下。他组了一个团体，臣便是其中一员。我们专事鼓动风潮。埃塞克斯迟早会加入我们。”

“罗克斯海斯，这可是件正经大事！”

“没有看上去那么严重，陛下。民众对天主教阴谋已经厌倦了。如今接替处决的，是一桩桩无罪开释。”

“可埃塞克斯若真同沙夫茨伯里合流，那就要出大乱子了！”

“这会让事情到了断的时候。乱子固然免不了，可您只要寸步不移，赢的便是您。蒙茅斯就要回来了。”

===Original===

Charles started up.

“What’s that? Monmouth defy me?”

“Shaftesbury sways him to his will. He induces him to come back to London.”

“It exceeds all bounds! It is direct insolence to me!”

“Therefore let be. It gives you yet another handle against our good Earl. You may trust me to further the dissension in the Council. Halifax is still for the Orange. Sunderland.﻿ ﻿…” He paused.

===Chinese===

查理霍地站起。

“你说什么？蒙茅斯敢违逆我？”

“沙夫茨伯里把他捏在手心里摆布，是他撺掇蒙茅斯回伦敦来的。”

“简直无法无天！这是当面对我的侮辱！”

“所以且由他去。这又给了您一件整治咱们好伯爵的把柄。枢密院里的嫌隙，臣自会替您推波助澜，您尽可放心。哈利法克斯仍向着奥兰治。桑德兰……”他顿住了。

===Original===

“What of Sunderland?”

“He has my admiration. He is very secret. As yet I can hardly say which party he supports: Orange or James. He waits to see which will win.”

“God’s Body! I am prettily served!”

“You are, Sir.”

“That Monmouth should treat me thus! My own son!”

===Chinese===

“桑德兰怎么了？”

“他令臣叹服。此人深藏不露。到此刻臣也说不准他投向哪一边：奥兰治，还是詹姆斯。他在等着看哪一边得胜。”

“上帝的圣体！我受的伺候可真周到！”

“可不，陛下。”

“蒙茅斯竟这般待我！我的亲儿子！”

===Original===

“Monmouth is a tool. You have very little to fear from that quarter. I have ascertained that every right-minded person in the country is opposed to him. They want Mary. Provided we can keep up the dissension, and use your influence in the Lords, the Exclusion Bill will be thrown out.”

“And in the meantime every town is garrisoned and I dare not move one way or the other for fear that popular feeling may turn against me! All this arming smacks of civil war.”

“Therefore I help to push it on. No one wants another war, and daily more men are coming round to your side.”

“You think that, David?”

“I am sure of it, Sir. But use your influence in the Upper House. The Bill will come again very soon and I think it will easily pass the Commons. The Peers are your one hope.”

===Chinese===

“蒙茅斯不过是件工具。那一头您大可不必多虑。臣已查明，国中但凡心术端正之人，无不反对他。他们要的是玛丽。只要咱们能让这嫌隙延续下去，再施展出您在上议院的影响力，排斥法案便会被否掉。”

“可与此同时，各镇皆已驻防，我进退都轻易动不得，唯恐民心一朝翻转过来与我作对！这满眼的整军备武，一股内战的气味。”

“所以臣才出力把它往前推。没有人想再打一仗，而每天都有更多的人转到您这边来。”

“你当真这么想，大卫？”

“臣对此确信无疑，陛下。但请在上议院施展您的影响。法案很快便会再度提交，臣想它在下议院轻易便能通过。上院的勋贵们才是您唯一的指望。”

===Original===

Charles rested his head in his hand.

“*Mordieu!* I am beset! I must look again to France.”

“Not yet. Let Shaftesbury run his course.”

“Oh, ay, ay! But what of the Orangists?”

“I told you some time ago, Sir, that Halifax blows cold when the rest blow hot. I believe he will oppose the Bill. Ostensibly it will be for the Duke of York, but William is at the back of his mind. He talks of another Bill of Securities that will vest all power in the Parliament. The Commons will never consent to that, I am sure. So if the Lords throw out the Exclusion, the Commons will throw out the Securities. Thus you gain time.”

===Chinese===

查理用手支着头。

“*Mordieu*——我的老天！我四面受敌！我不得不再向法国求告。”

“还不到时候。且让沙夫茨伯里跑完他这一程。”

“唉，正是，正是！那奥兰治派呢？”

“臣不久前便向陛下说过，哈利法克斯在旁人唱热调子时偏唱冷调子。臣信他会反对这法案。表面为的是约克公爵，他心里转的其实是威廉。他谈起另一部《担保法案》，要把一切权柄尽归议会。臣敢断言，下议院绝不应允这个。于是，上议院若否掉排斥案，下议院便会否掉《担保法案》。这样，您便争得了时间。”

===Original===

Charles sighed.

“You are wonderful, David. So you advise me to take no steps?”

The favourite dangled his gloves by their tassels. He was cool and very collected.

“Not yet. Exert your influence in the Upper House and leave the factions to quarrel. The Country itself is divided in half.”

Charles sat silent. Suddenly he rose.

===Chinese===

查理叹了口气。

“你真了不起，大卫。照你说，我一步都不必动？”

宠臣拎着手套，任流苏来回晃荡。他神色冷静，极是从容。

“还不到时候。在上议院施展您的影响，由着各派去争吵。乡村党自己也已裂作两半。”

查理默然坐着。忽然，他站起身。

===Original===

“It might be as well to recall James,” he said.

“If you like, Sir. It will bring him before the people again. It may bind his supporters more closely to him; on the other hand it will raise fresh opposition.”

“In fact,” said Charles, “it will raise more dissension, which you say we want.”

“Then send for him, Sir.”

===Chinese===

“把詹姆斯召回来，或许也是一策，”他说。

“悉听尊便，陛下。这会让他重新置身于民众眼前，或许能把拥戴他的人捆得更紧；可另一面，也会激起新的反对。”

“说穿了，”查理道，“它会激起更多的嫌隙，而照你说，咱们要的正是嫌隙。”

“那就召他回来吧，陛下。”

---

===Original===

A fortnight later Monmouth was travelling round England, having arrived in London secretly, by night, and Essex had joined with Shaftesbury. Russell and Cavendish handed in their resignations, and back came the Duke of York to London, furious at Monmouth’s return. The Exclusion Bill came and went; the tide was turning in the King’s favour.

Almost despairing, Shaftesbury brought in a Bill of Divorce, enabling the King to put away his Queen and remarry. Charles was very angry; the Duke of York was more so.

Then Roxhythe brought new and disturbing news to Court.

===Chinese===

半个月后，蒙茅斯已乘夜色秘密潜回伦敦，正巡行英格兰各地；埃塞克斯同沙夫茨伯里合了流。罗素与卡文迪什递上辞呈；约克公爵也回到伦敦，为蒙茅斯的归来怒不可遏。排斥法案来了又去；潮水正转向国王的一边。

几近绝望的沙夫茨伯里提出一部离婚法案（Bill of Divorce），使国王得以休弃王后另娶。查理大为震怒；约克公爵怒得更甚。

就在这时，罗克斯海斯带回一件新的、令人不安的消息上得宫来。

===Original===

“Sire, Shaftesbury is desperate, but he contemplates a last blow.”

“What is it?” asked Charles.

The Duke, who was present, eyed Roxhythe malevolently.

“He seeks to impeach Lord Stafford.”

Charles sank back in his chair.

“Impossible!”

===Chinese===
